================================================================================
AI Code Editor — Phase 7: Terminal Resize Fix, Split Terminals, Shell Selector
================================================================================

STATUS: complete and verified. Resize, side-by-side split terminals and the
per-OS shell dropdown all work; import/export, editor, sidebar and AI panel are
untouched and still render.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
1. TERMINAL RESIZE — ROOT CAUSE AND FIX
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
THE HANDLER WAS NEVER THE BUG. The panel was simply unreachable.

`.editor-area` is a flex item inside `.editor-column` (a column flexbox). With
no explicit `min-height`, it defaults to `min-height: auto`, so it refuses to
shrink below its own content. Once the welcome screen / Monaco is taller than
the viewport, the docked terminal panel is pushed completely below the fold.

MEASURED BEFORE THE FIX (headless Chrome, 484px viewport):
  .editor-column  436px
  .editor-area    958px   <- would not shrink
  .terminal-panel bottom at 1266px, i.e. ~780px BELOW the viewport
So the handle existed and its listeners fired, but a user could neither see nor
click it — which looks exactly like "the resize drag is broken".

THE FIX (client/src/components/Editor/Editor.css):
  .editor-area    min-height: 0        <-- the actual fix
  .editor-body    overflow: hidden     <-- keeps Monaco / welcome inside the
                                           editor box when the panel grows
  .empty-state    overflow: auto, and the centring moved from align/justify
                  to `.empty-state-card { margin: auto }` so the welcome screen
                  scrolls instead of bleeding over the terminal.
After the fix the panel bottom sits exactly on the viewport edge (verified: it
grows/shrinks live, editor shrinks to match).

HANDLE REBUILT CLEANLY anyway, per the brief (TerminalPanel.jsx startResize):
  - mousedown on the 4px handle attaches mousemove + mouseup to `document`
    (not to the handle), so a fast drag cannot outrun it.
  - new height = startHeight + (clientY_start - clientY_now), clamped to
    [TERMINAL_MIN_HEIGHT, window.innerHeight * TERMINAL_MAX_HEIGHT_RATIO].
  - constants changed: TERMINAL_MIN_HEIGHT 100 -> 120,
    TERMINAL_MAX_HEIGHT_RATIO 0.7 -> 0.75.
  - a class on <body> (`.is-resizing-rows` / `.is-resizing-cols`) forces
    `cursor: row-resize` (resp. col-resize) + `user-select: none` on every
    element for the whole drag, even outside the handle.
  - listener removal is symmetric on mouseup.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
2. SPLIT TERMINALS — THE GROUP DATA STRUCTURE
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
terminalSlice no longer keeps a flat `terminals: []`. It keeps tab GROUPS:

  groups: [
    {
      groupId,                              // the top-level tab id
      sizes: [w1, w2, ...],                 // relative width weight per pane
      panes: [ { terminalId, title, shell } ],
    },
  ]

  - A group with ONE pane is a normal full-width terminal. A group with TWO OR
    MORE panes is a side-by-side split inside a single tab.
  - Every pane keeps its own `terminalId` -> its own xterm instance -> its own
    server-side pty, so panes are fully independent and independently closable.
  - activeGroupId  = which tab is visible.
    activePaneId   = which pane of that group owns the keyboard focus (every
                     visible pane is still `fit()`-ed on activation).
  - Reducers: addTerminal (new group), splitActiveGroup (push a pane and a
    weight), removeTerminal (drop a pane; 1 pane left -> sizes=[1] collapses the
    group back to full width; 0 panes -> the group is removed), setActiveGroup,
    setActivePane, setSplitSizes, resetTerminals, plus the existing
    togglePanel / setPanelHeight / toggleMinimize.

RENDERING (client/src/components/Terminal/):
  - TerminalPanel renders the tab bar from `groups`. For each group it renders
    either one TerminalView (single pane) or SplitTerminalView (2+ panes).
  - ALL groups (and every pane) stay MOUNTED; inactive ones are hidden with
    `visibility`, so switching tabs or minimizing never kills a shell.
  - SplitTerminalView (new file): panes are flex children with
    `flex-grow: sizes[i]` and `flex-basis: 0`, separated by a 5px draggable
    vertical divider. A 22px header above each pane shows its title and its own
    small X (closes just that pty). Dragging divider i moves weight between
    panes i and i+1 ONLY, keeping their combined weight constant and clamping
    each to TERMINAL_PANE_MIN_SHARE (0.15) so a divider can't hide a pane.
  - The tab's own X closes the whole group (every pane's pty is closed).
  - The Columns2 button next to "+" splits the active group. Works for 2, 3,
    N panes; closing panes one at a time collapses back to a single terminal.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
3. SHELL SELECTION — DYNAMIC, BASED ON THE SERVER'S OS
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Nothing is hardcoded any more; the list is computed at runtime from
`process.platform` (cached after the first call).

server/src/utils/terminalManager.js
  - getAvailableShells():
      win32 -> powershell (powershell.exe), cmd ($ComSpec || cmd.exe), and bash
               ONLY IF a real Git Bash exists (checked via
               %ProgramFiles%\Git\bin\bash.exe and `where bash.exe`)
      else  -> bash only ($SHELL || /bin/bash)
  - getShellOptions() -> credential-free { platform, default, shells:[{id,label}] }
    for the API/client.
  - resolveShell(requestedId) -> falls back to the platform default when the id
    is unknown or unavailable (e.g. a stale 'bash' request on a Windows host
    without Git Bash), so a bad request can never crash the spawn.
  - createTerminalSession(..., { shell }) spawns the resolved command instead of
    a hardcoded shell, and terminal:ready now echoes { shell: label, shellId }.

server/src/controllers/terminal.controller.js + routes/terminal.routes.js
  - GET /api/terminal/shells  ->  { platform, default, shells:[{ id, label }] }
  - mounted at /api/terminal in routes/index.js. Public (matches the socket,
    which is not auth-gated either).

server/src/socket/terminalSocket.js
  - terminal:create now reads `shell` from the payload and passes it through.

Client wiring
  - services/api.js: `terminalApi.getShells()`.
  - features/terminalSlice.js: `fetchAvailableShells` thunk (dispatched once
    from App.jsx on load) fills `availableShells` + `defaultShell`.
  - The "+" button is now a SPLIT-BUTTON: the icon opens a terminal with the
    default shell, the small chevron opens a dropdown listing exactly what the
    server returned (Windows dev: PowerShell / CMD / bash; Linux deploy: bash),
    with a check mark on the default. The chevron is disabled while the list is
    empty.
  - The active shell is shown as a label in each tab title and each pane header
    ("PowerShell 1", "CMD 2", "bash 3") plus a small per-shell colour chip.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
VERIFICATION PERFORMED THIS ENTRY
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  - oxlint: 0 warnings, 0 errors (45 files).
  - vite build: succeeds.
  - node --check: terminalManager.js, terminalSocket.js, terminal.controller.js,
    terminal.routes.js, routes/index.js all pass.
  - Headless Chrome (CDP) drove the real UI end to end — 26/26 checks:
      * panel docked INSIDE the viewport (editor shrinks to make room);
      * drag grows/shrinks the panel live; clamps at exactly 120px and 75% of
        the viewport; row-resize cursor held for the whole drag, restored after;
      * shell dropdown lists exactly the server's 3 shells;
      * choosing CMD opened "CMD 1" and the xterm buffer contained cmd.exe's own
        "Microsoft Windows [Version 10.0.26200.9457]" banner (a REAL pty);
      * split -> two 289px panes + divider; dragging it gives [407,170];
        both panes have live independent ptys; a 3-pane split works; the
        divider cannot squeeze a pane to 0 ([57,324,191]);
      * closing panes one at a time collapses back to one full-width terminal;
      * a tab's X removes the whole group; multiple groups stay switchable;
      * minimize (36px) / expand still work;
      * topbar, sidebar, activity bar, editor and AI panel all still render;
      * zero uncaught page exceptions.
  - Backend shell matrix over Socket.IO (Windows): powershell -> PowerShell,
    cmd -> CMD, bash -> bash (Git Bash), an invalid id -> PowerShell fallback.
  - POSIX branch checked by overriding process.platform: linux returns
    { platform:'linux', default:'bash', shells:[bash] } — bash only, as required.


================================================================================
AI Code Editor — Phase 8: node-pty Windows Crash, Shell Selection, Session List
================================================================================

STATUS: complete and verified (32/32 UI checks + a socket crash-stress run).
The server survived every create/split/close flow without crashing or
restarting (health uptime stayed monotonic throughout).

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
1. NODE-PTY CRASH ON WINDOWS (TypeError: ... reading 'forEach')
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
ROOT CAUSE (confirmed by reading the installed source and by a deterministic
A/B reproduction, not just the reported stack trace):

  node_modules/node-pty/lib/windowsPtyAgent.js, ConPTY kill path:

      this._getConsoleProcessList().then(function (consoleProcessList) {
          consoleProcessList.forEach(...);      // <-- TypeError when undefined
      });

  ...and the helper it forks, conpty_console_list_agent.js:

      var consoleProcessList = getConsoleProcessList(shellPid);
      process.send({ consoleProcessList: consoleProcessList });

  getConsoleProcessList() returns `undefined` once the shell process has
  already exited (the console is gone). The parent then calls .forEach on that
  undefined value INSIDE a `.then()`, which is an UNHANDLED PROMISE REJECTION —
  and Node treats those as fatal, so the whole server process aborts. That is
  exactly the reported crash, and it only exists on the ConPTY path.

FIXES
  a) server/package.json: node-pty range bumped ^1.0.0 -> ^1.1.0 and
     `npm install` re-run. NOTE: 1.1.0 IS the latest published stable release
     (npm dist-tags latest = 1.1.0; the only newer builds are 1.2.0-beta.*), so
     upstream has not fixed this yet and the workaround below is required.
  b) terminalManager.js createTerminalSession(): spawn options now include
         ...(process.platform === 'win32' ? { useConpty: false } : {})
     On Windows this forces node-pty onto the older, stable winpty backend,
     which uses a SYNCHRONOUS native getProcessList() and never runs the
     crash-prone ConPTY code above. Linux/macOS keep node-pty's defaults.
  c) Crash-proof kill paths. killTerminal() deletes the session from the map
     FIRST, then wraps ptyProcess.kill() in try/catch and logs
     "[terminal] kill(<id>) failed (session dropped anyway): ..." instead of
     letting the throw escape. killTerminalsForSocket() and killAllTerminals()
     now log failures too (they used to swallow them silently), and
     writeToTerminal() is guarded the same way. The session is always removed
     from the sessions map regardless of what kill() does.

PROOF — deterministic A/B against the real node-pty code (forcing the
undefined consoleProcessList that the exited-shell race produces):
  ConPTY  (useConpty: true)  -> CRASHED: TypeError: Cannot read properties of
                               undefined (reading 'forEach')
  winpty  (useConpty: false) -> SURVIVED (no crash, no unhandled rejection)
Also: a socket-level stress run did 12 kill-a-running-pty cases, 12
let-the-shell-exit-THEN-close cases (the reported trigger) and a batched
8-terminal teardown; the server's /api/health uptime only ever increased
(130s -> 147s) and the socket never dropped -> no crash, no restart.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
2. SHELL SELECTOR — WHY IT LOOKED LIKE "EVERYTHING IS BASH" + FIX
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
GET /api/terminal/shells was already correct (verified live on win32):
  { platform:'win32', default:'powershell',
    shells:[{id:'powershell',label:'PowerShell'},{id:'cmd',label:'CMD'},
            {id:'bash',label:'bash'}] }
and the full chain was traced working: dropdown -> createTerminal(shellId) ->
terminal:create { shell } -> terminalSocket.js -> createTerminalSession({shell})
-> resolveShell() -> pty.spawn(). Real pty output proved it (cmd.exe printed its
own "Microsoft Windows [Version 10.0.26200.9457]" banner).

THE ACTUAL BUG: a tab's label came from the shell the CLIENT *requested*, not
the one the SERVER spawned. fetchAvailableShells() runs once at page load; if
the API was not reachable at that moment (server still starting / restarted),
the list stayed empty, defaultShell stayed null, so every "+" requested
nothing/`bash` and every tab said "bash ..." — even though PowerShell was what
really spawned. The label was a guess, never a fact.

FIXES
  - terminalSlice: panes now store `seq` (the stable 1,2,3) and a new
    setTerminalShell({ terminalId, shellId, label }) reducer.
  - TerminalView: on terminal:ready it dispatches setTerminalShell with the
    server's reported shell, so the tab/pane title and chip are corrected to
    what was REALLY spawned ("PowerShell 1" instead of "bash 1"). The ready
    event is the source of truth, since the server falls back to its platform
    default whenever the requested shell is unavailable.
  - The client now sends the requested shell as null when it has no list, so
    the server picks its own default rather than being told "bash".
  - Recovers from a failed initial fetch: while the shell list is empty the
    panel retries once when it is (re)opened, and the chevron click retries
    again (showing "Loading shells..." meanwhile). New shellStatus field
    ('idle'|'loading'|'ready'|'failed') keeps that from looping.

PROOF: with the shells endpoint deliberately BLOCKED (so the client has no
list at all), clicking "+" still produced a tab named "PowerShell 1" with the
is-powershell chip and a session-list row "PowerShell 1", and the buffer
contained a real PowerShell banner. The "all bash" symptom is gone because the
label now follows the spawn instead of the request.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
3. RIGHT-SIDE TERMINAL SESSION LIST (VS Code style)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
NEW FILE: client/src/components/Terminal/TerminalSessionList.jsx

Panel layout is now: [tab bar on top] -> [terminal content | session list].

  - A slim 200px column (min 180 / max 220) down the RIGHT edge of the terminal
    panel, separated from the output area by `border-left: 1px solid #3c3c3c`,
    with an uppercase "Terminals" header. It renders only while the terminal
    panel is open (the whole workspace row is hidden when minimized).
  - It flattens EVERY pane of EVERY group/split into one list, so all open
    sessions are visible at a glance regardless of nesting. Each row shows:
      * a small icon chosen by shell (TerminalSquare for PowerShell,
        SquareTerminal for CMD, Terminal for bash) tinted with the same
        per-shell colour used by the tab chip;
      * the session label ("PowerShell 1", "CMD 2", "bash 3");
      * a small X that closes just that one pty (emits terminal:close and
        removes the pane) without activating the row underneath.
  - The focused terminal's row is highlighted with the accent background plus
    an accent left border (`.is-active`).
  - Clicking a row dispatches setActivePane(terminalId), which brings the
    parent tab group to the front and, when the session lives in a split,
    focuses that exact pane.
  - The top tab bar still switches groups/splits, and the split divider and
    panel resize handle are untouched by the new column (the divider drag, the
    handle drag and the session list were all exercised together).

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
VERIFICATION PERFORMED THIS ENTRY
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  - oxlint: 0 warnings, 0 errors (46 files). vite build: succeeds.
  - node --check on terminalManager.js and terminalSocket.js: passes.
  - Headless Chrome (CDP) drove the real UI — 32/32 checks:
      * shells endpoint content is platform correct;
      * dropdown lists exactly PowerShell / CMD / bash;
      * selecting each one really spawns that shell (per-pty banner proof) and
        the tab/pane/session-list labels match;
      * with the endpoint blocked, the label still resolves to the real shell;
      * the session list is 200px wide with the #3c3c3c left divider, shows all
        sessions, exactly one highlighted, and each row carries its shell class;
      * clicking a row focuses that terminal; clicking a split-pane row focuses
        that pane (asserted via the active pane title);
      * closing from the list X, from a split pane's header X, and from a tab X
        all behave (and the split collapses when it drops to one pane);
      * the split divider still drags with the list present;
      * no uncaught page exceptions;
      * /api/health uptime increased across the whole flow (no crash/restart).
  - Cleanup: temp scripts deleted, headless Chrome stopped and its profile
    removed. The user's own API + vite dev servers were left running.


================================================================================
AI Code Editor — Phase 9: Floating Terminal Panel + Shared Scratch Workspace
================================================================================

STATUS: complete and verified (16/16 backend scratch checks + 29/29 UI checks).

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
1. THE TERMINAL PANEL NOW FLOATS OVER THE EDITOR
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Before, the terminal was a normal flex child of `.editor-column`, so opening it
pushed the editor up (it resized, and Phase 7 even had to add `min-height: 0`
to `.editor-area` so it could shrink at all). Now it is an overlay.

HOW IT WORKS
  App.css        .editor-column { position: relative; ... }
                 -> becomes the positioning context for the terminal.

  TerminalPanel.css
    .terminal-panel {
      position: absolute;      /* out of flow: the editor is untouched */
      left: 0; right: 0; bottom: 0;
      z-index: 30;             /* above the editor, below topbar/toasts/modals */
      background: #1e1e1e;     /* SOLID, it sits over live editor content */
      border-top: 1px solid var(--border);
      box-shadow: 0 -6px 18px rgba(0, 0, 0, 0.45);   /* top-edge separation */
    }
  - `flex: 0 0 auto` was removed; the inline `height` (Redux terminal.panelHeight)
    still drives the panel, so drag-to-resize behaves exactly as before — it now
    just changes the FLOATING panel's height instead of a flex layout.
  - z-index ladder: sidebar resize handle 5 < terminal 30 < sticky topbar 40 <
    toast 50 < AuthModal 60. The terminal therefore never covers a modal or a
    toast.
  - Nothing else changed: the sidebar and AI panel keep their own
    `.panel-slot` widths and are untouched; only the terminal's relationship to
    the editor column changed. The resize handle, the split divider, the shell
    dropdown and the right-side session list all keep working (all re-verified).

  Verified in a real browser: editor height 852px before opening the terminal,
  852px after, and still 852px after dragging the panel from 260px to 400px.
  Panel computed style: position absolute, bottom flush with the editor column,
  background rgb(30,30,30), box-shadow present, z-index 30. Sidebar (250px) and
  AI panel (320px) widths unchanged.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
2. FILES/FOLDERS NOW WORK WITHOUT AN IMPORTED PROJECT (SHARED SCRATCH)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
The scratch workspace the terminal already used is now shared with the whole
Explorer, so before any import the app is fully usable — not just for terminals.

SERVER (server/src/middlewares/ensureWorkspace.js)
  The single workspace guard used by every /api/workspace route now does:
      if (isScratchProjectId(projectId))  ensureScratchWorkspace(projectId)
      else if (!workspaceExists(projectId)) -> 404 WORKSPACE_MISSING
  i.e. a scratch folder is created on demand (same folder the terminal uses as
  cwd), while a real imported project that vanished still 404s exactly as
  before. No second scratch system was introduced and no new routes were added —
  tree/file/create/rename/delete/export all just work against scratch ids.

CLIENT
  NEW client/src/utils/workspace.js — one place that defines the two kinds of
  workspace and, crucially, `scratchProjectIdFor(userId)` so the Explorer and
  the terminal can never drift apart:
      scratchProjectIdFor(userId) -> `scratch-${userId}` (or 'scratch-guest')
      isCloudWorkspace / isScratchWorkspace / hasActiveWorkspace / workspaceLabel

  features/fileSystemSlice.js
    + setScratchWorkspace({ projectId, name, tree }) — same shape as
      setCloudWorkspace but `source: 'scratch'`, and it deliberately does NOT
      clear open tabs. From here on a scratch workspace is handled by exactly
      the same reducers/thunks/routes as an imported project.
  features/workspaceThunks.js
    + openScratchWorkspace({ projectId }) thunk: GETs the tree (which also
      creates the folder server-side and returns anything created earlier, so
      the scratch workspace is REUSED across sessions) and dispatches
      setScratchWorkspace.
  components/Sidebar/Sidebar.jsx
    - `hasProject` is now `hasActiveWorkspace(workspace)`, so the New File /
      New Folder / Collapse All buttons are enabled with no project.
    - startCreating() → guard(...) (sign-in first, like Import/Export/Save) →
      ensureWorkspaceReady(): if there is no workspace it dispatches
      openScratchWorkspace(scratchProjectIdFor(userId)) and awaits it, THEN sets
      the inline create input. Because the scratch tree now exists, the input
      renders inside the root folder instead of having nowhere to go.
    - submitCreate/handleRename/handleDelete read the active project id from a
      ref that is kept in sync with the store, so they are correct even though
      the workspace id changes asynchronously on the first creation.
    - The empty-state notice was reworded: "No project imported — New File
      starts a scratch workspace, or click Import Folder…". As soon as the
      first file is created the notice disappears and the scratch tree shows.

  Scratch is now treated like a real project everywhere it matters:
    Editor/EditorArea.jsx      loads file content via hasActiveWorkspace()
    hooks/useSaveActiveFile.js saves via hasActiveWorkspace()
    Sidebar/SearchInFiles.jsx  searches file CONTENT in scratch too
    Editor/EmptyState.jsx      shows "Workspace: Scratch workspace"
    Topbar/Topbar.jsx          Save enabled for any workspace; the chip reads
                               "Scratch workspace"; Export ZIP and Sync-to-laptop
                               stay cloud-project-only, because a scratch
                               workspace has no imported laptop folder.

  App.jsx — terminals no longer reset when entering scratch. The reset is keyed
  to a REAL project only:
      const cloudProjectId = isCloudWorkspace(workspace) ? workspace.projectId : null
  so `none -> scratch` leaves any already-open terminal alive, while
  `scratch -> imported project` (or project -> project) still resets, because the
  pty's cwd genuinely changes. The file-content cache is still cleared on every
  workspace change. resetTerminals() was added to the sign-out effect to keep the
  old teardown behaviour there.

  components/Terminal/TerminalPanel.jsx now resolves its cwd with the SAME helper:
      effectiveProjectId = workspace.projectId || scratchProjectIdFor(userId)
  so the terminal and the Explorer always point at one folder.

  IMPORTING LATER stays independent: useOpenFolder creates a brand new project
  (POST /api/projects) with its own workspace id, saves it as the restore ref and
  becomes the active workspace; the scratch folder is never touched or deleted.

NOTES FOR THE NEXT DEVELOPER / AI TOOL
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  - A scratch workspace is a FIRST-CLASS workspace: same tree/editor/save/
    search paths as a cloud project, distinguished only by `workspace.source`.
    When adding a feature, decide explicitly whether it should apply to scratch
    (file operations: yes; Export ZIP / Sync-to-laptop / StorageNotice: no).
  - Use utils/workspace.js helpers rather than comparing `source === 'cloud'`
    by hand — that string check was the reason the Explorer was dead before an
    import in the first place.
  - The scratch folder is never registered in MongoDB and is never written to
    workspaceStorage, so a reload always starts from "no project" and the
    scratch files are reached again by clicking New File (which reuses them).
  - The terminal panel is an overlay now: do not give it `flex` sizing, and keep
    its z-index below the topbar (40)/toasts (50)/AuthModal (60).

================================================================================
AI Code Editor — Phase 10: Terminal Number Reuse + Explorer Context Menu
================================================================================

STATUS: complete and verified (13/13 duplicate-API checks + 41/41 UI checks).

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
1. TERMINAL NUMBERS NOW REUSE FREED SLOTS (per shell type)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Before, a single module-level counterRef in TerminalPanel.jsx incremented
forever: opening PowerShell 1, 2, 3, closing 1 and 2 and opening another one
gave "PowerShell 4". The counter never looked at what was actually open.

NEW RULE (both places compute the same thing)
  The number for a new terminal = the LOWEST positive integer that no
  currently-open terminal of that SAME shell type is using. Shells are counted
  independently: PowerShell, CMD and bash each keep their own sequence, so a CMD
  terminal never shifts PowerShell numbers and vice versa.

  components/Terminal/TerminalPanel.jsx
    - `counterRef` was DELETED.
    - + lowestFreeSeq(shell): walks every group's panes, collects `seq` values of
      panes whose `shell` matches, then returns the smallest integer >= 1 that is
      not in that set (`while (used.has(seq)) seq += 1`). The answer is computed
      FRESH on every create — nothing is cached and nothing only increases.
    - nextPane(requestedShell) calls lowestFreeSeq(shell) and builds the title
      `${shellLabel(shell)} ${seq}`. createTerminal() and splitActiveTerminal()
      both go through it.

  features/terminalSlice.js
    - + a module-level copy of lowestFreeSeq(groups, shell, excludeTerminalId).
      It exists because the number is allocated against the REQUESTED shell, but
      the server is the source of truth for what actually spawned (a fallback for
      an unavailable shell, or the platform default when the client had no shell
      list yet).
    - setTerminalShell(...) now: if the reported `shellId` differs from the
      pane's current shell, it switches `pane.shell` AND re-allocates `pane.seq`
      via lowestFreeSeq(..., terminalId) (excluding the pane itself), then
      retitles from `label + seq`. Without this, two panes could end up both
      labelled "PowerShell 1" after a server fallback.

  EXPLICIT TEST (the scenario from the request)
    open PowerShell 1 / 2 / 3 -> close 1 and 2 -> open another PowerShell
      => "PowerShell 1"  (the lowest free number, NOT 4).
    CMD numbering verified independently: CMD 1, CMD 2 -> close CMD 1 -> next
      CMD is "CMD 1", and the PowerShell numbering is unaffected by CMD panes.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
2. RIGHT-CLICK CONTEXT MENU IN THE EXPLORER
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Right-clicking any row in the tree (file, folder or the root) opens a small
dark-theme menu at the cursor with working file operations. The existing hover
pencil/trash icons are still there — the menu is an additional path to the same
actions, not a replacement. Left-click still expands/collapses folders.

NEW components/Sidebar/TreeContextMenu.jsx
  - `position: fixed` (so it appears at the cursor regardless of scroll), height
    above everything it must cover. z-index 45 (below toasts 50 / AuthModal 60).
  - Full viewport clamping via useLayoutEffect: if the menu would overflow the
    right or bottom edge it is nudged back on screen.
  - `role="menu"`, one row per item (`{ id, label, icon, onSelect, disabled?,
    danger? }`); `{ separator: true }` draws a divider; null entries are filtered
    out (used to conditionally hide Paste).
  - Closes on: item click, any outside `mousedown`, and Escape. Right-clicking a
    second row moves the menu to the new cursor position. Rows also fire the
    row's normal select so the item being acted on is highlighted.

NEW features/clipboardSlice.js  (registered as `clipboard` in app/store.js)
  { path, type: 'file'|'folder', mode: 'cut'|'copy' } + setClipboard() /
  clearClipboard(). Only a lightweight reference travels through Redux — never
  file content.

NEW utils/clipboard.js
  copyTextToClipboard(text): navigator.clipboard.writeText with a hidden-
  textarea + document.execCommand('copy') fallback. Returns a boolean.

components/Sidebar/FileTree.jsx
  - Every row (and the root) gets onContextMenu -> openMenu(node) (which also
    selects the row). buildMenuItems(node) builds the list per node type, and
    <TreeContextMenu> is rendered once at the bottom of the tree.
  - A cut row is dimmed with the `.is-cut` class.
  - Rename was refactored into a child <RenameInput> component that owns its
    draft text, keyed `menu-<id>` (started from the menu) or `inline-<id>`
    (started from the hover pencil). The API changed from a boolean `forceRename`
    to an id: FileTree passes `renamingId` down and each row compares
    `renamingId === node.id`. (FIXED A REAL BUG found while testing: the old
    boolean was resolved at the top level, so a menu Rename on a NESTED row could
    never start editing.)

components/Sidebar/Sidebar.jsx — the handlers
  - handleRequestCreate(parentId, type): signs in first, then opens the inline
    New File / New Folder input INSIDE that specific folder.
    [UPDATED 2026-09-28] A row's menu still targets that row, but the TARGET of
    the toolbar buttons and of the empty-area menu is now resolved by
    utils/explorerTarget.js: folder row -> inside it, file row -> its parent,
    NOTHING selected -> the TOP LEVEL (a sibling of the project folder inside the
    user's container). The old "nothing selected -> the project root" rule is
    gone — see the multi-level create-target entry at the end of this file.
  - handleCut(node)     -> setClipboard({ path: node.id, type, mode: 'cut' })
  - handleCopy(node)    -> setClipboard({ path: node.id, type, mode: 'copy' })
  - handlePaste(folder) -> CUT = a real server-side MOVE via the existing rename
    endpoint (newPath = joinPath(target, baseName(source))); COPY = a real
    server-side duplicate via the new endpoint. Pasting into the folder the item
    already lives in is a no-op toast. A cut is consumed (clipboard cleared) so
    Paste disappears; a copy stays on the clipboard (VS Code behaviour) so it can
    be pasted again.
  - handleCopyPath(node) -> copyTextToClipboard(node.id) and a
    "Copied path: <path>" toast.
  - The active project id is read from projectIdRef for all of these so they
    stay correct even while the workspace id changes asynchronously.

MENU CONTENTS (exact)
  FILE:        Cut | Copy | ─ | Rename | Delete | ─ | Copy Path
  FOLDER:      New File | New Folder | ─ | Cut | Copy | (Paste if clipboard
               non-empty) | ─ | Rename | Delete | ─ | Copy Path
  ROOT (locked workspace root — cannot be cut/renamed/deleted):
               New File | New Folder | ─ | (Paste) | ─ | Copy Path
  Icons (lucide-react): Scissors=Cut, Copy=Copy, Clipboard=Paste, Pencil=Rename,
  Trash2=Delete, FilePlus=New File, FolderPlus=New Folder, Link=Copy Path.
  Delete is styled red (`.is-danger`, #c62828); the panel is rounded with a
  subtle border and a hover highlight per row (styles in Sidebar.css).

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
3. NEW BACKEND ENDPOINT: POST /api/workspace/:projectId/duplicate
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  Body:    { sourcePath: string, targetFolderPath?: string }  ('' = root)
  201 ->   { sourcePath, newPath, duplicated: true }

  server/src/routes/workspace.routes.js
    router.post('/:projectId/duplicate', ensureWorkspace, controller.duplicateEntry)

  server/src/controllers/workspace.controller.js
    duplicateEntry: validates sourcePath, calls copyPathInWorkspace(...) and
    touchProject(projectId). Optional targetFolderPath -> workspace root.

  server/src/utils/workspaceManager.js
    + copyPathInWorkspace(projectId, sourceRelativePath, targetFolderPath = '')
      resolves both paths inside the workspace (traversal-guarded), refuses to
      copy a folder into itself or a descendant, keeps the ORIGINAL name, and
      copies via copyFileSync (file) or copyDirectoryRecursive (folder).
    + copyDirectoryRecursive(from, to): mkdir -p then walk readdirSync with
      withFileTypes, recursing into directories and copying every file — so a
      whole folder tree is duplicated.
    - renamePathInWorkspace now also rejects moving a folder into itself/a
      descendant (400 INVALID_PATH), matching the copy guard.

  Error codes:
    400 INVALID_PATH  — missing/empty sourcePath, path escapes the workspace,
                        folder copied into itself/its own subtree.
    400 NOT_A_FOLDER  — targetFolderPath points at a file.
    404 FILE_MISSING  — the source path does not exist.
    409 ALREADY_EXISTS — an item with that name is already in the target folder.

  Client plumbing: services/api.js workspaceApi.duplicateEntry(projectId,
  sourcePath, targetFolderPath) -> POST /duplicate; features/workspaceThunks.js
  duplicateWorkspaceEntry thunk calls it then refreshes the tree.

NOTES FOR THE NEXT DEVELOPER / AI TOOL
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  - Terminal numbers are PER SHELL and recomputed from the open panes every
    time. If you add another place that creates a pane, route it through
    nextPane()/lowestFreeSeq() — do not reintroduce a counter.
  - The clipboard stores only { path, type, mode }; any new "paste" target must
    go through handlePaste so the cut/copy distinction and the error toasts
    stay in one place.
  - The context menu is generic (TreeContextMenu): build a new item list and
    pass it in rather than writing another positioned menu.

    server-side and traversal-guarded, and both refuse a folder into its own

AI Code Editor — Phase 11: Terminal Numbering Removed + Paste Fix
================================================================================

STATUS: complete and verified (25/25 duplicate/rename API checks + 34/34 UI checks).

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
1. TERMINAL NUMBERING IS GONE
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Terminal tabs, the split-pane headers and the right-side TERMINALS list now show
ONLY the shell name — "PowerShell", "CMD", "bash" — with no number, timestamp
or letter. Two terminals of the same shell therefore show the same label, which
is intended and accepted.

  components/Terminal/TerminalPanel.jsx
    - `lowestFreeSeq()` deleted (it was added in Phase 10; the whole idea of a
      per-shell sequence is gone).
    - nextPane(requestedShell) now returns { shell, title: shellLabel(shell) } —
      the title is just the label.
    - createTerminal()/splitActiveTerminal() no longer send a `seq`.

  features/terminalSlice.js
    - the module-level `lowestFreeSeq()` deleted, and `seq` removed from the pane
      shape entirely: panes are { terminalId, title, shell }.
    - addTerminal / splitActiveGroup no longer read a `seq`.
    - setTerminalShell just records the shell the server really spawned and sets
      `pane.title = label` (previously it re-allocated a number here). No
      renumbering, no collision handling — none is needed without numbers.

  WHAT IS STILL UNIQUE — deliberately: every pane keeps its own
  `terminalId` (crypto.randomUUID()). That id is what React keys, the xterm
  instances, the split layout and every `terminal:*` socket message use. Nothing
  in the UI derives a number from it any more.

  Verified in a real browser: three terminals of one shell are all titled the
  plain shell name (no digit anywhere), the TERMINALS list matches, closing two
  and opening another still shows the plain name, and a CMD terminal added from
  the shell dropdown is labelled just "CMD".

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
2. CUT/COPY/PASTE — ROOT CAUSE AND FIX
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
The dispatch chain itself was correct (Copy/Cut -> clipboardSlice
{ path, type, mode } -> Paste only rendered when clipboard.path is set -> on
click it calls the rename endpoint for a cut and /duplicate for a copy with the
FOLDER's own path as targetFolderPath). Two behaviours made it fail in practice:

  ROOT CAUSE 1 — a name collision was a hard error.
    copyPathInWorkspace() threw 409 ALREADY_EXISTS whenever the target folder
    already contained an item of that name. A copy ALSO kept the clipboard on
    purpose (VS Code behaviour), so the very common sequence "copy a file, paste
    it, paste it again" — or pasting an item back into a folder that already had
    it — hit a red error toast and the second paste looked like it did nothing.

    FIX (server/src/utils/workspaceManager.js)
      + uniqueNameInFolder(folderAbsolute, name): if the name is taken, the copy
        is written as "<base> (copy)<ext>", then "<base> (copy 2)<ext>",
        "<base> (copy 3)<ext>", … So a duplicate never overwrites and never
        fails — copying a.txt into a folder that already has a.txt produces
        a (copy).txt and leaves the original untouched. copyPathInWorkspace no
        longer throws ALREADY_EXISTS at all.

  ROOT CAUSE 2 — a stale clipboard.
    After a successful copy-paste the clipboard was left populated, so Paste kept
    being offered and repeating it reproduced the collision above.

    FIX (client/src/components/Sidebar/Sidebar.jsx — handlePaste)
      The clipboard is now cleared after ANY successful paste (cut or copy), so a
      second Paste does nothing until something new is cut/copied. It is cleared
      only AFTER the request resolves (via .unwrap()), so a FAILED paste keeps the
      clipboard and can be retried.

  Also confirmed correct (no change needed):
    - the paste target is the CLICKED FOLDER's own path (`folderNode.id`, '' for
      the workspace root) — never the source item's path or its parent;
    - a CUT is a real server-side MOVE via PATCH /rename, so the original is gone
      afterwards and nothing is duplicated;
    - a COPY is a real server-side POST /duplicate; the original stays put;
    - after either one the tree refetches (refreshWorkspaceTree) and the new item
      is visible immediately.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

NOTES FOR THE NEXT DEVELOPER / AI TOOL

================================================================================
AI Code Editor — Phase 12: Monaco Path Aliases, Vite Watcher Fix, Delete Confirm,
               Explorer Undo/Redo + Keyboard Cut/Copy/Paste
================================================================================

STATUS: complete. client oxlint (0 warnings / 0 errors, 55 files) and `vite build`
        both pass. Manual browser verification was NOT run in this environment.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
1. MONACO NO LONGER FLAGS VALID IMPORTS / JSX AS ERRORS
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
WHY: Monaco's TypeScript language service runs in a web worker that cannot read
the project on disk and knew nothing about the project's path aliases, so a
Next.js-style "@/" import and plain JSX were underlined red.

NEW client/src/utils/monacoTsconfig.js
  - TS_CONFIG_FILENAMES = ['tsconfig.json', 'jsconfig.json'].
  - parseTsconfigJson(text): strips // and /* */ comments (string-aware) and
trailing commas, then JSON.parse — tsconfig files are JSONC and plain
JSON.parse rejects them.
  - extractCompilerOptions(config): pulls the serializable subset Monaco needs —
baseUrl, paths, jsx, target, module, moduleResolution. `paths` values are
normalised to arrays of strings.
  - buildMonacoCompilerOptions(monaco, options): maps the tsconfig STRING values
to the real Monaco/TypeScript enum numbers (ScriptTarget, ModuleKind,
ModuleResolutionKind, JsxEmit). Falls back to modern React/Next defaults (ESNext,
ESNext, Bundler, react-jsx, allowJs, noEmit, resolveJsonModule, skipLibCheck,
esModuleInterop) whenever a value is missing, so a project with NO tsconfig still
type-checks JSX. When `paths` exists without a `baseUrl`, baseUrl defaults to
'.' so "./src/*"-style targets resolve.
  - applyMonacoTsConfig(monaco, options): pushes the built compilerOptions AND
the diagnostics options into BOTH typescriptDefaults and javascriptDefaults.
  - DIAGNOSTIC_CODES_TO_IGNORE: because the worker still cannot see node_modules
or unopened files, these codes are always false positives and are ignored:
2307 (Cannot find module), 2792 (did you mean moduleResolution), 7016 (no
declaration file), 2688 (no type definition file), 2503 (Cannot find namespace
'React'), 2686 ('React' refers to a UMD global), 7026 (JSX element implicitly
any), 17004 (JSX needs the --jsx flag). Syntax errors and real local semantic
errors are still reported.

NEW client/src/features/tsconfigSlice.js
  state { fileName, compilerOptions } + setTsConfig / clearTsConfig.

MODIFIED client/src/features/workspaceThunks.js
  loadWorkspaceTsConfig({ projectId }) reads tsconfig.json then jsconfig.json via
the workspace API (so it covers import, reopen AND the scratch workspace),
parses it and stores the extracted options; a project with neither file clears
the config and uses the defaults.

MODIFIED client/src/App.jsx
  An effect on workspace.projectId dispatches loadWorkspaceTsConfig (and
clearTsConfig when there is no project).

MODIFIED client/src/components/Editor/EditorArea.jsx
  - Models are created with a real file URI (file:///<path>) so the TS worker
treats them as files on disk.
  - onMount calls applyMonacoTsConfig(monaco, latestConfig); a second effect
re-applies it whenever the config changes, keeping a ref of the latest value so a
late-mounting editor is configured too.

NET EFFECT: "@/" and other tsconfig alias imports, plus JSX, no longer show red
squiggles, and the options genuinely come from the project's tsconfig.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
2. VITE NO LONGER FULL-PAGE RELOADS ON WORKSPACE ACTIVITY
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
WHY IT RELOADED: the dev server's root is client/, but Vite picks its watch root
by walking up for a workspace/lockfile, so it ended up watching the whole repo —
including server/workspaces, the live sandbox where every imported project, file
write, `npm install` and terminal command lands. Vite treated those as project
changes and did a FULL reload of CodeMatrix itself.

MODIFIED client/vite.config.js
  server.watch.ignored = ['**/server/workspaces/**', '**/server/node_modules/**',
                          '**/.git/**']
  Glob patterns (not absolute paths) keep it working on Windows. Now only real
edits under client/src trigger HMR; terminal/file/npm activity no longer reloads
the tab.

TERMINAL `cd`: no code change was needed. Each tab is a real pty spawned with
cwd = server/workspaces/<projectId>, and terminal:input is forwarded to the shell
verbatim, so `cd <folder>` changes that shell's own working directory and later
`dir`/`ls` run from inside it. It only looked broken while the watcher was
reloading the page out from under the terminal.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
3. DELETE CONFIRMATION
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
NEW client/src/components/Common/ConfirmModal.jsx + .css
  Reusable dark-theme dialog (overlay + card). Escape / backdrop click cancel;
the confirm button is autofocused and destructive confirm is a red button.

MODIFIED client/src/components/Sidebar/Sidebar.jsx
  Both the hover trash icon and the context-menu Delete call requestDelete(),
which opens the modal:
    title:  Delete '<name>'?
    file:   "This action cannot be undone."
    folder: "This will permanently delete this folder and everything inside it.
             This action cannot be undone."
  The delete API call only runs on confirm.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
4. EXPLORER UNDO / REDO (Ctrl+Z / Ctrl+Y)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
NEW client/src/features/explorerHistorySlice.js
  { undoStack, redoStack } capped at 20 entries (EXPLORER_HISTORY_LIMIT).
Reducers recordExplorerAction (push + cap + clear the redo branch),
shiftUndoToRedo, shiftRedoToUndo, clearExplorerHistory. Pure bookkeeping — no I/O.

NEW client/src/features/explorerHistoryThunks.js
  - deleteExplorerEntry: snapshots the subtree BEFORE deleting (every folder path
plus every file's content, fetched from the server), deletes, then records a
{ kind:'delete', snapshot } entry.
  - revertExplorerEntry / applyExplorerEntry run an entry backwards / forwards:
      create    -> delete the created path          / re-create it
      delete    -> restore folders + files from the / delete it again
                   snapshot
      rename    -> rename newPath back to oldPath   / oldPath -> newPath
      move      -> same as rename                   / same as rename
      duplicate -> delete the copy's newPath        / duplicate again
    Renames/moves go through renameWorkspaceEntry, so open tabs are remapped.
  - undoLastExplorerAction / redoLastExplorerAction read the top of the relevant
stack, run it, move the entry to the other stack, then refresh the tree from the
backend (the true state).

MODIFIED client/src/hooks/useGlobalShortcuts.js
  Ctrl/Cmd+Z undoes; Ctrl+Y and Ctrl+Shift+Z redo. isTextEntryFocused() bails out
when focus is in Monaco (its hidden textarea), an <input> or a contenteditable,
so Monaco's own per-file text undo is untouched. Failures show an error toast.

MODIFIED client/src/components/Sidebar/Sidebar.jsx
  create, rename, move (cut-paste) and duplicate (copy-paste) each record an entry
after they succeed. MODIFIED client/src/App.jsx clears the history when the
active workspace changes.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
5. KEYBOARD CUT / COPY / PASTE IN THE EXPLORER (Ctrl+X / Ctrl+C / Ctrl+V)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
MODIFIED client/src/components/Sidebar/FileTree.jsx
  - The tree element is focusable (tabIndex -1) and is focused on mousedown
(except on text fields), giving the shortcuts a scope that cannot overlap Monaco
or the search inputs.
  - handleKeyDown reuses the exact same clipboardSlice state and handlers as the
context menu: Ctrl+C copies the selected row, Ctrl+X cuts it, Ctrl+V pastes into
the selected folder — or the workspace root when a file/nothing is selected. It
ignores events from inputs/textareas/contenteditable.

MODIFIED client/src/components/Sidebar/Sidebar.css
  Selected rows get an accent left stripe (inset box-shadow) + a white label on
top of the existing background, so the selected row is unmistakable.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
NOTES FOR THE NEXT DEVELOPER / AI TOOL
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  - Path aliases are best-effort: the TS worker only resolves against models that
    are OPEN in the editor, so full cross-file type-checking is still not
    possible. That is exactly why the cannot-find-* codes above are ignored — do
    not "fix" that by enabling noSemanticValidation, which would hide real errors
    too.
  - tsconfig `extends` (e.g. "extends": "next/tsconfig.json") is NOT followed;
    only the file's own compilerOptions are read.
  - Explorer undo/redo is per-workspace and in-memory only (no persistence) and
    capped at 20 entries; a fresh action clears the redo branch.
  - Undoing a delete re-uploads the saved content, so undoing the delete of a
    huge folder is slow — acceptable for now.
  - Keyboard cut/copy/paste and Explorer undo/redo both ignore keystrokes whose
    target is an input/textarea/contenteditable; keep that guard if you add more
    Explorer shortcuts.

================================================================================
AI Code Editor — Phase 13: Cross-Platform Path Audit, Display-Name Disk Folders,
               Terminals Start Above the Project, Terminal Copy/Paste
================================================================================

STATUS: complete. `node --check` passes on every server source file; client
        oxlint (0/0) and `vite build` pass. Verified against a live server and a
        real pty.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
0. CROSS-PLATFORM PATH AUDIT (production runs on Linux)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Searched the whole server tree for backslashes, drive letters ("C:\..."), manual
'/'-or-'\' string joins, and process.cwd()/__dirname misuse.

FINDING (the only one): src/utils/terminalManager.js findWindowsBash() built the
Git-Bash candidate paths with backslash template literals:
    `${process.env.ProgramFiles || 'C:\\Program Files'}\\Git\\bin\\bash.exe`

FIX: those paths are now composed with path.win32.join(...); no backslash or
drive-letter literal remains anywhere in the server. findWindowsBash() stays
Windows-only (called only when process.platform === 'win32'), so none of it can
run on the Linux deployment.

CONFIRMED CORRECT (no change needed):
  - workspaceManager.js builds every disk path with path.join / path.resolve,
    rooted at WORKSPACES_ROOT, which is path.resolve(env.workspacesRoot) or
    path.join(SERVER_ROOT, 'workspaces') where SERVER_ROOT derives from
    fileURLToPath(import.meta.url) (the ESM equivalent of __dirname). The only
    forward-slash strings are API-RELATIVE paths, and those are produced with
    `.split(path.sep).join('/')` so they are correct on Windows too.
  - resolveInsideWorkspace() resolves + path.relative()-checks every user path,
    so traversal escapes nothing on either OS.
  - terminalManager.getAvailableShells() branches on process.platform:
    win32 -> PowerShell / CMD (+ Git Bash only if it actually exists);
    linux/darwin -> bash. resolveShell() falls back to the platform default, so a
    stale 'bash' request can never assume a Windows shell exists.
  - env.js loads .env via path.resolve(here, '..', '..', '.env'); server.js
    mkdirs WORKSPACES_ROOT recursively. No hardcoded separators anywhere.
  - Every fs.* call receives a value produced by path.join / resolve / dirname.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
1. DISK FOLDER NAME NOW MATCHES THE PROJECT'S DISPLAY NAME
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
WHY: folders were created as server/workspaces/<projectId> (a raw UUID) while the
Explorer shows the human name ("shahzaib.art") as its root — so `cd shahzaib.art`
in the terminal could never work.

NEW NAMING: sanitized display name + a deterministic short suffix
  sanitizeFolderName('shahzaib.art')  -> 'shahzaib-art'
  folder name                         -> 'shahzaib-art-<4 hex of sha1(projectId)>'
  e.g. 'shahzaib-art-6822'
Collisions (same name, different project) extend the hash slice; the operation is
idempotent, so re-importing reuses the same folder.

LAYOUT: every user gets a per-user container so a terminal can start one level
above the project:
  server/workspaces/
    .workspaces.json                  <- registry: projectId -> { container, folderName }
    u-<ownerId>/                      <- the terminal's start directory
      shahzaib-art-6822/              <- the project (its real files)
      scratch-<hash>/                 <- the scratch workspace, same container

TERMINALS START ONE LEVEL ABOVE the project folder (the container), so the
project shows up as a genuine, enterable subfolder matching the Explorer's root
node. terminal:ready now also carries `folderName`, and the client prints:
  "Project folder: shahzaib-art-6822 — run  cd shahzaib-art-6822"

SERVER CHANGES
  - models/Project.model.js        + diskFolderName (String)
  - models/project.repository.js   upsert/createProject persist diskFolderName
  - utils/workspaceManager.js      + on-disk registry (.workspaces.json),
      sanitizeFolderName, workspaceOwnerKey, registerWorkspaceFolder,
      registerWorkspaceFromDiskPath, getWorkspaceRecord, getTerminalRootPath,
      getWorkspaceFolderName; getWorkspacePath / ensureScratchWorkspace /
      createWorkspaceFolder / deleteWorkspaceFolder now resolve + maintain it.
      A legacy folder named after the raw projectId still resolves (fallback).
  - controllers/project.controller.js   names the folder from the display name,
      stores diskFolderName, returns it in the public project.
  - controllers/workspace.controller.js (import) does the same.
  - utils/terminalManager.js        cwd = getTerminalRootPath(projectId); the
      terminal:ready payload gains folderName.
  - middlewares/ensureWorkspace.js  rebuilds the registry from Project.diskPath
      if the registry file is ever lost but the folder survived (so the Mongo
      metadata is not write-only).
  - client components/Terminal/TerminalView.jsx  prints the cd hint.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
2. REAL COPY / PASTE INSIDE THE TERMINAL
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
MODIFIED client/src/components/Terminal/TerminalView.jsx + TerminalPanel.css
  - Ctrl/Cmd+C: WITH a selection, it copies via
    navigator.clipboard.writeText(term.getSelection()) and swallows the key (no
    \x03). With NO selection it returns true, so xterm sends the normal interrupt
    byte — Ctrl+C still kills a running process.
  - Ctrl/Cmd+V (and Ctrl+Shift+V): navigator.clipboard.readText() is written into
    the pty through the existing 'terminal:input' socket event, as if typed.
    preventDefault() stops the browser's own paste from double-firing.
  - Right-click on a terminal with an active selection copies it.
  - If clipboard access is denied/unavailable, a small inline overlay appears
    inside THAT terminal ("Clipboard access denied — check browser permissions")
    and auto-dismisses after 4s.
  - All of it is scoped with term.attachCustomKeyEventHandler / a contextmenu
    listener on the terminal element, so the Explorer's Ctrl+C/X/V shortcuts and
    the global Ctrl+Z/Y are untouched (they only fire with the relevant focus).

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  - node --check on every server source file: passes.
  - client oxlint 0/0 and vite build: pass.
  - Isolated workspaceManager run against a temp WORKSPACES_ROOT:
      sanitizeFolderName('shahzaib.art') = 'shahzaib-art';
      create -> '<root>/u-<owner>/shahzaib-art-<hash>', idempotent on re-run;
      getTerminalRootPath === path.dirname(getWorkspacePath);
      a second project with the same display name gets a DIFFERENT folder;

  - Live server on a spare port (4100): health ok, DB connected, shells show the

    ...\u-6ab657aba46195d79efa2e75\shahzaib-art-6822 — the terminal really enters
