NOTES FOR THE NEXT DEVELOPER / AI TOOL
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  - The disk folder name is derived from the DISPLAY name; two same-named
    projects get different hash suffixes. The registry (.workspaces.json) is the
    fast path for projectId -> folder; MongoDB's diskPath/diskFolderName is the
    durable copy, and ensureWorkspace rebuilds the registry from it when needed.
    Keep the two in step if you change folder creation.
  - SCRATCH is a real project-shaped folder inside the same per-user container, so
    the Explorer and the terminal still agree on one directory tree.
  - Legacy projects (folders named after the raw projectId) still resolve via the
    fallback, but their terminal starts at the workspaces root and `cd` needs the
    UUID — re-import to get the friendly name.
  - Terminal copy/paste deliberately makes Ctrl+C copy ONLY when text is selected;
    do NOT make plain Ctrl+C always copy or you lose the interrupt signal.

================================================================================
AI Code Editor — Phase 14: CORRECTION — Workspace Folders Really Are Named After
               the Project, plus a Startup Migration for Existing Projects
================================================================================

STATUS: complete. `node --check` on every server file passes; client oxlint (0/0)
        and `vite build` pass. Verified against the live DB, disk and a real pty.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
WHY PHASE 13 WAS NOT GOOD ENOUGH
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Phase 13 DID create display-based folders for NEW imports, but for the projects
that already existed it did nothing: their folder was still the raw UUID, so the
client was changed to print a HINT ("Project folder: 55aeb831-… — run cd
55aeb831-…"). That is not a fix — it just told the user the wrong name. It also
appended a hash suffix to EVERY new folder ("shahzaib-art-6822") and lowercased /
stripped dots, so even new projects never matched the visible name exactly.
This phase removes the hint and fixes the real thing.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
1. THE REAL FOLDER NOW USES THE VISIBLE PROJECT NAME
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
sanitizeFolderName() was rewritten to stay as close to the original as possible:
  - ONLY genuinely unsafe characters are replaced with '-': / \ : * ? " < > |
    plus control characters.
  - dots, spaces, case and unicode are KEPT, so the Explorer's root name and the
    on-disk name are the same ("shahzaib.art" -> folder "shahzaib.art").
  - leading/trailing dots and spaces are trimmed (Windows cannot store those) and
    Windows device names (CON, PRN, AUX, NUL, COM1-9, LPT1-9) are guarded.

COLLISIONS: no suffix by default. The plain sanitized name is used unless it is
already taken IN THE SAME CONTAINER, in which case "-2", "-3", … is appended
(e.g. "shahzaib.art", "shahzaib.art-2"). "Taken" means the registry maps it to a
different project, or a folder of that name already exists that is not the
project's own current folder.

LAYOUT (unchanged from Phase 13):
  server/workspaces/u-<ownerId>/<project's visible name>/
Terminals still start at the container (one level above) so the project is a real
subfolder. PREFER the name over an ID everywhere: getWorkspacePath(), every file
operation, the terminal cwd and the migration all resolve via the registry entry
(`folderName`), never via the projectId.

MongoDB keeps diskFolderName (the real folder name) and diskPath (its absolute
path); the registry (.workspaces.json) is the fast path and ensureWorkspace can
rebuild it from diskPath.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
2. STARTUP MIGRATION FOR EXISTING PROJECTS
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
NEW server/src/utils/workspaceMigration.js  ->  migrateWorkspaceFolders()
Runs from server.js start() right after connectDatabase(), BEFORE listen:
  1. loads every Project record (most-recently-used first, so when several share
     a display name the ACTIVE one keeps the plain name and the duplicates get
     -2, -3 — the empty duplicates are the ones suffixed);
  2. for each project whose folder still exists, computes the intended container
     (u-<ownerId>) and name, planning the whole set first so two projects can
     never be assigned the same name;
  3. fs.renameSync()s the old folder (workspaces/<uuid>) to
     workspaces/u-<owner>/<name>, creating the container with fs.mkdirSync;
  4. writes the registry entry and updates the Project document
     (diskPath + diskFolderName);
  5. skips projects whose folder is gone (ephemeral disk) and never clobbers an
     existing folder.
It is idempotent — a second boot finds everything already in place and only
re-syncs the registry/DB rows.

NEW repository helpers: listAllProjects(), updateProjectDiskInfo().
NEW workspaceManager exports: workspaceFolderPath(), setWorkspaceRecord(),
resolveExistingWorkspaceFolder(); getWorkspaceFolderName() was removed (it only
existed for the deleted hint).

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
3. HINT MESSAGE REMOVED
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
client/components/Terminal/TerminalView.jsx no longer prints
"Project folder: … — run cd …"; the server no longer sends `folderName` in
terminal:ready. After the migration the user types `cd shahzaib.art` — exactly
what the Explorer shows — and it just works, with no explanation needed.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
VERIFICATION PERFORMED THIS ENTRY
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  - Isolated run against a temp WORKSPACES_ROOT:
      sanitizeFolderName('shahzaib.art') = 'shahzaib.art'; 'My Cool Project'
      stays 'My Cool Project'; unsafe chars -> '-'; trailing dots trimmed;
      three same-named projects -> 'shahzaib.art', 'shahzaib.art-2',
      'shahzaib.art-3'; getTerminalRootPath is the folder's parent; re-running
      createWorkspaceFolder is idempotent.
  - REAL migration on the dev database + disk. Before: three projects named
    "shahzaib.art" (owner …cedba9f08) living in UUID folders, one with 113 files
    (client/, server/, client/lib/data.js) and two empty. After restart:
      server/workspaces/u-6ab62726ce74736cedba9f08/shahzaib.art     (113 files)
      server/workspaces/u-6ab62726ce74736cedba9f08/shahzaib.art-2   (empty)
      server/workspaces/u-6ab62726ce74736cedba9f08/shahzaib.art-3   (empty)
    The registry maps each projectId to its new folder and every Project document
    now carries the matching diskFolderName + diskPath. The two projects whose
    folders were already gone (CB Test, E2E Project) were left untouched.
  - REAL pty test (cmd.exe) against the migrated project 55aeb831-…:
      session cwd = …\workspaces\u-6ab62726ce74736cedba9f08  (one level above)
      typed `cd shahzaib.art` -> prompt became …\shahzaib.art
      `dir /b` printed: client, data.js, server
    i.e. the user can `cd` straight into the folder whose name they see.
  - The server on :4000 was restarted and is now running this code (the migration
    runs on every boot and was a no-op on the restart after the first pass).

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
NOTES FOR THE NEXT DEVELOPER / AI TOOL
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  - Generate NEW workspace folders with registerWorkspaceFolder(projectId,
    { ownerId, displayName }) and ALWAYS resolve paths through getWorkspacePath()
    / the registry — do not build a path from the projectId by hand.
  - The migration is safe to keep forever; it is the thing that heals folders left
    by older code. If you rename the naming rules again, extend it (and keep it
    idempotent + never-clobber).
  - Folder names may contain dots and spaces (by design, to mirror the visible
    name). A name with spaces needs shell quoting (`cd My\ Project`), which is
    normal; names without spaces (the common case) work unquoted.

================================================================================
AI Code Editor — Phase 15: Live Tree Auto-Refresh (chokidar + Socket.IO) and the
               TSX False-Error Root Cause + Monaco Diagnostics Fix
================================================================================

STATUS: complete. `node --check` on every server file passes; client oxlint (0/0)
        and `vite build` pass. Both features verified live (real npm install +
        real Socket.IO client).

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
1. EXPLORER AUTO-REFRESHES WHEN THE FOLDER CHANGES ON DISK
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
WHY: the tree was only re-read on an explicit app action, so anything the
terminal did (npm install, git checkout, a build) left the Explorer stale.

NEW DEPENDENCY: chokidar ^4.0.3 (server). v4 rather than v5 on purpose — v5
requires Node >= 20.19, v4 works on Node >= 14.16, which is safer for the deploy.

NEW server/src/utils/fileWatcher.js
  - One refcounted chokidar watcher per ACTIVE project:
      startWatchingProject(projectId, notify)  -> +1 ref (starts on first call)
      stopWatchingProject(projectId)           -> -1 ref (closes at zero)
      closeAllWatchers()                       -> server shutdown
  - Watches the project folder (getWorkspacePath) and listens to add / addDir /
    unlink / unlinkDir / change.
  - DEBOUNCE: every event resets a 750ms timer; only when things go quiet is the
    single notify(projectId) fired. A burst of thousands of events (npm install)
    therefore collapses into ONE refresh.
  - node_modules/.git are never descended into: the top-level directory's own
    addDir/unlinkDir still fires, but nothing inside is watched.
  - Defence in depth: the same ignore rules are ALSO applied to every incoming
    event before it can schedule a refresh, because a directory created AFTER the
    watcher started (npm install creating node_modules) can otherwise leak events
    for its children.
  - The watched root itself is never ignored (chokidar calls `ignored` for it;
    returning true there silently watches nothing).
  - If the project folder does not exist yet (fresh scratch workspace, import in
    progress) it watches the parent container instead and scopes events to the
    project path, so it starts reporting the moment the folder is created.

NEW server/src/socket/workspaceSocket.js
  client -> server:  workspace:watch   { projectId }  (project became active)
                     workspace:unwatch { projectId }  (project changed/closed)
  server -> client:  workspace:treeChanged { projectId }  (debounced, emitted to
                     the project's room `workspace:<projectId>`)
  Watching is reference-counted per socket, so several tabs never create duplicate
  watchers and the watcher is released on unwatch or disconnect.
  Registered from server.js next to the terminal socket; shutdown also calls
  closeAllWatchers().

NEW client/src/hooks/useWorkspaceWatcher.js  (called from App.jsx)
  - On an active workspace it emits workspace:watch; on change/unmount it emits
    workspace:unwatch. It depends only on projectId + source (not the workspace
    object, whose identity changes on every refresh) so the subscription is not
    torn down repeatedly.
  - On workspace:treeChanged for the CURRENT project it debounces 400ms and then
    dispatches refreshWorkspaceTree({ projectId }) — i.e. GET
    /api/workspace/:projectId/tree — which replaces the tree in Redux and prunes
    dead tabs/buffers exactly like a manual refresh.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
2. TSX FALSE ERRORS — ROOT CAUSE AND FIX
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
INVESTIGATION
  - Confirmed the model IS created with a real file URI and the correct language:
    EditorArea passes path={`file:///${activeNode.id}`} (so the URI ends in
    ".tsx") and language = getLanguageFromFilename(name), which is 'typescript'
    for .tsx (utils/language.js). Monaco's TS worker derives the script kind (TSX
    vs TS) from the URI extension, so JSX PARSING was already enabled — the
    remaining red squiggles were NOT syntax errors.
  - The persistent errors were SEMANTIC: Monaco runs the language service in a
    single-file worker with no node_modules, no @types/react and no other open
    files, so it reports unknown types, unresolved modules and JSX-attribute type
    errors on perfectly valid code.
  - Second contributor: buildMonacoCompilerOptions honoured the project's own
    tsconfig `"jsx"` value, and Next.js writes `"jsx": "preserve"`. For the
    language service "preserve" means the classic React.createElement factory,
    which needs a `React` symbol the worker does not have.

FIX (client/src/utils/monacoTsconfig.js)
  - jsx is now ALWAYS JsxEmit.ReactJSX (the tsconfig value is deliberately not
    used) — the correct setting for type-checking modern JSX in a single-file
    worker.
  - target defaults to ES2020 (or later, from the tsconfig when present);
    moduleResolution Bundler; esModuleInterop, allowNonTsExtensions,
    resolveJsonModule, skipLibCheck and noEmit are all set.
  - setDiagnosticsOptions({ noSemanticValidation: true, noSyntaxValidation: false })
    — the pragmatic middle ground: SYNTAX validation stays on (so genuinely
    malformed JSX, e.g. an unclosed tag, is still reported) while the flood of
    false SEMANTIC errors disappears. DIAGNOSTIC_CODES_TO_IGNORE is kept as a
    belt-and-braces guard.
  - EditorArea.jsx carries a comment explaining that the model URI must keep the
    real ".tsx" extension and why.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
VERIFICATION PERFORMED THIS ENTRY
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  - node --check on every server file: passes. Client oxlint 0/0 and vite build:
    pass.
  - Watcher, driven live against a running server with socket.io-client:
      A. workspace:watch on a NOT-YET-EXISTING project folder, then creating the
         folder + a file  -> exactly 1 treeChanged event.
      B. a REAL `npm install left-pad` inside the watched folder -> exactly 1
         debounced treeChanged event, and node_modules/ exists on disk.
      C. writing 300 files INSIDE node_modules -> 0 events.
      D. adding a new top-level folder (client/index.js) -> exactly 1 event.
    Every test folder was removed afterwards.
  - Direct chokidar probe confirming that only the top-level file event fires and
    node_modules churn is ignored by the matcher.
  - Server was restarted on :4000 and is running this code.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
CLEANUP NOTE
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
The first npm-install test ran in a worker folder that had no package.json, so
npm walked up to server/package.json and installed left-pad into the repo. That
was detected and fully reverted (npm uninstall): server/package.json,
server/package-lock.json and server/node_modules are verified free of left-pad,
and chokidar is the only new dependency. The test then ran again with a local
package.json present.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
NOTES FOR THE NEXT DEVELOPER / AI TOOL
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  - The watcher is lazily started by the client's workspace:watch; there is no
    background watcher for inactive projects. Keep start/stop balanced — they are
    reference-counted, and an unbalanced start leaks a watcher until shutdown.
  - Two debounces are deliberate: 750ms server-side (batches the file-system
    storm) and 400ms client-side (coalesces anything still arriving, including
    our own save events).
  - Auto-refresh re-reads the tree only; it does NOT refresh file contents already
    open in the editor (Monaco keeps the user's buffer) nor the search-in-files
    content cache.
  - Semantic TS validation is intentionally OFF. If you ever want real type
    checking in the editor, the correct fix is to feed the worker virtual files
    (ambient .d.ts / a project service), not to re-enable noSemanticValidation.


================================================================================
FIX: Import-time exclusion vs. workspace-tree display are now FULLY separate
================================================================================

STATUS: complete and verified.

THE BUG
The Explorer hid folders that physically exist on disk. After running
`npm install` in a project's terminal, node_modules existed on the server but
never appeared in the tree; the same was true of .git, dist, build, .next, etc.

ROOT CAUSE
There were TWO independent exclusion lists, and the tree walker wrongly reused
the import concept:

  1. client/src/services/fileSystemAccess.js
       const IMPORT_SKIP_DIRECTORIES = new Set(['node_modules', '.git', 'dist',
                                                'build', 'out', '.next', '.cache'])
     This was CORRECT — it filters what gets read off the user's LOCAL LAPTOP
     and uploaded during import.

  2. server/src/utils/workspaceManager.js
       const SKIP_DIRECTORIES = new Set(['node_modules', '.git'])
     ...used by walkDirectory(), which getWorkspaceTree() (the
     GET /api/workspace/:projectId/tree endpoint) is built on. This is the BUG:
     the tree-display walk was applying an exclusion filter at all. Any folder
     named node_modules or .git on disk was silently omitted from the tree, no
     matter how it got there (import, `npm install`, `git init`, `npm run build`,
     or any other terminal command).

THE FIX — two distinct, independent pieces of logic
  * IMPORT-TIME EXCLUSION (unchanged behaviour, clearer intent)
      client/src/services/fileSystemAccess.js
      - Constant renamed SKIP_DIRECTORIES -> IMPORT_SKIP_DIRECTORIES, with a
        comment stating it applies ONLY to readAllFilesFromDirectory() while
        uploading a folder from the laptop. node_modules, .git, dist, build,
        out, .next and .cache continue to be skipped so huge/regenerable data is
        never uploaded.

  * WORKSPACE-TREE DISPLAY (zero filtering)
      server/src/utils/workspaceManager.js
      - The server-side SKIP_DIRECTORIES constant was DELETED entirely.
      - walkDirectory() no longer has any skip branch. It now returns 100% of
        what is on the server's disk for the project — every file and every
        folder, node_modules/.git/dist/build/.next included, at any depth.
      - getWorkspaceTree() and the /tree endpoint therefore reflect the real
        contents of the workspace exactly.

WHY THE WATCHER STILL BEHAVES (and is NOT a filter)
  server/src/utils/fileWatcher.js keeps NEVER_DESCEND_INTO = { node_modules,
  .git } ONLY to bound how deep chokidar reports live changes (watching every
  file in node_modules would swamp the process). It does NOT remove anything from
  the tree: the top-level addDir for node_modules still fires, so the debounced
  auto-refresh re-fetches the full tree and node_modules (with its contents)
  shows up. A comment was added to the constant making this explicit so it is not
  mistaken for a display filter again.

VERIFICATION PERFORMED THIS ENTRY
  - node --check on workspaceManager.js and fileWatcher.js: passes.
  - Direct getWorkspaceTree() run against a real workspace folder containing
    node_modules/ (with left-pad + .package-lock.json), .git/, dist/, build/,
    out/, .next/ and a nested deep/node_modules: ALL CHECKS PASSED — every folder
    appears at the top level, node_modules is an expandable folder listing its
    real children, and nested node_modules folders are present too.
  - The temporary verification script created for this run was removed; only
    workspaceManager.js, fileWatcher.js and fileSystemAccess.js changed.

RESULT
The Explorer now shows exactly what is on disk. Import exclusion and tree
 display are two separate concerns: the only place that filters names is the
 client import reader, and the tree walker filters nothing.


================================================================================
FIX: Freezing/lag when pasting files or folders (paste + auto-refresh)
================================================================================

STATUS: complete and verified. The severe freeze was NOT one bug — it was the
paste feature and the chokidar auto-refresh stacking three blocking costs. The
root causes were 1 and 2 below; 3 and 4 were confirmed/improved.

ROOT CAUSE 1 — duplicate tree fetch per paste (refresh budget doubled)
duplicateWorkspaceEntry (client/src/features/workspaceThunks.js) awaited a
MANUAL refreshWorkspaceTree() immediately after the POST /duplicate 201, and the
chokidar watcher ALSO saw the copied files and emitted workspace:treeChanged,
which the client turns into another refresh. So every paste fetched the whole
tree TWICE. Fixed by deleting the manual refetch and letting the watcher be the
single source of truth (one debounced treeChanged -> exactly one fetch).

ROOT CAUSE 2 — synchronous, blocking recursive copy (server froze)
workspaceManager.copyPathInWorkspace() -> copyDirectoryRecursive() used
fs.mkdirSync / fs.readdirSync / fs.copyFileSync in a recursive loop. Copying a
folder containing a node_modules-like tree held the Node.js event loop for the
ENTIRE copy, so the terminal and every other API call stalled — this is what
felt like "the whole app freezing". Fixed by converting the copy to fs.promises
(async) with a yield to the event loop every COPY_YIELD_BATCH (250) files.
copyPathInWorkspace is now async and the controller awaits it.

Also fixed while auditing the same freeze: getWorkspaceTree()/walkDirectory()
had the identical problem — a synchronous fs.readdirSync recursion that walked
EVERY file, and after the tree-fix above that includes all of node_modules on
every single auto-refresh. The walk is now async (fs.promises.readdir, which
yields between directories), and both callers (import + GET /tree) await it.
The tree is still 100% unfiltered — only the blocking characteristic changed.

ROOT CAUSE 3 — chokidar deep-watching node_modules: CONFIRMED NOT THE CAUSE
fileWatcher.js already refuses to descend into node_modules/.git: the custom
`ignored` function is true for anything strictly INSIDE them (so only the
top-level addDir/unlinkDir is watched), and the same isRelevant() filter is
re-applied to every event. No deep file watchers are registered. Verified live
below. A clarifying comment was added; no behaviour change was needed.

ROOT CAUSE 4 — Explorer re-rendered the entire tree on every refresh: IMPROVED
Every refresh built brand-new node objects, so React re-rendered every row even
when nothing in most branches changed — expensive once node_modules adds
thousands of rows.
  - toReduxTree() (client/src/utils/cloudTree.js) now does STRUCTURAL SHARING:
an unchanged node (same name/type/path/open state and identical child object
references) is returned as the PREVIOUS object, so whole subtrees keep identity.
  - The tree row is wrapped in React.memo (FileTree.jsx), and the Sidebar
callbacks passed into it (submitCreate, handleRename, requestDelete,
handleCancelCreate, reportError) are now useCallback-stable, so the shallow prop
comparison actually holds. An auto-refresh now re-renders only changed branches.

TEMP DIAGNOSTIC LOGGING (safe to remove)
  - server/src/controllers/workspace.controller.js
      [duplicate] <project> <src> -> <newPath> copied in <ms>
      [tree] <project> walked in <ms>
  - server/src/utils/fileWatcher.js
      [watcher] <project>: <N> fs event(s) -> 1 treeChanged (<ms>)
    The last line is the proof a bulk paste collapses into ONE refresh.

VERIFICATION PERFORMED THIS ENTRY
  - ASYNC COPY: copied a real folder of 1200 files (20 nested groups)
    via copyPathInWorkspace -> all files copied, contents intact, and the event
    loop served 1171 setInterval ticks DURING the 1376ms copy (a synchronous copy
    would produce ~0). ALL CHECKS PASSED.
  - WATCHER: on a workspace containing node_modules, writing 200 files INSIDE
    node_modules produced 0 notifies; one top-level write produced exactly 1
    debounced notify. Log line printed: "1 fs event(s) -> 1 treeChanged".
    ALL CHECKS PASSED.
  - ASYNC TREE: getWorkspaceTree() returns the copied folder as an expandable
    node (20 children) and still lists the original — no filtering.
  - node --check on all changed server files: passes. Client oxlint: 0/0 (56
    files). Client vite build: succeeds.
  - Temporary verification scripts were removed; no test artefacts left behind.


================================================================================
FIX: Explorer not updating live after paste (restore explicit post-action
     refresh, keep chokidar for terminal/external changes)
================================================================================

STATUS: complete and verified.

PROBLEM
The Explorer only showed a pasted file after a manual page reload. The backend
was correct (every paste created the right files), so this was purely a UI
refresh problem. The previous fix (entry above) had REMOVED the manual tree
refetch that followed a successful paste and relied SOLELY on the chokidar
watcher. Chokidar can miss changes written by the same Node.js process that
performs the write, so the UI had no reliable refresh trigger of its own — it
was depending on a mechanism designed for something else.

FIX — two independent refresh paths, each with a clear owner
1) EXPLICIT, DIRECT REFRESH FOR EVERY UI ACTION. Every Explorer mutation thunk
   in client/src/features/workspaceThunks.js now awaits
   refreshWorkspaceTree({ projectId }).unwrap() right after its API call
   succeeds, so the tree updates the instant the action resolves — no watcher
   involved:
     - createWorkspaceEntry   (create file/folder)
     - deleteWorkspaceEntry   (delete)
     - renameWorkspaceEntry   (rename AND cut+paste move)
     - duplicateWorkspaceEntry (copy+paste)  <-- the refetch was restored here
   The old comment claiming the manual refetch was intentionally removed has
   been replaced by one explaining why it must stay.
2) CHOKIDAR + SOCKET STAY AS A SEPARATE PATH, unchanged, for changes that have
   NO UI callback to hook onto: terminal commands (npm install, git, scripts).
   useWorkspaceWatcher -> workspace:watch -> fileWatcher (750ms debounce) ->
   workspace:treeChanged -> client (400ms debounce) -> refreshWorkspaceTree.
   This is untouched and still required.

NO DOUBLE-FETCH STACKING
  - The two paths are not simultaneous for the same change: the explicit
    refetch is immediate, while the watcher's event arrives ~750ms (server) +
    400ms (client) later and coalesces into one extra cheap fetch. That extra
    fetch is expected and harmless.
  - A genuine duplicate was removed inside undo/redo. explorerHistoryThunks.js
    called revertExplorerEntry/applyExplorerEntry (whose branches already go
    through refetching thunks) and THEN fetched the tree again, so an undo/redo
    fetched twice. The trailing fetch is gone; the snapshot-restore branch
    (restoreSnapshot uses raw API calls, not thunks) now performs the single
    explicit refresh itself. Net result: exactly ONE tree fetch per undo/redo.
  - The freeze fix from the previous entry is untouched: the copy/tree walk
    remain async, so an immediate refetch cannot block the event loop.

TEMP DIAGNOSTIC LOGGING still present and unchanged (see previous entry).

VERIFICATION PERFORMED THIS ENTRY
  A) CLIENT THUNKS, exercised for real via Vite's SSR module loader with a
     stubbed fetch that counts GET /tree calls:
       - 3 pastes in a row (different targets) -> 3 tree fetches (one each).
       - folder paste -> 1 fetch.
       - create + rename + delete -> 3 fetches (one each).
       - undo create -> 1; redo duplicate -> 1; undo delete (snapshot) -> 1.
     ALL CHECKS PASSED. Confirms the UI refreshes itself on every action, and
     that undo/redo no longer double-fetch.
  B) CHOKIDAR / TERMINAL PATH, server-side harness on a real workspace folder:
       - simulated npm install (create node_modules/left-pad + 50 files) ->
         exactly 1 debounced notify, log "1 fs event(s) -> 1 treeChanged".
       - 30 subsequent writes INSIDE node_modules -> 0 extra notifies.
       - one top-level write (git-checkout analog) -> 1 more notify.
     ALL CHECKS PASSED. Terminal/external changes still refresh via chokidar
     with no UI action involved.
  C) Client oxlint: 0 warnings / 0 errors (56 files). Client vite build:
     succeeds. Temporary verification scripts were removed.

NET BEHAVIOUR
  - UI-triggered file-system operations (paste copy, paste cut/move, create,
    delete, rename, duplicate, undo, redo) refresh the Explorer immediately and
    deterministically via an explicit post-action fetch.
  - Terminal/external changes keep flowing through the chokidar watcher + socket
    as before.
  - The two coexist without conflicting and without reintroducing the earlier
    paste freeze.


================================================================================
FIX (round 2): repeated Paste was a silent no-op — EXACT root cause found by
          logging the numbered paste chain in order
================================================================================

STATUS: complete and verified. The previous entry's explicit post-action refetch
        is correct and was NOT the (remaining) problem.

METHOD (systematic, not guessing)
Temporary [STEP1..STEP9] console.log statements were added to the real paste
chain on BOTH sides and exercised against real code:
  frontend  Sidebar.handlePaste (STEP1), api.duplicateEntry (STEP2),
            duplicateWorkspaceEntry (STEP7), refreshWorkspaceTree (STEP8),
            FileTree render (STEP9), response handling (STEP6)
  backend   workspace.controller duplicateEntry (STEP3), the post-copy
            fs.existsSync disk check (STEP4), the 201 body (STEP5)
The frontend was driven through the ACTUAL Paste context-menu item inside a real
jsdom render of <Sidebar> (not a stubbed handler), and the backend through the
real copyPathInWorkspace/getWorkspaceTree on a real workspace folder.

WHAT THE LOGS SHOWED — FIRST paste (the previously attempted fix was fine)
  STEP1 handler fired          -> yes
  STEP2 POST /duplicate sent   -> url .../api/workspace/p1/duplicate, body
                                  {sourcePath:'f.txt',targetFolderPath:'A'}
  STEP3 backend received       -> sourcePath=f.txt targetFolderPath=A
  STEP4 copy finished          -> newPath=A/f.txt, existsOnDisk: TRUE
  STEP5 response               -> 201 {sourcePath, newPath, duplicated:true}
  STEP7 refetch dispatched     -> yes, right after the 201
  STEP8 tree fetched           -> rootNewRef: TRUE, new node A/f.txt present
  STEP9 Explorer re-rendered   -> DOM gained [data-node-id="A/f.txt"] with NO
                                  reload ("pasted file visible in DOM: true")
So steps 1-9 all fire, in order, with correct data, and the UI updates. The
refetch/re-render chain was never broken for a single paste.

THE EXACT BREAKING POINT — between STEP 1 and STEP 2 on the SECOND paste
After the first successful copy-paste, Sidebar.handlePaste called
`dispatch(clearClipboard())`. On the next paste the logs read:
  [STEP1] paste handler fired { clipboard: { path: null, ... } }
  [STEP1] ABORT: no projectId or clipboard.path { activeProjectId: 'p1',
                                                  sourcePath: null }
  -> no [STEP2], no request, no refetch, no re-render.
The Paste context-menu entry also disappeared entirely (canPaste requires a
clipboard path), so a second paste into folder B/C did nothing at all. With a
single copy the user could paste ONCE; every later paste was a silent no-op,
which is perceived as "the Explorer does not update after paste". The backend was
always fine: a direct copy of the same source into a second folder returned
newPath=B/f.txt with existsOnDisk: TRUE.

ROOT CAUSE (precise)
client/src/components/Sidebar/Sidebar.jsx, handlePaste: the COPY branch
unconditionally consumed the clipboard after a successful duplicate. Unlike VS
Code (which retains the clipboard so one copy can be pasted many times), this
made the paste handler bail out at its `if (!activeProjectId || !sourcePath)
return` guard before ever building the STEP 2 request.

THE FIX (one behaviour change, no logging left behind)
  - COPY + paste now KEEPS the clipboard, so the same item can be pasted into
    A, B and C in a row with zero re-copying and zero reloads.
  - CUT + paste still CLEARS the clipboard (the source moved away, so reusing
    that reference would be wrong) — unchanged.
  - All temporary [STEP1..STEP9] logs and the temporary harness scripts were
    removed.

VERIFICATION
  - Real jsdom render of <Sidebar>, real Paste menu item clicked three times on
    one clipboard value (copy f.txt once):
      paste into A -> request #1, visible in Explorer: true, clipboard kept
      paste into B -> request #2, visible in Explorer: true, clipboard kept
      paste into C -> request #3, visible in Explorer: true, clipboard kept
      dupCalls=3, treeCalls=3, "ALL THREE PASTES WORKED WITH ZERO RELOADS: true"
  - Backend on a real workspace folder: copy f.txt -> A and later -> B both
    reported existsOnDisk: TRUE and the tree walk listed both copies.
  - client oxlint: 0 warnings / 0 errors (56 files). client vite build:
    succeeds. node --check on the controller: passes.
  - The temporary logging and harness files were deleted; only Sidebar.jsx and
    the (already-correct) thunk/history files remain changed.

FEATURE: "Export to Folder" + full tooltip audit + Settings modal with 5 themes

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
PART 1a — EXPORT TO FOLDER (separate from the ZIP export)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
NEW: client/src/hooks/useExportToFolder.js
NEW helpers in client/src/services/fileSystemAccess.js:
  - pickDirectory()            showDirectoryPicker WITHOUT touching the stored
                               import handle (so Sync keeps its original folder)
  - createNewFolder()          getDirectoryHandle(seg, { create: true }) chain
  - createNewFile()            getFileHandle(name, { create: true })
  - writeFileContent()         createWritable -> write -> close (finally)
  - writeFilesToDirectory(handle, files, { onProgress })  now reports progress
  - collectExportablePaths(serverTree)  walks the SERVER tree and returns
                               { files: [paths], skippedFolders } using
                               EXPORT_SKIP_DIRECTORIES (= the import skip list:
                               node_modules, .git, dist, build, out, .next,
                               .cache), matched by folder NAME at any depth.

HOW IT WORKS (Topbar icon -> exportToFolder):
  1. Supported-browser check (showDirectoryPicker); unsupported -> error toast.
  2. window.showDirectoryPicker() picks the destination. Cancel (AbortError) is
     ignored with an "Export cancelled." info toast.
  3. GET /api/workspace/:projectId/tree, then collectExportablePaths() plans the
     file list and counts the excluded folders it will skip.
  4. Each file's content is fetched (GET .../file?path=) with live progress.
  5. writeFilesToDirectory() recreates the exact nested structure on disk.
  6. A floating "Exporting… X of Y files" pill (Topbar.css .export-progress)
     shows progress the whole time; the button shows a spinner and is disabled.
  7. Success toast: "Exported N files to <folder>" plus, when folders were
     skipped, "— node_modules and build folders were skipped, run npm install
     there to regenerate them".

      node_modules/.git/dist returned exactly the non-excluded files and counted
      3 skipped folders; writeFilesToDirectory() wrote a real nested structure
      (src/a.txt, src/nested/b.txt, top.md) with one progress callback per file.
  - Settings validation/clamping and localStorage round-trip (theme, font size,
      preferred shell) verified.
  - monacoThemeFor('light')='vs', monacoThemeFor('forest-green')='cm-forest-green';
      terminalThemeFor('light').background='#ffffff' and differs from dark themes.
  - Client oxlint: 0 warnings / 0 errors (64 files). Client vite build: succeeds.
  - The temporary verification harness was deleted; no test artefacts remain.


================================================================================
FIX: Freezes on delete / copy / paste — root causes found and eliminated
================================================================================

STATUS: complete and verified. Client oxlint 0/0 (64 files); vite build passes;
        node --check on every changed server file passes.

METHOD: audited the watcher, the server file operations and the client refresh
path, then measured them directly on a real workspace containing 3000 files under
node_modules. Temporary stress harness was used and then deleted.

THREE contributing root causes were found (not one):

RC1 (the hard freeze) — SYNCHRONOUS RECURSIVE DELETE
  server/src/utils/workspaceManager.js deleteFromWorkspace() used
  `fs.rmSync(dir, { recursive: true })` (and deleteWorkspaceFolder() the same).
  Removing a folder that contains node_modules blocked the Node event loop for
  the ENTIRE deletion — the server, every other API call and the terminal all
  froze until it finished. This is not a tree-refresh problem: it is a blocking
  filesystem call on the main thread.
  FIX: `await fsp.rm(path, { recursive: true, force: true })` (one call handles
  files and folders). deleteWorkspaceFolder() is now async too. rename/copy were
  audited in the same pass: `fs.renameSync` -> `await fsp.rename`,
  `fs.statSync`/`fs.existsSync` guards -> `await fsp.stat` / `fsp.access`, and
  uniqueNameInFolder() (the "(copy)" name picker) is now async instead of a
  sync existsSync loop. The recursive copy itself was already async with a
  250-file yield.
  MEASURED: deleting 3000 files took 685ms with 89 event-loop ticks DURING the
  delete (a sync rmSync yields ~0 ticks). The server stayed responsive.

RC2 — CHOKIDAR WATCHED FILE-HEAVY BUILD DIRS DEEPLY
  server/src/utils/fileWatcher.js already refused to descend into node_modules
  and .git, but dist / build / out / .next / .cache were watched recursively.
  Those hold thousands of regenerable files, so chokidar maintained watchers and
  processed events for all of them on every copy/build. The watcher also had no
  depth cap.
  FIX: NEVER_DESCEND_INTO now covers node_modules, .git, dist, build, out,
  .next and .cache (the folder's OWN addDir/unlinkDir is still reported, so the
  Explorer still updates when one appears or disappears — the tree walk itself
  has NO exclusions and still displays them). Added `depth: 12` as a hard cap,
  kept `ignoreInitial: true`, and kept the reference-counted one-watcher-per-
  projectId design (startWatchingProject reuses an existing entry, stopWatching/
  disconnect release refs, closeAllWatchers on shutdown) — audited and confirmed
  there is exactly one instance per project.
  MEASURED: 100 writes INSIDE node_modules produced 0 notifies at the watcher.

RC3 — EVERY UI ACTION CAUSED TWO FULL-TREE WALKS
  The client already refetches the tree directly after create/delete/rename/
  duplicate. The chokidar watcher ALSO saw the same fs writes and, after its
  750ms debounce, emitted workspace:treeChanged, which the client turned into a
  SECOND full GET /tree. With node_modules present each walk reads and sorts
  thousands of entries, so every delete/copy/paste did the expensive work twice.
  FIX: new `suppressWatcher(projectId, ms)` in fileWatcher.js. The controller
  calls it at the start of delete / rename / duplicate / create / save / import,
  opening a ~1600ms window; a debounced notify that fires inside the window is
  dropped (logged as "suppressed (own API change)"). External changes (terminal,
  npm install, git) never call it, so they still notify normally.
  NET: exactly ONE client tree refetch per UI action.
  MEASURED: a self-suppressed top-level write produced 0 notifies; after the
  window expired a write notified again (1).

AUDITED, NO DEFECT FOUND:
  - Duplicate socket listeners: client/src/hooks/useWorkspaceWatcher.js already
    attaches `workspace:treeChanged` inside a useEffect with a matching
    socket.off cleanup, a correct [projectId, source, dispatch] dependency array
    and a client-side 400ms debounce; the server reuses one watcher per id. No
    listener/leak duplication exists.
  - Explorer memoization: TreeNode was already React.memo and toReduxTree already
    does structural sharing, so unchanged subtrees keep identity. The tree
    selector `state.fileSystem.tree` returns a stable reference across unrelated
    Redux updates (Sidebar does not subscribe to terminal/ui/editor slices), so
    unrelated state never re-maps the Explorer. Hardened anyway: FileTree itself
    is now wrapped in memo, and the Sidebar callbacks it receives were
    stabilized (cut/copy/copy-path via useCallback; paste/request-create via
    refs mirrored in an effect so no stale closures and no ref-write during
    render).

REQUIRED BEHAVIOUR TESTED (real fs, 3000-file node_modules workspace):
  - Delete node_modules (folder): 685ms, non-blocking, gone from disk, tree no
    longer lists it.
  - Copy f.txt then paste into A, B and C in quick succession: 17ms total, all
    three files exist, tree lists A/B/C plus the renamed file — no reload.
  - Rename/move f.txt -> g.txt: reflected on disk and in the tree.
  - Watcher: node_modules-internal writes 0 notifies; top-level write 1; own
    suppressed change 0; resumes after the window.
  - Client verifies: oxlint 0/0 (64 files), vite build succeeds.

CONCLUSION: delete/copy/paste are now instant and non-blocking. The freeze was
primarily RC1 (synchronous recursive delete) with RC2 (deep chokidar watching of
build dirs) and RC3 (doubled tree walks per action) compounding the lag. All
three were addressed together; no speculative features were added.

1. DELETE THE WHOLE PROJECT
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
SERVER
  - server/src/models/project.repository.js   NEW deleteProject(projectId):
    Project.findOneAndDelete({ projectId }).lean(); degrades to null with no DB.
  - server/src/controllers/project.controller.js  NEW deleteProjectHandler:
    requires a DB, 404 PROJECT_NOT_FOUND when the record is missing, 403
    FORBIDDEN when the record belongs to another user, then
    suppressWatcher(projectId) -> deleteWorkspaceFolder(projectId) (async,
    recursive) -> deleteProject(projectId). Returns { deleted: true, projectId }.

    redundant tree refresh for a workspace the client is about to clear anyway.
  - server/src/routes/project.routes.js  NEW DELETE /api/projects/:projectId
    (behind the existing `protect` JWT middleware).

  - features/workspaceThunks.js  NEW deleteWorkspaceProject thunk: calls the API,
    then clearWorkspaceRef() (forget the localStorage workspace), clears the
    file-content cache, and dispatches clearWorkspace() — which resets
    workspace to { source:'none' }, empties the tree, close every tab and clears
    the active file. That is what returns the Explorer to its "No project
    imported" empty state.
  - features/uiSlice.js        isDeleteProjectModalOpen + open/closeDeleteProjectModal
    so BOTH entry points share one modal + one code path.
  - components/Common/ConfirmModal.jsx  NEW confirmPhrase prop. When set, the

  - components/Settings/SettingsModal.jsx  Settings > General gained a Project

Typed confirmation text: "Delete project '<name>'?".

  - components/Settings/SettingsModal.jsx  NEW "Auto Save" toggle row at the top

  - hooks/useAutoSave.js (NEW)  Debounced silent save. It watches the active
    file's content, its dirty flag, the autoSave setting and the workspace.

4. WORD WRAP (and every other editor option) NOW APPLIES LIVE
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
ROOT CAUSE: the editor settings only ever reached Monaco through the <Editor>
options={...} prop. That is an initial-mount path; changing a setting afterwards
never re-applied the option to the live instance, so toggling Word wrap visibly
did nothing until the tab was re-opened.
FIX: components/Editor/EditorArea.jsx gained an effect keyed on the editor
settings object that calls the live instance directly:

    useEffect(() => {
      const editor = editorRef.current
      if (!editor) return
      editor.updateOptions({ fontSize, fontFamily, minimap: { enabled },
                             wordWrap: wordWrap ? 'on' : 'off', tabSize })
    }, [editorSettings])

The options prop still seeds the first mount. Result: Word wrap, font family,
font size, tab size and minimap now change immediately in the open editor.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Settings > Editor > Font family now offers 9 choices: System Default,
Cascadia Code, Fira Code, JetBrains Mono, Consolas, Source Code Pro, Monaco,
Menlo and Courier New, each with a monospace fallback chain. Fonts not installed
fall back gracefully (no font files are bundled). Because of the effect in item
4, changing the font updates Monaco in real time — the fontFamily was wired the
exact same way the word wrap bug was, so it is fixed by the same change.

  - utils/themes.js is the SINGLE source of truth: a SPECS array of compact

    monacoBase / monacoColors / swatches. THEMES.length is 50, spanning
    categories dark, light, colorful and high-contrast. The original five

================================================================================
FIX: Slow delete (10-15s) root-caused and eliminated + a VISIBLE Delete Project
     button on the Explorer root row
================================================================================

STATUS: complete and verified. oxlint 0/0 (65 files); vite build passes; a live
        server benchmark + a 28/28-check jsdom harness driving the REAL Sidebar
        against the LIVE API (both temp scripts deleted afterwards).

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
ROOT CAUSE OF THE SLOW DELETE — MEASURED, NOT GUESSED
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
The question was: synchronous fs calls, waiting for a full refetch, or both?
Answer: NEITHER of those two remained as the dominant cause — both had already
been fixed in the earlier "Freezes on delete" entry and were re-audited as
still correct. The remaining 10-15s was a THIRD, client-side cause:

RC1 (THE cause) — the PRE-DELETE UNDO SNAPSHOT.
  features/explorerHistoryThunks.js captureSnapshot() ran BEFORE the DELETE
  request and fetched the content of EVERY file in the subtree ONE HTTP request
  at a time (each also costing the server a MongoDB touchProject round trip).
  The delete did not even start until the last snapshot read returned.
  MEASURED against the live server: 126.8 ms/file sequential (300-file sample =
  38.05s) — i.e. ~13s for a ~100-file folder and ~380s extrapolated for the
  3000-file node_modules. That is exactly the reported 10-15s: for folders with
  hundreds/thousands of files the UI sat on this loop before anything else ran.

RC2 (minor) — the UI waited on the full refetch.
  deleteWorkspaceEntry awaited a complete GET /tree re-walk (180-232ms
  measured) before the Explorer updated, even though the client already knew
  exactly which node disappeared.

RE-AUDITED, NOT THE CAUSE:
  - The server delete path is async end to end: deleteEntry controller
    (asyncHandler) -> deleteFromWorkspace -> await fsp.rm(path, { recursive:
    true, force: true }); no rmSync/unlinkSync anywhere. MEASURED: a 3000-file
    folder is removed in 947ms with 20 /api/health probes completing DURING the
    delete (max 129ms — libuv threadpool contention, not a blocked event loop).
  - deleteWorkspaceFolder (whole-project delete) is equally async.

HOW IT WAS FIXED (client only; no server change was needed):
  - captureSnapshot is now BOUNDED: SNAPSHOT_MAX_FILES = 200, fetched with
    SNAPSHOT_CONCURRENCY = 12 through a small mapWithConcurrency helper. A
    subtree with more files is deleted with NO snapshot; the undo entry is still
    recorded (snapshot: null) and Ctrl+Z then says "Nothing restored — it was
    too large to keep an undo copy" instead of silently pretending it worked.
    Small/medium deletes still snapshot — now in parallel, so they are faster.
  - deleteWorkspaceEntry is now optimistic: on a successful DELETE response it
    immediately dispatches the NEW fileSystemSlice removeNode(path) (drops the
    node from Redux and prunes dead tabs) and then fires refreshWorkspaceTree in
    the BACKGROUND (not awaited) as a reconcile pass; the debounced chokidar
    notify stays as a second safety net. The row disappears the moment the API
    confirms — no full-tree re-walk in the critical path.

MEASURED END TO END (real Sidebar, live server, real folders on disk):
  - node_modules, 3000 files: row gone 1197ms after confirming — 1064ms of that
    is the server physically deleting 3000 files, client overhead only 133ms,
    0 snapshot requests, and the UI updated while a deliberately slowed (2.5s)
    GET /tree refetch was still in flight.
  - vendor folder, 1000 files: 444ms total (delete API 412ms) — comfortably
    under 1 second.
  - Small file delete: still exactly 1 getFile (snapshot captured); Ctrl+Z
    restored the file on disk and in the tree.
  - Whole-project DELETE endpoint: 117ms. Root-button UI flow: 261ms.
  The residual time for a huge folder is the PHYSICAL rm on the server — the UI
  can never reflect a deletion before the server confirms it; everything the
  client added (snapshot loop + refetch wait) is gone.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
DELETE PROJECT BUTTON IS NOW CLEARLY VISIBLE IN THE EXPLORER
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  - components/Sidebar/FileTree.jsx  The locked ROOT row now renders ONE
    danger-styled hover button (lucide-react Trash2, class
    "tree-action is-delete-project", aria-label "Delete project <name>", title
    "Delete project “<name>” (removes every file)") INSTEAD of the per-row
    rename/delete pair (which would target a single child path anyway — the
    workspace root itself is never deletable). canDeleteProject / onDeleteProject
    are threaded through the row component and every nested row.
    It uses the SAME hover-icon pattern as regular files/folders (.tree-actions
    is opacity 0 and reveals on row hover/selection).
  - components/Sidebar/Sidebar.css  .tree-action.is-delete-project is coloured
    var(--danger) (never the neutral grey of per-row actions) with a
    danger-soft / danger-strong hover, so it clearly reads as "delete the whole
    project" rather than "delete this item".
  - Clicking it dispatches openDeleteProjectModal() — the SAME
    type-the-project-name ConfirmModal (confirmPhrase = project name; confirm
    button disabled until the exact name is typed, Enter also confirms). The
    right-click "Delete Project" menu entry is KEPT untouched — this is an
    additional, more discoverable entry point, not a replacement.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
VERIFICATION PERFORMED THIS ENTRY
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  - oxlint: 0 warnings, 0 errors (65 files). vite build: succeeds.
  - Live benchmark (real server + MongoDB + 3000-file node_modules): numbers
    above; planted folders, registry entries and MongoDB rows cleaned up.
  - jsdom + Vite-SSR harness rendering the REAL Sidebar/FileTree/ConfirmModal
    against the LIVE API: 28/28 checks — root row shows the Delete Project
    button (danger-coloured, hover-reveal CSS present, no per-row buttons left
    on the root), context menu still offers "Delete Project", 3000-file and
    1000-file folder deletes update the UI optimistically (0 getFile calls, row

================================================================================
AI Code Editor — Phase 7: AI Panel Layout, Send Fix, Password-Gated Key
               Reveal/Edit
================================================================================

STATUS: complete and verified. Three things changed: (1) the AI panel's bottom
        area is no longer cramped and its textarea auto-grows, (2) sending a
        task no longer fails silently, (3) saved API keys can be revealed and
        edited, but only after re-entering the account password.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
1. ROOT CAUSE OF THE "SEND BUTTON DOES NOT SEND" BUG
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
The send path itself was wired correctly: the composer's <form onSubmit> and
the textarea's Enter keydown both call handleSend(), which dispatches the
startAgentTask thunk over the agent Socket.IO channel ('agent:run'), and the
server streams 'agent:action' events back. Driving the real App (jsdom + Vite
SSR) against a live server showed both Enter and clicking the send button
start a run.

What actually LOOKED like a broken button was a silent gate on the active
workspace:

  - A freshly signed-in user has fileSystem.workspace = EMPTY_WORKSPACE
    ({ source:'none', projectId:null }) until a project is imported (or a file
    is created, which opens the scratch workspace).
  - The AI panel still renders its framework selector, key selector and the
    whole composer in that state, because the composer only requires a saved
    KEY — not a project. So key + framework both LOOK selected.
  - BUT the send button was disabled whenever !hasWorkspace, and pressing
    Enter fell into `if (!projectId) return` inside handleSend. Either way
    there was ZERO feedback: no error, no toast, no bubble. Clicking send
    simply did nothing.
  - A second, rarer silent path: any unexpected throw inside the
    startAgentTask thunk (before the ack handled it) produced no agent:done,
    so nothing reached the chat area either.

THE FIX
  - startAgentTask (features/aiSlice.js) now wraps its whole body in
try/catch: a missing project/workspace, a socket/connection problem, an
expired session or an unknown key id all resolve to
    "Failed to reach the AI agent: <reason>"
  pushed into the timeline via agentDone({ ok:false }), so it is ALWAYS
  visible in the chat area. The 20s no-response timeout message was reworded
  to fit that prefix.
  - The send button is no longer disabled by !hasWorkspace: it stays enabled
  whenever there is text, so clicking it (or Enter) with no project surfaces
  the visible error above instead of doing nothing.
  - handleSend() now rejects while a send is in flight (a ref guard, so a
  rapid double Enter cannot dispatch two runs), resets the draft, dispatches
  startAgentTask, and swallows the already-rendered rejection so the console
  stays clean.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
2. LAYOUT FIXES (client/src/components/AIPanel/*)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  - Every bottom row now has real breathing room: suggestion chips
    padding 12px 16px, controls row 12px 16px, composer 12px 16px 16px, with
    10–12px flex gaps (was 6–10px with tighter padding).
  - Suggestion chips wrap onto as many lines as needed
    (flex-wrap + gap:8px, white-space:normal, overflow-wrap:anywhere) and can
    never poke past the panel edges.
  - The controls row wraps instead of overflowing at the 280px minimum, and
    the key select can shrink (flex:0 1 auto; min-width:0).
  - The composer textarea AUTO-GROWS: an effect resets it to height:auto and
    sets the measured scrollHeight, clamped at 120px, past which it scrolls
    internally (was pinned to 52px by a .ai-input/.ai-textarea specificity
    clash — fixed with a combined .ai-input.ai-textarea rule).
  - The send button is a fixed 36×36, align-self:center, so it stays centered
    and never squashes as the textarea grows.

  VERIFIED in real headless Chrome (built CSS) at 280 / 340 / 420 / 600px:
    zero overflow on the panel, controls, composer and suggestions rows; the
    send button measured 36×36 with a 0px vertical-center delta; a 20-line
    draft grew the textarea to exactly 120px and was capped there.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
3. PASSWORD-GATED REVEAL + EDIT FOR SAVED API KEYS
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Backend (server/src/…)
  - POST /api/keys/:id/reveal  { password }
  - PUT  /api/keys/:id         { password, label?, key? }
  Both are JWT-protected (the /api/keys router already runs `protect`).
  apiKeyService.verifyAccountPassword(userId, password) loads the User and
  uses the model's own comparePassword() (bcrypt). A wrong or missing password
  throws 401 (code INVALID_PASSWORD / PASSWORD_REQUIRED) and NO key data is
  returned. Only then does revealKeyForUser() decrypt the AES-256-GCM value,
  or updateKeyForUser() re-encrypt a replacement value / save a new label.
  Ownership is enforced by filtering every query on owner — another user's
  key id is a 404 even with a correct password.

Client
  - services/api.js: agentKeysApi.reveal(keyId, password) and
    agentKeysApi.update(keyId, { password, label, key }).
  - features/aiSlice.js: revealApiKey and updateApiKey thunks (update also
    refreshes the masked list and toasts success/error).
  - components/Settings/KeyAccessModal.jsx (NEW): one password-gated dialog
    shared by both actions. Reveal mode = password only. Edit mode adds a
    Label field and an optional "Replace key value" field that are saved
    together with the password in one request.
  - components/Settings/SettingsModal.jsx: every saved key row gains an eye
    icon (Eye/EyeOff) and a pencil icon. Eye -> dialog -> on success the row
    shows the FULL key with a Copy button. Pencil -> the edit dialog.
  - Re-masking: a revealed key hides itself again after 30s
    (REVEAL_HIDE_MS), immediately after Copy, when the eye is toggled off, and
    whenever Settings is closed (the close handlers clear the state, so
    plaintext never survives leaving the view).

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
VERIFICATION PERFORMED THIS ENTRY
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  - oxlint: 0 warnings, 0 errors (72 files). vite build: succeeds.
  - node --check on every changed server file: passes.
  - Live API run (real server + MongoDB, 16/16 checks): reveal requires auth;
    wrong password 401 with NO key data; missing password 401; correct password
    returns the exact saved key; another user's reveal 404; edit wrong password
    401; label-only edit keeps the masked value; value replacement re-encrypts
    and reveal returns the new key; malformed replacement 400 KEY_INVALID; the
    list endpoint still never contains the full key.
  - Real-App jsdom harness (Vite SSR) against the live API, 25/25 checks:
    masked list has no plaintext; eye opens the password dialog; wrong password
    shows an inline error, keeps the dialog open and never reveals; correct
    password shows the full key + Copy; Copy writes to the clipboard and
    re-masks; pencil opens the edit dialog with label + replace fields; wrong
    password rejected; correct edit renames + replaces (masked value updates,
    reveal returns the replacement); eye-off hides; reopening Settings is
    masked again.
  - Full pre-existing AI agent harness re-run: 49/49 checks (send via Enter and
    via the send button both still start a live run end to end).
  - Temporary harnesses/scripts and their logs were deleted; git status shows
    only the intended source files (plus client/tmp-ui-harness.mjs, which only
    lost one unused variable to keep oxlint clean).

================================================================================
AI Code Editor — Phase 12: Inline Explorer Naming, Dynamic Groq Model
               Selection, Workspace-Free Agent
================================================================================

STATUS: complete and verified. Three fixes: (1) "New File"/"New Folder" now
        prompt for a name inline and create NOTHING until Enter, (2) the Groq
        404 is fixed at the root by resolving the model dynamically from the
        user's key instead of hardcoding it, (3) the agent no longer requires a
        pre-existing project or files — an empty scratch workspace is a normal
        starting point.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
1. NEW FILE / NEW FOLDER: INLINE NAME, NO AUTO-NAMING ("Scratch")
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
ROOT CAUSE
  The inline name row already existed, so the visible symptom ("New Folder
  creates a folder called Scratch") came from an EAGER side effect, not from a
  default name string. beginCreatingIn() called ensureWorkspaceReady() BEFORE
  showing the input, and with no imported project that bootstrapped the
  per-user scratch workspace — whose on-disk folder and root node are literally
  named "Scratch" (server ensureScratchWorkspace -> displayName 'Scratch').
  So one click on New Folder instantly materialised a "Scratch" folder that the
  user read as their new folder, even though the real input row was also shown.

FIX (client only)
  - components/Sidebar/Sidebar.jsx:
      * beginCreatingIn(parentId, type) is now SYNCHRONOUS: it only sets the
        `creating` row. It never touches the server and never opens a workspace.
      * ensureWorkspaceReady() (useCallback) moved to submitCreate(). It is
        called ONLY after the user typed a name and pressed Enter, and it
        RETURNS the project id (projectIdRef is not yet updated inside the same
        async handler). Nothing is created — not even the scratch folder —
        before that point.
      * submitCreate trims the name and returns on an empty one, so confirming
        an empty field (or Escape) leaves the workspace completely untouched.
  - components/Sidebar/FileTree.jsx: the root-level inline row now renders
    whenever the target parent has no row of its own yet
    (parentId === null || parentId === ROOT_ID, and no node with that id). This
    is what makes the inline field appear at the TOP level the very first time,
    when there is no workspace (and therefore no root row) yet.
  The toolbar buttons and BOTH context-menu entries ("New File"/"New Folder",
  including the root row) all funnel through this one path, so all of them
  behave identically.

ONE-TIME CLEANUP
  The stray, EMPTY "Scratch" folders left by the old eager behavior were removed
  from server/workspaces (the real projects in the same user container were left
  untouched): the stale u-<user>/Scratch roots, the empty legacy scratch-guest
  dir, and the test-only roots created while verifying. A scratch workspace is
  recreated on demand, so nothing is lost. The regular folder Delete (tree Trash
  button -> ConfirmModal) was verified to remove a folder from both the tree and
  the disk, so a stray folder can always be deleted by the user in one click.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
2. GROQ 404 "MODEL DOES NOT EXIST" — ROOT CAUSE + DYNAMIC MODEL SELECTION
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
ROOT CAUSE (confirmed)
  server/.env has no GROQ_MODEL, so env.groqModel fell back to the hardcoded
  'llama-3.3-70b-versatile'. Groq announced the retirement/deprecation of
  llama-3.3-70b-versatile (and qwen/qwen3-32b) in 2026; on a key that no longer
  serves it the Chat Completions call answers 404 "model does not exist". The
  endpoint and header were already correct — the model string was simply dead.
  (This is a config/time problem, not a formatting bug: the endpoint really is
  https://api.groq.com/openai/v1/chat/completions and the key really was sent as
  "Bearer <key>". Both are now also defensively .trim()'d so a stray space can
  never corrupt them.)

FIX — the model is resolved per run, never hardcoded (server)
  - config/env.js: groqModel now defaults to '' (an OPTIONAL preferred model,
    trimmed) and groqBaseUrl is .trim()'d, so a stray space in .env cannot break
    the URL.
  - services/agentService.js:
      * TOOL_CAPABLE_MODELS — an allowlist that mirrors Groq's "Tool Use"
        support table (verified against console.groq.com/docs/tool-use), ordered
        best-first: openai/gpt-oss-120b, openai/gpt-oss-20b, qwen/qwen3.8-27b,
        minimaxai/minimax-m2.7, llama-3.3-70b-versatile, llama-3.1-8b-instant,
        openai/gpt-oss-safeguard-20b, then older ids (kimi-k2, qwen3-32b,
        llama-4-maverick/scout) for keys that still have them.
      * listAvailableModels(apiKey): GET {groqBaseUrl}/models with
        "Authorization: Bearer <trimmed key>" and a 20s timeout; 401/403 ->
        "key rejected", clear error otherwise.
      * resolveGroqModel(apiKey): the first allowlisted id the key actually
        has; throws a clear, actionable message listing what IS available when
        none match. GROQ_MODEL, when set, is tried first.
      * runAgent resolves the model ONCE before the loop, emits it with the
        first 'thinking' event AND in the final 'complete'/'error' event, and
        returns it; callGroq(apiKey, messages, model) trims both the key and the
        model id before sending.
  - socket/agentSocket.js: agent:done now carries `model` too.

CLIENT — the panel shows the model in use
  - features/aiSlice.js: state.model (null initially); any agent event whose
    details.model is present updates it, as does the agent:done payload.
  - components/AIPanel/AIPanel.jsx + .css: a small, ellipsised .ai-model-label
    next to the Ready/Working badge showing the resolved model id.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
3. THE AGENT NO LONGER REQUIRES A PRE-EXISTING PROJECT OR FILES
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  - socket/agentSocket.js: the "!workspaceExists(projectId)" pre-check is GONE.
    An empty (or brand-new) workspace is a normal starting state; the agent's
    first tool calls create the scaffolding. A missing task is still rejected.
    The workspace id itself is still required (the client always provides the
    active one). For a scratch id, ensureScratchWorkspace() is called so the
    folder exists before the tools run.
  - features/aiSlice.js: startAgentTask no longer throws when there is no
    project. If the active workspace is empty/absent it opens (or reuses) the
    scratch workspace via openScratchWorkspace and runs against it.
  - components/AIPanel/AIPanel.jsx: the old "open or import a project first"
    guard/error and the "Open or create a project first…" placeholder are
    removed. The only remaining requirements are a signed-in user, a saved/
    selectable Groq key, a task, and (already enforced) a framework.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
VERIFICATION PERFORMED THIS ENTRY
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  - oxlint: 0 warnings, 0 errors (75 files). vite build: succeeds.
  - node --check on every changed server file: passes.
  - Socket/API test (real server + Mongo + scripted mock Groq, 17/17 checks):

      llama-3.1-8b-instant, so a hardcoded id would have failed); EVERY
      /chat/completions request used that id, the header was exactly
      "Bearer <key>" (trimmed), all 6 tools were sent, and the model came back
      to the client. config/env.js is asserted to be the exact
      https://api.groq.com/openai/v1 endpoint with no hardcoded model left.
  - Real-App jsdom harness (Vite SSR) against the live API, 51/51 checks — the
    full pre-existing agent flow plus two new ones: the panel shows
    "openai/gpt-oss-120b" after the run, i.e. the dynamically resolved model.
  - Inline-create harness (real App in jsdom, 21/21 checks):
      * toolbar New Folder shows a focused inline field and creates NOTHING —
        no workspace bootstrapped, no folder on disk;
      * Enter creates a folder with the typed name (on disk too), bootstrapping

      * Escape cancels with no row and no file;
