================================================================================
AI Code Editor — Phase 13: Automatic Key/Model Fallback on 429, Token-Lean
               Agent Prompts, Scratch -> Named Project Adoption
================================================================================

STATUS: complete and verified. Three fixes to the AI Agent system:
        (1) 429 rate limits are handled transparently by cycling the user's
            saved keys and then the tool-capable models — the task keeps going;
        (2) the prompt sent to Groq each turn is trimmed so a simple task no
            longer resends every old file body / tool result forever;
        (3) an empty "Scratch" workspace is renamed after the task into a real,
            tracked project (e.g. "todo-app") and the UI relabels immediately.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
1. AUTOMATIC KEY + MODEL FALLBACK ON 429
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
THE CHAIN, IN ORDER (server/src/services/agentService.js)
  When an agent run starts, agentSocket resolves ALL of the caller's saved keys
  in preference order (explicit keyId first, then newest-first) via the new
  apiKeyService.resolveKeysForUser(ownerId, keyId). runAgent receives that list
  as `apiKeys` and builds a MODEL PLAN with resolveModelPlan(keys):
      * every saved key is asked for its live GET /models list;
      * the union is filtered through the existing TOOL_CAPABLE_MODELS allowlist
        (preferredModelOrder, so GROQ_MODEL still wins if set) -> the ordered
        list of models this user can actually run.
      * A key that cannot list models is skipped; only when NO key can list does
        the run fail with that key's error.
  For every chat completion, requestCompletion() tries, in this exact order:
      1. the currently-active model with the currently-active key;
      2. every REMAINING saved key on that SAME model (key 2, key 3 …);
      3. only then the NEXT model in the allowlist, again across all saved keys
         (model x key matrix).
  So "gpt-oss-120b rate-limited" walks gpt-oss-120b/key1 -> gpt-oss-120b/key2
  -> gpt-oss-120b/key3 -> llama-3.3-70b/key1 -> … -> llama-3.1-8b/keyN.

WHAT COUNTS AS "TRY THE NEXT ONE"
  - 429  -> rate limit: advance to the next key/model (the whole point).
  - 404  -> that model is not served by that key: skip just that pair.
  - 401/403 -> that key is rejected: skip that key.
  Any other status (5xx, network) is a real failure and is surfaced as before.

RETRY-AFTER RESPECTED
  callGroq attaches error.retryAfterMs by reading the standard `Retry-After`
  header (seconds OR an HTTP date) and/or Groq's own text ("Please try again in
  15.79s", "…in 1m30s"). After EVERY key/model pair has been tried and all were
  rate-limited, if the shortest suggested wait is <= 15s (MAX_FALLBACK_WAIT_MS),
  the agent emits a "waiting Ns before one retry" note, sleeps exactly that
  long, and retries the active combination ONCE. Only if that also fails (or the
  wait is long) does the run end. The FINAL error is exactly:
      "All available API keys/models are currently rate-limited. Please wait a
       moment and try again, or add another API key in Settings."
  and carries code RATE_LIMITED.

VISIBLE IN THE LIVE FEED + MODEL INDICATOR
  Every switch emits an `agent:action` event of type 'fallback' with a plain
  note ("Rate limit hit on openai/gpt-oss-120b with primary — switching to
  secondary…", "Rate limited on openai/gpt-oss-120b across saved keys —
  switching to llama-3.1-8b-instant…", "Recovered — now using …"). Each event
  also carries details.model, so the AIPanel header's model label updates to the
  combination actually in use at every point. The panel gained a RefreshCw icon
  for 'fallback' rows (and FolderCog for the rename row below).
  agentSocket no longer picks a single key: it passes the whole decrypted list
  and still drops every reference in `finally`.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
2. TOKEN-USAGE REDUCTION PER TURN
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  The loop still keeps the FULL canonical history in `messages`, but it now
  sends a TRIMMED view every turn via trimMessages():
    - the most recent 8 messages (KEEP_RECENT_MESSAGES) are sent untouched — the
      model always sees the immediate context in full;
    - older `role:'tool'` results longer than 400 chars are replaced with
      "[Older tool result omitted to save tokens (N chars). Re-run the tool if
      you need it again.]" — so a big read_file no longer rides along for 20
      more turns;
    - older assistant tool_calls have their arguments slimmed: create_file /
      edit_file file bodies are DROPPED, leaving only { path } (plus command for
      run_command). This is the single largest win, because a create_file call
      embeds the entire file in its arguments.
      [SUPERSEDED — see the final entry "Agent Placeholder Bug Fix + HTML-First
       Workflow + Auto-Continue + Prompt Timer". Rewriting old tool-call
       arguments is GONE (it made the model copy "[written: N lines]" straight
       into later create_file calls). condenseHistory now replaces each old
       (assistant tool_call + tool result) pair with a short "[did: …]" note plus
       a running workspace-state note, and the real arguments are never
       mutated.]
  Nothing is mutated on the canonical history (re-running a tool is always
  possible), and tool_call/tool pairing stays intact (we only shrink strings).
  Also reduced the per-result caps: MAX_TOOL_RESULT_CHARS 12000 -> 8000 and
  MAX_READ_CHARS 30000 -> 24000. The system prompt still carries only the
  dynamic tree context; no static repetition was added.
  Net effect: a basic HTML/CSS/JS todo task stays lean because each turn only
  pays for the last few turns plus the current tool result, not the whole run.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
3. SCRATCH WORKSPACE -> NAMED, TRACKED PROJECT
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  On agent:run, before the first tool call, agentSocket calls
  maybeAdoptScratchProject({ projectId, task, userId, emit }):
    - Only for a scratch id (scratch-<userId>). A real imported project is never
      touched (its Project record + folder stay exactly as imported).
    - If the scratch folder is still the default 'Scratch', a project name is
      derived deterministically from the task WITHOUT an extra model call:
      deriveProjectName() lowercases, drops filler verbs/prepositions, and joins
      the first two meaningful words — "Create a todo app with localStorage" ->
      "todo-app", "Build a landing page with a contact form" -> "landing-page",
      empty -> "my-project".
    - The folder is renamed on disk by the new workspaceManager
      renameWorkspaceFolder(projectId, { displayName }), which reuses the SAME
      sanitizeFolderName + availableFolderName collision handling as a real
      import (name free -> use it, else "-2", "-3" …), and updates the
      .workspaces.json registry in place.
    - upsertProject() creates the MongoDB Project record (name, owner,
      diskPath, diskFolderName), transitioning the anonymous workspace into a
      real tracked project.
    - The projectId is deliberately UNCHANGED, so all paths/tools that are
      mid-run keep resolving; a 'project' agent:action event with
      { projectId, name } is streamed.
  CLIENT
    - useAgentEvents intercepts the 'project' event, dispatches the new
      fileSystem.applyAgentProject reducer (keeps tree + tabs, flips source to
      'cloud', renames the Explorer root node) and persists it via
      saveWorkspaceRef so a reload restores the project under its new name.
    - Because source changes 'scratch' -> 'cloud', useWorkspaceWatcher re-runs
      and re-subscribes on the RENAMED path, so live tree updates keep working.
    - Topbar chip now shows the project name (workspaceLabel returns
      workspace.name for a cloud workspace); the Explorer root label follows the
      renamed root node immediately, while the agent is still working.
  If the user is already in a real imported project, adoption is a no-op.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
VERIFICATION PERFORMED THIS ENTRY
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  - oxlint: 0 warnings, 0 errors (76 files). vite build: succeeds.
  - node --check on every changed server file: passes.
  - In-process unit test (tmp-agent-fallback-unit.mjs, 13/13 checks): the real
    runAgent loop with a stubbed fetch proved
      A) ALL combos 429 with a long Retry-After -> the exact final rate-limit
         message + code RATE_LIMITED, an error action emitted, >=3 fallback
         notes, and NO pointless wait;
      B) ALL combos 429 with Retry-After 0.3s -> waits ~0.3s, retries once and
         SUCCEEDS ("Recovered after the wait"), model reported in the result;
      C) deriveProjectName cases (todo-app / landing-page / my-project).
  - Full Socket.IO integration test (client/tmp-agent-fallback-test.mjs, 24/24
    checks) against the live server + Mongo + a rate-limiting mock Groq:
      * a mid-task 429 completed the run: the feed showed exactly the expected
        notes (gpt-oss-120b/primary -> secondary -> llama-3.1-8b-instant);
      * the final model indicator was llama-3.1-8b-instant;
      * the empty scratch folder was renamed to "todo-app" on disk, the old
        "Scratch" folder was gone, the agent's files landed in the renamed
        folder, and GET /api/projects showed the new Project record with
        diskFolderName "todo-app";
      * a 'project' event with name "todo-app" streamed to the client;
      * prompt trimming verified from the recorded request bodies: the big
        read_file result became the "[Older tool result omitted" placeholder in
        later turns, the big.js body was sent in FULL while recent and slimmed
        to {"path":"big.js"} once old, and every tool-call argument in the last
        prompt was < 1200 chars.
        [SUPERSEDED — the "slimmed to {\"path\":\"big.js\"}" behaviour was the
         placeholder bug's ancestor: old tool-call arguments are no longer
         rewritten at all. See the final entry.]
  - Regression: the pre-existing dynamic-model test (client/tmp-agent-model-
    test.mjs, 17/17) still passes — one GET /models for a single key, every

  - Temporary harnesses/scripts (tmp-agent-fallback-unit.mjs,

    verify, matching the existing temporary-test convention.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
NOTES FOR THE NEXT DEVELOPER / AI TOOL
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  - The agent still has NO built-in Groq key: every key comes from the user's
    saved, encrypted keys and is dropped at the end of the run.
  - Fallback is intentionally per chat completion; a run that hits the limit
    later simply falls back again on the next turn.
  - The final error string and its RATE_LIMITED code are user-facing contract
    text — keep them in sync with whatever the panel shows.
  - trimMessages only changes what is SENT, never the stored history. If you add
    a tool whose result is large AND needed long-term, prefer writing it to the
    workspace and re-reading on demand over enlarging KEEP_RECENT_MESSAGES.
    [SUPERSEDED — the function is now condenseHistory(); it still only changes
     what is SENT, and it no longer rewrites tool-call arguments (see the final
     entry).]
  - Scratch adoption keys off the registry folder name 'Scratch'; if you change
    ensureScratchWorkspace's displayName, update SCRATCH_FOLDER_NAME in
    socket/agentSocket.js to match.


================================================================================
AI Code Editor — Phase 8: Live Server Preview (static preview + auto reload)
================================================================================

STATUS: complete and verified. A project can now be viewed like VS Code's Live
        Server: open an .html file in a real browser tab and it reloads itself
        whenever the workspace changes on disk — including when you save a file
        from inside CodeMatrix.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
PART 1 — THE STATIC PREVIEW ROUTE (/preview/:projectId/*)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Mounted at the app level in src/app.js (NOT under /api), so the URL is a clean
static path a browser can load directly:

    GET /preview/:projectId/index.html?token=<jwt>
    GET /preview/:projectId/styles/site.css?token=<jwt>
    GET /preview/:projectId/                 -> index.html

NEW SERVER FILES
  src/routes/preview.routes.js
    Two patterns — '/:projectId' (bare root) and '/:projectId/*' (everything
    else, captured in req.params[0]) — both behind the SAME guard chain:
      protectPreview -> validateProjectId -> authorizeProject -> ensureWorkspace
    The last three are the exact middlewares the /api/workspace routes use, so
    ownership and 'folder exists' semantics are identical.

  src/middlewares/protectPreview.js
    Reuses the existing JWT (jsonwebtoken + env.jwtSecret). Because a preview is
    opened as a PLAIN browser tab it cannot send the Authorization header, so
    the token is read from the `token` query parameter (and the Authorization
    header too, for programmatic callers). A missing/expired/forged token -> 401;
    a valid token sets req.user = { id } exactly like authMiddleware.protect.

  src/controllers/preview.controller.js
    - contentTypeFor(path): small built-in MIME map (html/htm, css, js/mjs/cjs/
      jsx, json/map, txt, xml, svg, png/jpg/jpeg/gif/webp/avif/bmp/ico, woff/
      woff2/ttf/otf/eot, mp3/wav/ogg, mp4/webm, pdf, wasm, zip); anything else is
      application/octet-stream. No new dependency.
    - servePreviewFile: resolves the request path with resolveWorkspaceFile()
      (the same traversal guard as every other workspace path builder), turns a
      directory (or empty path) into its index.html, 404s a missing file, and
      always sets Cache-Control: no-store + X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff so
      a reload shows the current bytes, never a cached copy.
    - HTML injection: for .html/.htm ONLY, the response body gets a small script
      inserted immediately before the closing </body> tag via a string replace
      (the file on disk is NEVER modified). If there is no closing </body>
      (malformed/fragment HTML) the script is appended at the end instead.
    - Binary safety: non-HTML files are read and sent as raw bytes (Buffer), so
      images/fonts survive byte-for-byte.

  src/utils/workspaceManager.js
    + resolveWorkspaceFile(projectId, relativePath) -> { root, absolute, relative }
      (reuses the private resolveInsideWorkspace guard; lets the preview read raw
      bytes instead of the UTF-8 decoded readFileFromWorkspace).

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
THE INJECTED LIVE-RELOAD SCRIPT
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Inside every served .html file, just before </body>:

    <script>
    (function () {
      var PROJECT_ID = "<projectId>";
      function connect() {
        var socket = window.io({ transports: ['websocket', 'polling'] });
        socket.on('connect', function () {
          socket.emit('preview:watch', { projectId: PROJECT_ID });
        });
        socket.on('preview:reload', function (p) {
          if (!p || p.projectId === PROJECT_ID) window.location.reload();
        });
      }
      var s = document.createElement('script');
      s.src = '/socket.io/socket.io.js';   // served by the SAME Socket.IO server
      s.async = true; s.onload = connect;
      document.body.appendChild(s);
    })();
    </script>

The Socket.IO client bundle is loaded from /socket.io/socket.io.js, which the
Socket.IO server already serves on its own origin. The preview page is served
by that same server, so the connection is same-origin — no CORS, no second URL.
The projectId is validated to [A-Za-z0-9_-] before it reaches here and is
JSON-encoded into the script.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
PART 2 — LIVE RELOAD THROUGH THE EXISTING CHOKIDAR + SOCKET SYSTEM
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
No new watcher was added. The one chokidar watcher per active project
(src/utils/fileWatcher.js, already used for Explorer auto-refresh) now drives
BOTH signals:

  - src/utils/fileWatcher.js
      startWatchingProject(projectId, notify, previewNotify) gained a second,
      optional notifier. Each relevant fs event schedules:
        scheduleNotify()          -> debounced 750ms -> notify()
        schedulePreviewReload()   -> debounced 350ms -> previewNotify()
      The preview timer is deliberately NOT gated by suppressWatcher(): a save
      made through our own API suppresses the redundant full-tree walk, but that
      save is EXACTLY the change a preview must reflect, so it still reloads.
      A burst of writes (8 saves in a row) collapses into ONE reload. Both timers
      are cleared in stopWatchingProject / closeAllWatchers.

  - src/socket/workspaceSocket.js
      One helper builds the watcher callbacks:
        treeChanged -> io.to('workspace:<id>').emit('workspace:treeChanged')
        preview     -> io.to('workspace:<id>').emit('preview:reload')
      In addition to workspace:watch, a new client event preview:watch joins the
      SAME room and takes the SAME reference-counted watcher reference. That
      means a preview tab left open with no editor attached keeps the watcher —
      and therefore live reload — alive on its own. disconnect/workspace:unwatch
      release references exactly as before.

  FLOW: save style.css -> server writes the file -> chokidar 'change' -> the
  debounced preview timer fires -> preview:reload to the project room -> the
  injected script calls location.reload() -> the tab shows the new CSS, with no
  manual refresh.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
PART 3 — THE TWO FRONTEND TRIGGERS (both open a NEW browser tab)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
NEW CLIENT FILE  src/utils/preview.js
  isHtmlPath(), collectHtmlFiles(tree), findRootIndexHtml(tree),
  buildPreviewUrl(projectId, path) — builds
      <API_BASE_URL>/preview/<projectId>/<path>?token=<current jwt>
  (reads the token from services/api.getAuthToken() at click time, so it is
  always the live token), and openPreviewTab() which calls window.open(url,
  '_blank'). API_BASE_URL is the API/Socket.IO origin — the tab must point there,
  not at the Vite dev origin.

1. EXPLORER RIGHT-CLICK — "Open with Live Server"
   src/components/Sidebar/FileTree.jsx adds the menu item (lucide Play icon)
   ONLY for non-folder nodes whose path ends in .html/.htm. The Sidebar wires
   the onPreview handler (stable-ref pattern, like paste/requestCreate) and it
   calls openPreviewTab(projectId, node.path).

2. TOPBAR PLAY BUTTON — src/components/Topbar/LivePreviewButton.jsx
   A lucide Play icon-btn placed just left of the Terminal toggle, with the
   tooltip "Open live preview". It is enabled only while the active workspace
   hasActiveWorkspace() AND the tree contains at least one .html file
   (collectHtmlFiles). Click resolution order:
     1. the file currently open in the editor, when it is .html;
     2. index.html at the workspace root;
     3. otherwise a small dropdown listing every .html file (a lone .html file
        is opened directly).
   Every path goes through useAuthGuard (opens Sign In when signed out) and
   opens a real tab via window.open.

      * HTML 200 + text/html + Cache-Control: no-store; the reload script is

NOTES FOR THE NEXT DEVELOPER / AI TOOL
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  - The preview server lives on the API origin and is intentionally NOT under
    /api. The JWT always travels in ?token= (a plain tab cannot send headers);
    protectPreview also accepts the Authorization header.
  - HTML injection is a response-time string replace; the file on disk is never
    touched, so Export/Import and the editor buffer are unaffected.
  - The preview reload timer must stay OUTSIDE suppressWatcher — that is what
    makes save-from-CodeMatrix reload the preview. Only the tree refresh is
    suppressed for our own API mutations.
  - Absolute asset paths in a previewed page (/style.css) resolve against the
    server root, not the project. Relative paths and the usual ./ / ../ work.
  - The watcher already skips descending into node_modules/.git/dist/build/out/
    .next/.cache, so a change in those does not reload a preview.

AI Code Editor — Phase 9: Default Model = llama-3.1-8b-instant + Live Rate-Limit Usage
================================================================================

STATUS: complete. The agent's primary model is now llama-3.1-8b-instant, and the
        Groq rate-limit numbers returned in every response header are captured,
        stored, streamed live, and drawn as progress bars in Settings and the AI
        panel.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
PART 1 — THE DEFAULT AGENT MODEL IS NOW llama-3.1-8b-instant
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
server/src/services/agentService.js
  - TOOL_CAPABLE_MODELS now lists 'llama-3.1-8b-instant' FIRST, so it is the
    model every new task resolves to. Reason: Groq's free tier grants it a far
    larger daily request budget than the 70b/120b models (~14,400/day vs
    ~1,000/day), which is what makes all-day agent sessions sustainable.
  - The larger / more capable models stay in the SAME list right below it
    (openai/gpt-oss-120b, openai/gpt-oss-20b, qwen/qwen3.8-27b, minimax-m2.7,
    llama-3.3-70b-versatile, gpt-oss-safeguard-20b, and the older ids), so the
    existing fallback chain is UNCHANGED: a 429 on 8b-instant falls through to
    the next model (and the user's next saved key) exactly as before.
  - Selection stays dynamic: at the start of each run the agent asks GET /models
    which ids THIS key can use and picks the first allowlisted one. GROQ_MODEL
    still overrides the front of the list when set (config/env.js comment updated).

server/src/services/agentFrameworks.js
  - SHARED_RULES rewritten to suit a smaller model: an explicit NUMBERED
    workflow (1 UNDERSTAND → 2 INSPECT with list_directory/read_file → 3 PLAN the
    smallest change → 4 ACT with the tools → 5 VERIFY via read-back/build →
    6 FINISH with a tool-call-free summary), a clear relative-path rule, and
    direct RULES (only the provided tools, non-interactive scaffolding, never a
    long-running dev server, treat tool errors as facts). Less ambiguity, more
    step-by-step than the previous prose paragraphs.

CLIENT — the AI panel model indicator
  - NEW client/src/utils/agentDefaults.js exports DEFAULT_AGENT_MODEL =
    'llama-3.1-8b-instant' (mirrors TOOL_CAPABLE_MODELS[0]).
  - AIPanel shows `model ?? DEFAULT_AGENT_MODEL`, so before the first run the
    header reads llama-3.1-8b-instant; once a run starts the real resolved id
    (possibly a fallback) streams in and replaces it.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
PART 2 — CAPTURING GROQ RATE-LIMIT HEADERS
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Every Groq HTTP response carries: x-ratelimit-limit-requests,
-remaining-requests, -limit-tokens, -remaining-tokens, -reset-requests and
-reset-tokens. They are now captured on EVERY call.

NEW server/src/services/usageService.js
  - recordUsageFromHeaders({ keyId, userId, label, headers }) parses those six
    headers into one record:
      { keyId, userId, label, remainingRequests, limitRequests,
        remainingTokens, limitTokens, resetRequestsAt, resetTokensAt, updatedAt }
    Reset windows arrive as durations ("1m2.4s", "350ms", "30" seconds, "1h") and
    are parsed to ABSOLUTE ISO timestamps.
  - Storage is a two-tier cache: an always-present in-memory Map (authoritative
    while the process runs, and works with no database) plus a best-effort
    MongoDB upsert so the numbers survive a restart. getUsageForKeys() reads the
    Map and hydrates anything missing from the DB when one is connected.
  - A key that has never made a request has NO record, which is exactly what
    drives the neutral "Not used yet" state in the UI.

NEW server/src/models/ApiKeyUsage.model.js
  - keyId (unique), userId, label, the four limit/remaining numbers, the two
    reset timestamps and updatedAt. Durable mirror only — never stores the key.

server/src/services/agentService.js
  - listAvailableModels() and callGroq() accept an onResponse callback invoked as
    soon as each HTTP response arrives — BEFORE the status check, so the headers
    of a 429 (budget exhausted) are captured too.
  - resolveModelPlan() records the /models call's headers per key, so real
    numbers can appear before the first chat completion.
  - runAgent() gained emitUsage and userId; every key/model combination (and the
    post-wait retry) reports its captured record through emitUsage.

server/src/socket/agentSocket.js
  - Forwards each record as a new 'agent:usage' socket event, emitted alongside
    the 'agent:action' feed, so the UI updates live with no polling.

server/src/controllers/apiKey.controller.js + routes/apiKey.routes.js
  - NEW GET /api/keys/usage (JWT-protected). Returns, per saved key, the masked
    id/label plus `usage` (or null). It NEVER calls Groq itself — it returns the
    most recently captured values, so it can be slightly stale until the next
    real agent request refreshes it.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
PART 2 — DISPLAYING THE USAGE
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
NEW client/src/utils/rateLimit.js
  - usageStats(usage, 'requests' | 'tokens') -> { limit, remaining, used,
    usedPercent, remainingPercent, resetAt }. Requests = daily budget; tokens =
    per-minute budget.
  - usageTone(usedPercent): <70% 'ok', 70-90% 'warn', 90%+ 'danger'.
  - resetHint(resetAt) -> "resets in 3m".

NEW client/src/components/Common/UsageBar.jsx + .css
  - A thin progress bar shared by BOTH views (so the numbers can never drift).
    The fill uses the theme accent until 70% used, then orange (--warning), then
    red (--danger). Full mode shows "Requests today  X / Y used"; compact mode is
    a bare 3px bar.

client/src/features/aiSlice.js
  - New state usageByKeyId (map) + usageStatus. New thunk fetchKeyUsage()
    (GET /api/keys/usage) and reducer usageUpdated(record) for the live socket
    snapshot.

client/src/hooks/useAgentEvents.js
  - Listens for 'agent:usage' and dispatches usageUpdated live, AND refetches
    GET /api/keys/usage once an 'agent:done' arrives (reconcile after the run).

client/src/components/Settings/SettingsModal.jsx
  - Each saved key row now shows TWO thin bars under its label: "Requests today"
    and "Tokens this minute" (X / Y used), coloured by usageTone. A key with no
    captured usage shows the neutral "Not used yet". Usage is (re)fetched every
    time Settings opens.

client/src/components/AIPanel/AIPanel.jsx
  - The model indicator became a full-width strip just under the header (it used
    to be a cramped inline label) reading e.g.
    "llama-3.1-8b-instant · 68% daily requests remaining", followed by a thin bar
    with the same tone colour. The active key is the explicit selection, else the
    newest key (which is the server's default). Before any usage is captured it
    reads "not used yet". The strip's tooltip carries the exact X / Y and reset
    window. The strip keeps the .ai-model-label class, so existing selectors keep
    working.

client/src/App.jsx dispatches fetchKeyUsage() on sign-in alongside fetchApiKeys().

================================================================================
AI Agent Fix Pass: Project Naming + File Linking + Action Feed + Usage Bar
================================================================================

STATUS: complete and verified. Four reported issues fixed, plus two more bugs
found while verifying them (both were breaking the live preview). Acceptance:
37/37 server-flow checks, 23/23 headless-Chrome UI checks, oxlint clean, build
ok. The same tests run against the PREVIOUS code score 14/34 and 16/24, i.e.
they reproduce the reported bugs before the fix.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
1. ROOT CAUSE — THE NESTED "todo-app" FOLDER (issues #1 and #4)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
THE RENAME WAS NEVER A COPY. renameWorkspaceFolder() has always been a true
top-level fs.renameSync of the folder itself (workspaceManager.js), so contents
keep their relative positions. The real defect was TIMING + PATH, three parts:

  (a) THE CONTAINER CAME FIRST. POST /api/workspace/scratch ignored the task and
      always created/reused a folder literally named "Scratch", so every fresh
      agent task started its life inside a folder that was not the project.
  (b) THE RENAME COULD ONLY HAPPEN AFTER THE MODEL SPOKE. agentSocket renamed
      the folder when the model's first message contained a PROJECT_NAME line
      (and otherwise in `finally`, i.e. AFTER every tool call). A model's first
      message is very often tool_calls with content: null — extractProjectName()
      then returned null and the rename ran at the very end of the run.
  (c) THE MODEL NESTED ITS OWN NAME. Nothing told the model that the workspace
      root already IS the project folder, so it wrote paths prefixed with the
      name it had just chosen ("todo-app/index.html").

  Net effect, reproduced on the ORIGINAL code (tmp-fresh-task-test: 14/34):
      Scratch/task-tracker/{index.html,style.css,script.js}
  …and, when the name only arrived at the end, the rename produced the reported
  double:  task-tracker/task-tracker/*  — a project nested inside a copy of
  itself, with GET /preview/.../index.html answering 404 because the Explorer and
  the preview both expect the files at the project root.

THE FIX — the name is settled BEFORE the folder exists and BEFORE the first
file-write, and paths can never re-create the project's own name:

  server/src/controllers/workspace.controller.js
    POST /api/workspace/scratch now takes an optional { task }. With a task:
      * a FRESH workspace is created DIRECTLY under its final name (the
        backend-derived slug, chooseScratchProjectName/deriveProjectName) — no
        generic "Scratch" container is ever created;
      * an anonymous Scratch being reused for a task is renamed IN PLACE (still
        a true folder rename) before anything is written;
      * the response carries that real name with isScratch: false, because a
        workspace named after a task IS a real project from that moment.
      Without a task (Explorer "New File" with no project) the classic
      anonymous Scratch behaves exactly as before — verified 21/21 by the
      legacy tmp-create-test.

  client/src/services/api.js + features/workspaceThunks.js + features/aiSlice.js
    openScratchWorkspace({ task }) forwards the task; when the server answers
    with isScratch:false the thunk dispatches applyAgentProject, so the Explorer
    root label, the topbar chip and workspace.source ('cloud' -> Export/Sync)
    flip at OPEN time instead of waiting for a mid-run adoption.

  server/src/socket/agentSocket.js
    adopt(null) now runs BEFORE runAgent builds its prompt: any still-anonymous
    folder is renamed to the task slug before the first tool call. The model's
    PROJECT_NAME line can still rename it again (to a better name) on its first
    message — also before any tool executes — and `finally` remains the safety
    net. This is what made the legacy fallback test's rename assertions flip
    from FAIL to PASS (16/24 -> 21/24 with only its pre-existing, unrelated
    fallback-note expectations failing).

  server/src/services/scratchProjectService.js
    adoptScratchProject() is now idempotent and precise: it renames only when
    the folder is still anonymous OR the model proposed a DIFFERENT name, only
    upserts the Project record when it is out of date, and only emits the
    'project' feed row when the workspace actually transitioned — so repeated
    calls (pre-run + first message + finally) can no longer double-emit.

  server/src/services/agentService.js — THE SELF-NESTING GUARD
    normalizeToolArgs() runs on every file tool's path (and on the path shown in
    the feed): when the FIRST segment equals the workspace folder's own name AND
    no entry of that name exists at the root — i.e. the call would CREATE the
    project's own name as a nested folder — the segment is dropped
    ("task-tracker/index.html" -> "index.html"). An existing entry with that
    name is left completely alone, so a task that genuinely asks for such a
    folder still works. Normalisation is idempotent; run_command is untouched.

  server/src/services/agentFrameworks.js
    SHARED_RULES now states it outright: the workspace folder is already named
    after the project, PROJECT_NAME only NAMES it (never mkdir it), and files go
    at the ROOT (index.html, not "todo-app/index.html").

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
2. FILE LINKING IN GENERATED HTML/CSS/JS (issue #2) — TWO BUGS, BOTH FIXED
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  (i) THE NESTING above: with files at Scratch/<name>/* the browser could not
      resolve anything relative to the root. Fixed by issue #1's fix.
 (ii) FOUND WHILE VERIFYING IN A REAL BROWSER: even with flat siblings, the
      preview's stylesheet/script requests were answered 401. A browser resolves
      href="style.css" inside /preview/<id>/index.html?token=… to
      /preview/<id>/style.css — THE QUERY STRING IS DROPPED — and protectPreview
      only accepted ?token= / Authorization. Every generated site therefore
      rendered unstyled and with dead JS even though the files existed.
      server/src/middlewares/protectPreview.js now mirrors a presented
      query/header token into a per-project, path-scoped cookie
      (cm_preview_<projectId>; Path=/preview/<projectId>; HttpOnly; SameSite=Lax)
      and accepts it on later sub-resource requests. No HTML rewriting, and it
      also covers CSS url(), fonts and fetch() inside the page.

CONFIRMED WORKING (headless Chrome, real preview URL):
  - GET /preview/<id>/index.html -> 200 text/html;
  - <link rel="stylesheet" href="style.css"> -> applied (body rgb(15,23,42),
    h1 rgb(167,139,250) — exactly the generated style.css values);
  - <script src="script.js"> -> executed (typeof window.saveTodo ===
    'function') and functional (it wrote localStorage);
  - a bare asset request with no token and no cookie is refused 401, and the
    cookie alone authenticates it 200 text/css — so the cookie is what makes
    relative linking work.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
3. ROOT CAUSE — BROKEN ACTION FEED ("stack of flat gray lines")
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
ROOT CAUSE: a CSS flexbox shrink, not the data and not a stuck loader. There is
no skeleton state anywhere in the client; every event rendered — at 8 pixels
high.

  .ai-messages is `display:flex; flex-direction:column; overflow-y:auto`.
  Its children kept the default `flex-shrink: 1`, and `.ai-action` has
  `overflow: hidden`, which removes a flex item's content-based minimum height
  (min-height resolves to 0 for scroll containers). So as soon as the feed was
  taller than the panel the browser SHRANK the action cards instead of
  scrolling: the chat bubbles (overflow: visible -> min-height: auto) survived
  at full height while every action card collapsed to a ~8px sliver, showing
  only its 1px top/bottom borders — the "thin repeated horizontal line elements"
  in the screenshot. The container's scrollHeight equalled its clientHeight
  because the content had been squashed to fit, so the feed never scrolled.

  MEASURED in real Chrome against the built stylesheet:
    before: 10 action cards x 8px, feed 402px viewport = 402px content (squashed)
    after:  10 action cards x 30px, feed 384px viewport, 622px content (scrolls)

FIX (client/src/components/AIPanel/AIPanel.css): one rule,
`.ai-messages > * { flex: 0 0 auto; }` — rows keep their natural height and the
container scrolls. Every action type goes through the same ActionRow, so this
covers all of them; icons, descriptions, exit-code badges and expanded details
all render at full size again.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
4. VISUAL RATE-LIMIT PROGRESS BAR IN THE AI PANEL (issue #4)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
ROOT CAUSE OF "only compact text": the panel's private .ai-usage-mini span WAS
in the DOM but drew a 54x3px track whose FILL was 0px wide at 100% remaining
(usedPercent = 0), so at a healthy budget the bar was visually empty and only
the text read as content.

FIX (client/src/components/AIPanel/AIPanel.jsx + .css): the strip now renders
the SHARED Common/UsageBar component (the same one Settings uses — one set of
numbers and tone thresholds can never drift) in compact mode, on its own row
under the model indicator:
  - rail 100% wide, 5px tall, drawn on --bg-input with a --border ring so an
    empty bar at 0% used is still unmistakably a progress bar;
  - fill = today's request budget already USED, colour shifting accent -> orange
    (--warning) at 70% -> red (--danger) at 90%, i.e. it depletes into red;
  - the existing "…% daily requests remaining" text stays next to the model
    name, and the tooltip still carries X / Y + the reset window.

LIVE UPDATES: the bar re-renders from ai.usageByKeyId, which the server streams
on EVERY Groq response ('agent:usage' socket event, plus a reconcile fetch on
'agent:done'). Verified in the browser over a real run:
    fill 0px / "not used yet"  ->  167.5px (50%) / "50% daily requests remaining".

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
5. TWO MORE BUGS FOUND AND FIXED WHILE VERIFYING
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  - THE FILE WATCHER WENT STALE ON RENAME (fileWatcher.js + both rename call
    sites). The client subscribes to the workspace as soon as it opens, so the
    watcher was attached to the OLD folder name; when the agent renamed the
    folder mid-run, chokidar kept watching a path that no longer existed and
    every later write was invisible — no `workspace:treeChanged`, so the
    Explorer never showed the generated files (and the preview stopped
    live-reloading). NEW restartWatching(projectId) tears the watcher down and
    re-points it at the current path, preserving refs + both notifiers; it is
    called by adoptScratchProject() and openScratchWorkspaceHandler() whenever a
    rename actually happened. After the fix the Explorer lists the generated
    files seconds after the run.
  - ADOPTED WORKSPACES WERE REUSED AS THE NEXT SCRATCH (workspace.controller).
    Because a task-named workspace kept isScratch: true, findScratchProjectForOwner
    returned it for the NEXT anonymous open and the second task wrote into the
    first task's project. Task-named workspaces are now created/updated with
    isScratch: false, so a fresh task always gets a fresh workspace.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
VERIFICATION PERFORMED THIS ENTRY
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  - oxlint: 0 warnings, 0 errors (86 files). vite build: succeeds.
    node --check on every changed server file: passes.
  - SAME TESTS AGAINST THE OLD CODE (git stash, then pop): the flow test scores
    14/34 — Scratch container created, model paths nested task-tracker/*,

    rename check failing (rename raced agent:done). Both pass after the fix.
  - client/tmp-fresh-task-test.mjs — 37/37. Scripted mock Groq

      A) model NAMES its project (PROJECT_NAME: task-tracker) and writes

The tests registered throwaway accounts in the dev MongoDB (emails
probe*/fresh-*/ui-e2e-*@example.com) and created their workspace folders.
server/tmp-cleanup-session.mjs deletes exactly those users, their projects,
folders, registry entries and empty containers:

NOTES FOR THE NEXT DEVELOPER / AI TOOL
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  - A fresh agent task must be named BEFORE the first write. If you add another
    entry point that opens a workspace for the agent, pass the task through
    POST /api/workspace/scratch { task } — never let the agent start in a
    folder that is not the project.
  - normalizeToolArgs() is the only guard against the model re-creating its own
    project folder. If you add a tool that takes a path, run it through there.
  - Never give .ai-messages children a shrinking flex basis again: the feed is a
    scrolling flex column and `.ai-action`'s overflow:hidden makes its minimum
    height 0 — that combination is what produced the flat-gray-line bug.
  - The preview credential is a PATH-SCOPED COOKIE (cm_preview_<projectId>),
    not a rewritten URL — do not strip it, and do not "simplify" protectPreview
    back to query-only or every generated site loses its CSS again.
  - restartWatching() must be called after ANY rename of a workspace folder
    while a watcher may be attached (renameWorkspaceFolder itself cannot do it:
    fileWatcher imports workspaceManager, so a static import would be a cycle).
  - The AI panel's bar is Common/UsageBar in compact mode — extend THAT rather
    than adding another private bar; Settings and the panel share its numbers.


================================================================================
AI Code Editor — Phase 8: Long Multi-File Tasks Complete Reliably
================================================================================

STATUS: complete. Long (5+ file) agent tasks no longer stop halfway on a
        malformed tool call, a rate limit, or an early "done" — the agent
        recovers, keeps a plan, verifies it and can be resumed.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
1. THE 400 "Failed to parse tool call arguments as JSON" BUG
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
ROOT CAUSE: when a model tried to emit a whole large file in ONE tool call, the
completion hit its (default) token ceiling and was cut off mid-JSON. Groq then
rejected the request with HTTP 400 (code tool_use_failed) BEFORE returning a
message, so the agent loop saw a non-retryable error and aborted the whole task.

FIX (server/src/services/agentService.js):
  - callGroq now sends an EXPLICIT max_completion_tokens (8192) so a long file
    write is not truncated mid-JSON in the first place.
  - A 400 whose message mentions tool-call parsing (tool_use_failed /
    failed_generation / "failed to parse tool call arguments") is classified by
    isToolCallParseError() as RECOVERABLE, not fatal.
  - On such an error the loop retries the SAME turn up to MAX_PARSE_RETRIES (2)
    times with the temperature LOWERED (0.2 -> 0.1 -> 0.0) and appends the
    corrective note: "Your last tool call had invalid JSON … create_file for the
    first part and append_to_file for the rest, keep each call under about 120
    lines, and properly escape quotes and newlines."
  - If it still fails, escalateAfterParseFailure() moves to the NEXT model in
    the fallback chain (and then the next key) with the SAME message history and
    keeps the task going instead of ending it.
  - Both recoveries stream to the live feed as a soft note ("Tool call was
    malformed, retrying with smaller chunks…") and a normal fallback row — no
    red fatal error.

CHUNKED WRITING (part 2): a NEW tool append_to_file(path, content) appends to an
EXISTING file (creating it when missing), backed by appendFileToWorkspace() in
utils/workspaceManager.js. The system prompt now REQUIRES files longer than about
120 lines to be written as create_file (part 1) + append_to_file (parts 2..n).
The streamed feed labels each append "Writing index.html (part 2)" (FilePlus2
icon). A whole large HTML/CSS/JS file must never go into one call.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
2. PLAN + CHECKLIST + VERIFY (the agent FINISHES the task)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  - The system prompt makes the agent's FIRST message start with a
    "PLAN: file1, file2, …" line. extractPlanFiles() parses it and the plan is
    stored in the per-project backend session (agentSessionService).
  - Before accepting a final, tool-call-free "done" message, runAgent checks the
    PLAN against the real filesystem (existsInWorkspace). If any planned file is
    missing it sends the model "These planned files are still missing: […].
    Continue and create them." and continues the loop (up to 3 reminders).
  - The per-run tool-call cap was raised from 25 to 60 (MAX_TOOL_CALLS) so a
    multi-page site can finish; a safety cap still exists.
  - Vanilla HTML/CSS/JS prompt rules now require multi-page sites to be plain
    linked .html files sharing one style.css/script.js, Tailwind + icon
    libraries via CDN script tags (no build step), a plain-CSS replacement for
    any build-only library (e.g. Framer Motion) reported in the summary, and
    correct relative links between pages.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
3. CONTINUE / RESUME (part 4)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  - NEW server/src/services/agentSessionService.js keeps each project's message
    history (trimmed), its plan and framework in memory.
  - A run that ends in an error or hits the cap persists its session.
  - The AI panel shows a "Continue task" button when the last run ended that way;
    typing "continue" does the same. It re-runs with the saved history plus
    "Continue the previous task. First call list_directory … then finish the
    remaining files from the plan." The socket rejects a resume with no session
    (NO_SESSION).

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
4. RATE LIMITS: WAIT, DON'T ABANDON (part 5)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  - On 429 the Retry-After / "try again in Xs" value is read. When every key and
    model is rate-limited and the wait is <= 60s (MAX_FALLBACK_WAIT_MS), the
    agent waits it out with a LIVE 1-second countdown note ("Waiting 12s for
    rate limit to reset…") and retries the SAME request automatically. Only a
    longer wait, or exhausted retries, falls through to failure.
  - After each response the remaining-token headers are inspected; when less than
    ~10% of the per-minute token budget is left, the next request is paced a few
    seconds (a note is streamed) so large file writes do not burn out the budget.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
5. AI-BASED PROJECT NAMING (part 6, fixes "aapko-ek")
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  - The "first words of the prompt" fallback is GONE. At the start of a fresh
    scratch task the backend makes ONE small Groq call (temperature 0, tiny max
    tokens) that reads the request in any language (English / Urdu / Roman Urdu /
    Hinglish) and replies with a kebab-case name such as
    "three-page-static-website". The result is sanitized (lowercase a-z 0-9
    hyphen, max 40 chars) and collision-handled by the existing rename logic.
  - If that call fails, the name falls back to the SELECTED FRAMEWORK
    (html-project, react-project, next-project, …) — still never a prompt word.
  - openScratchWorkspace now always creates an anonymous Scratch; the socket
    renames it with the AI name (or the framework fallback) BEFORE the first
    file is written.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
6. MODEL SELECTION AUDIT (part 7)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  - preferedModelOrder() already put llama-3.1-8b-instant first and nothing
    overrode it (GROQ_MODEL is unset; the old stale test expectation of
    openai/gpt-oss-120b was the only place claiming otherwise). This is now
    proven: resolveModelPlan([key]) returns llama-3.1-8b-instant first, and the
    first agent request uses it.
  - The AI panel gained a MODEL selector next to the framework selector: Auto
    (recommended), Fast: llama-3.1-8b-instant, Balanced: llama-3.3-70b-versatile,
    Smart: openai/gpt-oss-120b. The choice is persisted per user
    (services/aiStorage.js) and sent with each run; the server honors it when the
    key can serve it. Auto escalates to a larger model automatically after
    repeated tool-call failures.
  - The panel header always shows the model the CURRENT run resolved (reset on
    each new run, then updated by every streamed event / agent:done).

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
FILES TOUCHED
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  server/src/services/agentService.js        parse recovery, append_to_file,
                                              plan+verify, resume, pacing,
                                              max_completion_tokens, 8b order,
                                              AI naming (suggestProjectName)
  server/src/services/agentSessionService.js  NEW per-project session store
  server/src/services/agentFrameworks.js      plan/chunked-writing/static-site
                                              rules + framework fallback names
  server/src/services/scratchProjectService.js framework fallback, no prompt name
  server/src/socket/agentSocket.js            AI naming before first write, resume
  server/src/controllers/workspace.controller.js always-anonymous scratch
  server/src/utils/workspaceManager.js        appendFileToWorkspace
  client/src/features/aiSlice.js              modelChoice, canContinue, resume
  client/src/components/AIPanel/AIPanel.jsx   model menu, Continue button, icons
  client/src/components/AIPanel/AIPanel.css   model menu + Continue styles
  client/src/services/aiStorage.js            per-user model preference
  client/src/utils/agentModels.js             NEW model options

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
VERIFICATION PERFORMED
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  - oxlint: 0 warnings / 0 errors (client). vite build: succeeds.
    node --check: passes on every changed server file.
  - tmp-agent-recovery-test.mjs — 22/22, driving the REAL runAgent against a
    scripted Groq stand-in (no MongoDB, no socket):
      A  AI naming returns three-page-static-website; framework fallback is
         html-project; model suggestion wins; parse-error detector matches the
         400 shape.
      A2 Auto puts llama-3.1-8b-instant first; an explicit choice is honored
         first.
      B  3 malformed-tool-call 400s -> two lowered-temperature retries announced
         as "retrying with smaller chunks", then a switch to openai/gpt-oss-120b,
         and the task COMPLETES (never aborts).
      C  PLAN captured (5 files), append_to_file streams "Writing index.html
         (part 2)", a premature "done" is rejected (2 files still missing), and
         all 5 planned files end up on disk with index.html = part1 + part2.
      D  A <=60s 429 shows a live countdown and the SAME request is retried
         automatically after the wait.
      E  An unrecoverable run errors out (no hang), saves its session, then a
         resume run streams the resume note and finishes using the saved history
         plus the continue note.
  - Run it with:  node tmp-agent-recovery-test.mjs

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
NOTES FOR THE NEXT DEVELOPER / AI TOOL
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  - deriveProjectName() still exists but is NO LONGER used for naming; do not
    wire it back into the naming path.
  - MAX_COMPLETION_TOKENS / MAX_PARSE_RETRIES / MAX_FALLBACK_WAIT_MS / the
    corrective note live at the top of agentService.js — tune them there.
  - The session store is in-memory (per server process); scratch workspaces are
    ephemeral too, so a server restart clears both. Move it to Mongo if resume
    must survive a restart.
  - Add new streaming action types to ACTION_ICONS in AIPanel.jsx so they get an
    icon instead of the generic sparkles fallback.


================================================================================
AI Code Editor — Phase 9: Vanilla Agent Output Quality (modular prompts + gate)
================================================================================

STATUS: complete and verified for the "HTML/CSS/JS (Vanilla)" stack. The other
        seven stacks still use short stub prompts on purpose and are NOT gated.

SCOPE — READ THIS FIRST
The whole hand-written blueprint, the design system, the plan schema, the
per-minute tiering and the deterministic quality gate below apply to the
*vanilla* stack only. react / next / vue / nuxt / angular / express / nest got a
few-line stub each ("keep the base workflow, scaffold, build") so the pipeline
keeps working; they will be trained in later steps. The gate is skipped entirely
for them (framework !== 'html'), so nothing about their output is newly enforced.

NOTE ON CODE COMMENTS: the no-comments rule is about THIS repository's own
source. Every file added in this phase is comment-free. The website the agent
generates is the opposite: the vanilla prompt explicitly asks for "short helpful
comments marking sections in the generated code", and the gate does not touch
comments (it only forbids lorem ipsum / TODO / "coming soon").

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
1. PROMPT FILE LAYOUT (server/src/agent/prompts/)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
server/src/agent/prompts/
  index.js          buildSystemPrompt({ framework, tier, now,
                    projectStructure }) -> base + runtime context +
                    "SELECTED STACK: <label>." + stack prompt + design system
                    (+ PROJECT STRUCTURE when the caller passes the workspace
                    tree). Exports VANILLA_FRAMEWORK_ID ('html'),
                    COMPACT_TPM_THRESHOLD (20000), PROMPT_TIERS,
                    isVanillaFramework(), stackLabel(), resolveTier(),
                    __testables.
  context.js        buildRuntimeContext(now) / buildContextBlock(now) /
                    formatDay(now) — clock only, no other state.
  base.js           BASE_PROMPT (general agent behaviour) + PLAN_SCHEMA_SECTION.
  stacks/vanilla.js vanillaPrompt, vanillaDesignSystem and the compact pair
                    (vanillaCompactPrompt, vanillaCompactDesignSystem).
  stacks/react.js   reactPrompt            }
  stacks/next.js    nextPrompt             }  short generic stubs,
  stacks/vue.js     vuePrompt              }  a few lines each, to be
  stacks/nuxt.js    nuxtPrompt             }  trained later
  stacks/angular.js angularPrompt          }
  stacks/express.js expressPrompt          }
  stacks/nest.js    nestPrompt             }
  quality/thresholds.js  MIN_LINE_COUNTS (css/style.css 400, js/main.js 120,
                    index.html 220, about.html 140, contact.html 120),
                    EXTENSION_MIN_LINES fallback, SECTION_MIN_CHARS 200,
                    MIN_REPEATED_ITEMS 3, MIN_FAQ_ITEMS 5, MIN_MEDIA_RULES 2,
                    GATE_MAX_REPAIR_ROUNDS 3, REQUIRED_STYLESHEET/SCRIPT,
                    REQUIRED_JS_SNIPPETS, minLinesFor(path).
  quality/vanillaGate.js vanillaGate({ projectId | root, plan, now }) and
                    formatGateReport(result).

COMPOSITION ORDER (byte-identical for the whole run, so provider prompt caching
applies): BASE_PROMPT, RUNTIME CONTEXT, SELECTED STACK line, stack prompt,
design system (vanilla only), PROJECT STRUCTURE. Tools (OPENAI_TOOLS) are
unchanged and the system message is built exactly ONCE per run:
agentService.buildSystemPrompt(framework, treeContext) is gone; runAgent now
calls buildSystemPrompt({ framework, tier, now: new Date(), projectStructure }).

The tier is chosen from the ACTIVE candidate's per-minute limit:
resolveTier(candidate.limits) -> 'compact' when tpm < 20000 (every Groq model:
6K-12K), 'full' otherwise (Mistral: 500K). Compact = terser blueprint plus a
terser design system (the section ids and every gated requirement are kept, so
compact output still passes the gate). Full prompt ~6.8K chars, compact ~6.1K.

AGENT_PROMPT DEVIATION (deliberate, please keep): the spec's base text says
"the plan JSON only (schema in Part 5)". Part 5 is a section of the task
document the model cannot read, so base.js now carries the schema itself as
PLAN_SCHEMA_SECTION and the first bullet points at it. Without that the model
has no way to know the JSON shape.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
2. HOW THE RUNTIME DATE IS INJECTED (fixes the "2023 problem")
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
context.js reads the SERVER CLOCK on every call — new Date(), never a hardcoded
year — and emits exactly:

  Current date: <YYYY-MM-DD>. Current year: <YYYY>. Treat this as the real
  present. Use it for copyright years, blog and testimonial dates, timelines
  and 'since' years. Never use an earlier year as the current year.

runAgent passes `now: new Date()` when it builds the system prompt, so a single
run is stable while a run started tomorrow carries the new date. The gate
derives the expected copyright year from the same clock (new Date().getFullYear()).

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
3. WHAT THE VANILLA PROMPT ASKS FOR
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
stacks/vanilla.js holds the blueprint: pure HTML5/CSS3/vanilla JS, no library of
any kind (no Tailwind, no build step), inline-SVG icons, never a remote image,
Google Fonts via link with a system fallback; index/about/contact at root plus
css/style.css and js/main.js; identical header and footer on every page with
[UPDATED 2026-09-28] files are now css/base.css (tokens + shared components)
PLUS one css/<page>.css per page, and js/main.js; a follow-up on an existing
css/style.css project keeps the single-stylesheet convention. See the per-page
CSS entry at the end of this file.
class="active" + aria-current="page"; the NAV RULE (an item with no page of its
own becomes a section on the home page and is linked as index.html#services from
every page — no dead links, no href="#"); the exact section ids per page
(home: hero, services, features, testimonials, blogs, faq, cta / about: page-hero,
story, mission, vision, values, stats, team / contact: page-hero, contact-form,
contact-info); the per-page content spec (hero with eyebrow, gradient-highlighted
h1 keyword, two CTAs, SVG/CSS visual, trust stats; 6 service cards; 3
 testimonials with 5-star SVG; 3 blog cards with a date inside the last 6 months;
6-item accessible accordion; CTA banner; about timeline/values/counters/team;
contact form with labels + inline errors + in-page success, info cards, CSS/SVG
map); the navbar (SVG brand mark, hamburger with aria-label/aria-expanded/
aria-controls below 900px, sticky with blur, closes on link click, outside click
and Escape); the footer (blurb, Quick Links, Contact, 4 labelled social SVGs,
(c) <span id="year">current year</span>); and the design system (tokens --bg,
--surface, --text, --muted, --primary, --primary-600, --accent, --border, spacing
scale, radii, shadows, clamp() type, container, transitions, one cohesive brand
palette, prefers-color-scheme dark, 4.5:1 contrast, gradient hero, glass navbar,
card lift, focus rings, 5-7rem section padding, IntersectionObserver .in-view
reveal, prefers-reduced-motion), responsive rules (480/768/1024/1280,
auto-fit minmax(260px, 1fr), 44px touch targets) and the single guarded
js/main.js feature list (hamburger, sticky header, active link, smooth scroll
with header offset, reveal, accordion, count-up, form validation, footer year via
getFullYear, back-to-top).

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
4. PLAN JSON, PROGRESS HINTS AND STAGE GUIDANCE
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
The first model reply is the plan JSON only:

  { project_name, brand { name, tagline, industry, palette { primary, accent,
    background, text }, fontStack }, nav [{ label, href }],
    files [{ path, purpose }], pages [{ file, sections[] }],
    requirements [] }

parsePlanJson() (agentService.js) accepts a fenced block, a bare object or JSON
surrounded by prose (balanced-brace scan, string/escape aware), validates it and
returns the normalized shape. planDoc is stored on the agent session
(agentSessionService now persists `planDoc` next to `plan`) so a resume keeps the
brand, nav, page map and requirements. `plan` (the array of file paths) is the
project_name-independent list the missing-file check walks. project_name is
given to the existing scratch-adoption flow via onProjectName, so the workspace
folder and display name are the AI-derived slug (never scratch-<id>); the old
PLAN:/FILES: regex parser is still there as a fallback for a model that ignores
the JSON instruction.

Every SUCCESSFUL write tool result now ends with:

  Progress X of Y planned files. Remaining: <paths or none>. Next: <path or
  "finish with your short summary">

STAGE GUIDANCE RULE: plan reminders, the tool-call correction note and the gate
report are appended to the LAST tool-result message (appendGuidance) instead of
being pushed as a new user/system message. A user message is only pushed when
there is no tool result yet (the very first reminder, right after the plan
reply). This keeps the message prefix stable and keeps providers happy. The
plan reply is pushed to the history on that reminder path (it used to be lost).

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
5. THE QUALITY GATE (deterministic, cheerio)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
after the model says it is finished (a turn with no tool calls, and only after
every planned file exists) runAgent runs vanillaGate over the workspace. cheerio
is now a server dependency (npm install cheerio in server/).

CHECKS, in the order the report lists them:
  planned-files      every planned file exists and meets minLinesFor(): the five
                     thresholds from thresholds.js, 100/150/50 lines for other
                     .html/.css/.js files, 1 line for anything else.
  page-structure     per .html page: doctype first, html[lang], meta charset,
                     viewport, non-empty description, non-empty title, exactly
                     one h1, header, header nav, main, footer, a hamburger button
                     with aria-expanded AND aria-controls, a stylesheet link that
                     resolves to css/style.css and a script src that resolves to
                     js/main.js.
  shared-header-footer  nav label|href pairs and the normalized footer text are
                     compared across pages (4-digit years normalized to YYYY).
  links              every href/src: no "" and no "#"; relative targets must
                     exist (with a /index.html fallback for directory links); a
                     #fragment must exist as an id in the target file; remote
                     <img src> is rejected. plan.nav labels must all appear in
                     the home page navbar.
  sections           every section id planned for that file exists with at least
                     SECTION_MIN_CHARS (200) characters of text; services,
                     testimonials, blogs and team contain at least 3 repeated
                     sibling items (same tag+class group); #faq has at least 5
                     buttons with aria-expanded; the contact page has a form
                     with labeled name/email/subject/message fields and a submit
                     button.
  content-year       no "lorem ipsum", "TODO" or "coming soon" in the HTML/CSS/JS;
                     a footer copyright year other than the current one is only
                     accepted when the footer carries <span id="year"> (filled
                     by js/main.js).
  assets             js/main.js compiles with vm.Script and contains
                     addEventListener, IntersectionObserver, aria-expanded and
                     getFullYear; css/style.css has balanced braces, a :root
                     block, >= 2 @media rules, prefers-reduced-motion and no
                     remote url(http...).

REPAIR LOOP: a failing report is truncated to 12 numbered problems and appended
to the last tool result as "QUALITY GATE FAILED: N problem(s) ... Fix exactly
these problems with edit_file or append_to_file (never rewrite a file that
already passes)." plus a 1-second-resolution note. Up to GATE_MAX_REPAIR_ROUNDS
(3) rounds run, each one a normal tool turn. If it still fails after 3 rounds the
run finishes HONESTLY: the summary text gets a "Still failing the quality gate:"
list, the `complete`/returned payload carries gatePassed: false and
gateFailures, and the session is cleared. Every gate run and result is streamed
as a `gate` action-feed entry (failures included in details, max 12) and the AI
panel maps `gate` to the ShieldCheck icon.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
6. MODEL, TEMPERATURE AND THE per-minute BUDGET (vanilla only)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
For framework 'html' with Auto (no pinned model) pickCandidateForMode() keeps
the active provider the existing budget logic chose and then takes the STRONGEST
allowlisted model on it (capabilityScore, e.g. mistral-large-latest on Mistral)
instead of whatever sat first in the list. An explicitly chosen model is never
overridden. Temperature is 0.3 for vanilla (0.2 otherwise) and still steps down
by 0.1 per parse retry. max completion tokens are unchanged: the existing
planRequest guard sizes them from the per-minute window.

ONE ROBUSTNESS FIX (needed to make an 8K-tpm provider usable at all): the
`error.budget` branch used to advance to another model and, when none was left,
spin through waitForCoolingPool() — which returns immediately when nothing is
cooling — and then throw "no per-minute token budget left". It now cools the
blocked candidate for BUDGET_WAIT_STEP_MS (20s) and calls the new
waitForBudgetWindow(), which waits for the earliest cooling reset (or one 20s
step when nothing is cooling) and retries, bounded by MAX_TOTAL_WAIT_MS (180s).
A Groq-only run of a 5-file vanilla site therefore waits out the minute window
instead of dying. The 429 path is untouched.
