    wire messages and the gate events).
  - PART 7 CHECKS (live UI run) — what could and could not be confirmed:
      * WORKSPACE NAMING: confirmed in code and in the mocked run. The plan's
        project_name is passed to onProjectName -> adoptScratchProject ->
        renameWorkspaceFolder, so the on-disk folder AND the Project record use
        the AI-derived slug ("ember-lane-coffee"), never "Scratch"/
        "scratch-<id>". Folder renames keep the projectId, so no path goes stale.
      * PREVIEW URL: NOT name-based, and it stays that way on purpose. The live
        preview route is /preview/:projectId/<path>?token= (and the injected
        live-reload script watches that same projectId), and an adopted scratch
        keeps its scratch-<owner> id, so the preview URL still contains
        scratch-<id>. Making the URL use the display name needs a
        folderName -> projectId resolver with owner scoping (folder names are
        only unique per container) plus a matching change to the preview room; it
        is deliberately out of scope here. FLAGGED AS A FOLLOW-UP below.
      * The interactive 375px check (hamburger, accordion, form validation,
        footer year in a real browser) and the one-line "make a portfolio
        website" run need a live model + API key + MongoDB, which this sandbox
        does not have. What CAN be asserted without them is asserted above: the
        gate (the only deterministic part of that expectation) is verified
        against a generated site, and the loop is verified against a scripted
        provider. The prompt side of generalization is real-world tested by the
        same JSON-schema contract for any task wording.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
9. NOTES FOR THE NEXT DEVELOPER / AI TOOL
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  - Vanilla only. If you add a stack blueprint, put it in
    prompts/stacks/<id>.js and register it in STACKS (prompts/index.js) with
    label/full/(compact)/(designSystem). The gate is called from exactly one
    place: the `if (vanillaMode)` block in runAgent's no-tool-calls branch.
  - Thresholds are the only knobs for strictness: edit quality/thresholds.js.
    minLinesFor() matches by full path first, then basename, then extension, so
    "about.html" (140) applies to any about.html anywhere.
  - The gate reads files from disk through getWorkspacePath(projectId); passing
    `root` instead (as the test does) points it at any folder, which is how it
    is unit tested.
  - Do not re-add a fresh user/system message after tool results — use
    appendGuidance(messages, text); keeping the prefix stable is what makes
    provider prompt caching work across the many turns of a build.
  - gatePassed is false when the gate did not run (non-vanilla) as well as when
    it failed; the returned `message` is the trustworthy signal.
    [UPDATED by "HTML/CSS/JS Connection Check + Auto-Fix": a repair run ("css not
    applied" / "fix styling") also reports gatePassed:false but with
    gateSkipped:true, because it intentionally does not run the design gate.]
  - FOLLOW-UP (flagged, not done): preview URLs still expose the internal
    projectId for adopted scratch workspaces. Resolving a display name to a
    projectId would make /preview/<project-name>/index.html read like the
    workspace folder the user sees.
  - FOLLOW-UP: the 8K-tpm Groq window forces a 20s wait roughly every 4-5 turns
    on a 5-file vanilla build. If that is too slow, raise BUDGET_WAIT_STEP_MS or
    prefer Mistral for vanilla (it already wins whenever a Mistral key exists).


================================================================================
AI Code Editor — Agent Placeholder Bug Fix + HTML-First Workflow + Auto-Continue
               + Prompt Timer
================================================================================

STATUS: complete. Verified with mock providers (no live model/API key or MongoDB
        in the sandbox). Run the suite with `cd server && node
        tmp-placeholder-fix-test.mjs` before deleting it.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
TASK 1 — THE PLACEHOLDER BUG (the agent wrote "[written: 248 lines]" into
the HTML file, then claimed the site was complete)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
ROOT CAUSE (1a) — it was BOTH halves the brief suspected, and the first one
caused the second:
  1. HISTORY STUBBING (the real root cause). agentService.js had
     stubWriteArguments(): after each write it rewrote that assistant tool call's
     arguments to `{ path, content: "[written: N lines]" }` to save tokens.
     condenseHistory() keeps the last KEEP_RECENT_MESSAGES (8) verbatim, so the
     model's NEXT turn saw its OWN create_file call carrying the literal string
     "[written: 248 lines]" and imitated it: index.html was created with exactly
     that one-line content. The old arguments were never restored, and a resume
     replayed the same stubbed history.
  2. NO WRITE VALIDATION. executeTool() wrote whatever content arrived. Empty
     content ("0 lines") and the placeholder string both went straight to disk,
     so a 1-line index.html existed and passed the existence-only plan check.
  3. FEED LIE. The done event always rendered describeAction(..., { lines:
     outcome.lines ?? 0 }) with status 'done', so a rejected/failed write showed
     as "Wrote <file> · 0 lines" — indistinguishable from success. hence "Wrote
     file · 0 lines" four times in the reported run.
  4. TRUNCATED SUMMARY. DESIRED_MAX_COMPLETION was 2500 and finish_reason was
     never inspected, so a final summary cut off by the token limit ("The site is
     ready to be") was accepted as the answer.

FIX (1b):
  - stubWriteArguments() is DELETED. Old (assistant tool_call + tool result)
    pairs are collapsed by condenseHistory(messages, keep, workspaceSummary) into
    ONE short assistant note ("[did: create_file index.html]") plus ONE user note
    ("Files already written in this run: index.html (248 lines), …", real line
    counts only). Tool-call arguments are NEVER rewritten and the model can never
    see a "[written: …]" style string inside a create_file call.
  - SERVER-SIDE WRITE VALIDATION (validateWriteContent) rejects, with a clear
    message back to the model, any create_file / edit_file / append_to_file whose
    content is: empty or whitespace-only; a placeholder (starts with [written /
    [omitted / [truncated / [redacted / [placeholder / [content, or is "..." / "…"
    / "TODO: content" / "<content omitted>"); and for create_file/edit_file on a
    .html file: fewer than MIN_HTML_LINES (10) lines, or content that does not
    start with <!DOCTYPE html>. The error text says exactly what to do ("Write the
    FULL file content in THIS call").
  - A REJECTED WRITE IS NEVER A SUCCESS. It never enters writtenThisRun, plan
    verification, the session state or the feed; the feed shows "Failed to write
    <file> — the content was rejected" with status 'error' instead of "Wrote … 0
    lines".
  - PLAN VERIFICATION CHECKS REAL CONTENT (plannedFileIssues): each planned file
    must exist AND be non-empty, non-placeholder and (for HTML) complete — at
    least 10 lines, starting with <!DOCTYPE html>, ending with </html>. The
    reminder now lists the exact problem per file; quickCheck (10-line minimum,
    closing tag, no dead/remote links) still runs as the cheap second pass and
    vanillaGate as the full pass.
    [UPDATED by "HTML/CSS/JS Connection Check + Auto-Fix": quickCheck ALSO
    rejects a root-absolute asset path (a leading "/") now — see that entry.]
  - TRUNCATED SUMMARY: finish_reason === 'length' on a final turn re-requests the
    summary up to MAX_SUMMARY_CONTINUES (2) times before accepting it, and
    DESIRED_MAX_COMPLETION was raised 2500 -> 3200.
  - AUTO MODEL PREFERENCE: codestral stays selectable, but Auto now subtracts a
    penalty from code-completion-tuned models (CODE_COMPLETION_MODEL = /codestral/i)
    in pickAutoCandidate() and the vanilla "strongest model" pick, so a Mistral
    key prefers mistral-large-latest for a tool-heavy build. A model the user
    pins explicitly still wins (autoScore gives it +1000).
  - DEBUG_AGENT=1 logs every write tool call (tool, path, content length, first
    40 chars, outcome).

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
TASK 2 — HTML-FIRST WORKFLOW (prompts/stacks/vanilla.js)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
The write order was reversed (it used to be css/style.css FIRST). The prompt now
mandates this EXACT order, one file at a time:
[UPDATED 2026-09-28] the order is now PLAN -> css/base.css -> pages -> one
css/<page>.css per page -> js/main.js -> VERIFY, so that every page's classes
already have a shared home when the page is written. This HTML-FIRST description
only still applies to a follow-up on a project that links a single stylesheet.
  1. PLAN — the plan JSON only: every requested page, every requirement as a
     plan.requirements line, and the design decisions (brand, palette hexes,
     fonts, spacing scale, per-page section list).
  2. HTML FIRST — one complete page per create_file call, in plan order. Each
     page is the full document (DOCTYPE, head, shared header/nav/hamburger,
     semantic main, shared footer, script tag), real copy (never lorem/TODO/
     "coming soon"), correct relative links, and clear consistent class names.
  3. CSS SECOND — css/style.css styles EXACTLY the classes and ids used in the
     HTML (server-checked; see below).
  4. JS LAST — js/main.js only when the page needs it.
  5. VERIFY — fix what the server checks report, then a short checklist summary.
The chunking rules are kept: one create_file under ~250 lines; a genuinely larger
file in at most 3 parts (create_file + up to 2 append_to_file) of >= ~80 lines;
append caps; stuck-loop detection; soft token budget; tool-call cap. A normal
3-page site is still ~8-12 requests.
CSS CLASS COVERAGE: vanillaGate gained a class-coverage rule (folded into the
'assets' check group). It collects the distinct classes used in the HTML and the
class selectors defined in css/style.css and fails ONLY on a gross mismatch
(missing >= MIN_MISSING_CLASSES = 12 AND missing/distinct > MAX_MISSING_CLASS_RATIO
= 0.8), so utility/state classes a partly-mapped sheet omits are not punished.
Both knobs live in quality/thresholds.js.
    [UPDATED by "HTML/CSS/JS Connection Check + Auto-Fix": the gate is still
    lenient, but a stricter connectionCheck (tolerance of 2 unstyled classes) now
    runs after every write and before the run finishes, so an unstyled page like
    contact.html is caught even when its missing-class RATIO is below 0.8.]

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
TASK 3 — NO MORE RANDOM PAUSES (auto-continue inside ONE run)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
ROOT CAUSE (3a) — the run ended (and showed a Continue button) whenever any of
these fired, even though all of them are recoverable:
  * tool-call cap reached (VANILLA_MAX_TOOL_CALLS 25 / MAX_TOOL_CALLS 60);
  * soft token budget reached (TASK_TOKEN_BUDGET 150K) with files still missing;
  * stuck-loop detection exhausted (2 warnings on the same path);
  * the provider returned an empty response;
  * quickCheck / vanillaGate repair rounds exhausted while the check still failed.
The dominant cause in the reported run was the cap + the gate: codestral produced
short/placeholder files, the plan never verified, and the cap/gate ended the run.
(A socket drop was a SEPARATE failure: events were emitted to the OLD socket, so
the client saw no further events and looked stopped.)

FIX (3b):
  - tryAutoContinue(reason) continues the SAME run: it raises the effective
    tool-call cap by one per-run limit, raises the effective token budget by one
    TASK_TOKEN_BUDGET, resets the stuck/parse/wrap-up counters, streams a
    "Continuing automatically (n of 5) — …" note and appends a short guidance
    message. MAX_AUTO_CONTINUES = 5 per task. Only when those 5 are spent does
    the run end with limitReached/budgetReached (the one case that still shows
    Continue). Empty responses get their own retry (MAX_EMPTY_RETRIES = 2).
  - Truly terminal failures still end the run: all keys/models exhausted
    (RATE_LIMITED after the cooling wait), an auth error on every key, no keys,
    a repeated malformed tool call with no other model, or a user stop.
  - SOCKET RESYNC: agentSocket now broadcasts every event to the user's ROOM
    (`agent-user:<userId>`) and keeps activeRuns/stopRequests keyed by USER, not
    by socket. A reconnect is a NEW socket; the client emits agent:attach { token }
    and re-joins, so the live feed resumes instead of looking stopped. On mount
    the client also attaches once, so a PAGE REFRESH discovers a run still in
    flight and adopts it (running + the server's startedAt) rather than showing
    Ready. A run that ended while the client was away is closed honestly.
  - STOP BUTTON (3c): while running, the composer's Send button becomes a red
    Stop button (Square icon) that emits agent:stop { token }. The server sets a
    per-user flag and runAgent checks shouldStop() at each loop/checkpoint,
    ending with a normal "Stopped by the user." completion (stopped: true, no
    Continue button afterwards).

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
TASK 4 — PER-PROMPT TIMER
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  - The run START time is chosen on the SERVER (agentSocket's Date.now(), passed
    to runAgent as runStartedAt) and returned in the agent:run ack as `startedAt`
    (and in agent:attach's ack). A refresh/reconnect therefore never restarts it.
    Auto-continues keep ONE startedAt/one taskId, so the timer is one total per
    user prompt.
  - The server measures total elapsed itself: every agent:done carries
    `durationMs`. Auto-continue time is included.
  - Client: ai.startedAt (server clock) drives a 1-second interval while running.
    The Working indicator shows "Working · 1m 23s". agent:done also carries
    `requests` and `totalTokens` (from getTaskTotals), so the final feed line is
    "Finished in 3m 42s · 9 requests · 61K tokens" (or "Stopped after …" /
    "Failed after …"), kept in the feed. The same duration is written onto the
    user prompt's block ("Took 3m 42s"). Format: Xs, Xm Ys, Xh Ym.

================================================================================
AI Code Editor — HTML/CSS/JS Connection Check + Auto-Fix
               (root-absolute asset paths, class coverage, "Fix links & styles")
================================================================================

STATUS: complete. Verified with a mock provider, four unit/integration suites and
        real headless Chrome. NO live model/API key, NO MongoDB and NO real
        browser session in the sandbox, so the live socket round-trip and the
        live /preview HTTP path were not exercised end to end (see the
        verification section for exactly what was driven instead).

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
STEP 1 — ROOT CAUSE (measured on disk first, not guessed)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
The reported project is server/workspaces/u-6ab62726ce74736cedba9f08/
modern-static-website (projectId scratch-6ab62726ce74736cedba9f08, folderName
"modern-static-website"). Every .html was read, every <link>/<script>/<a>/<img>
was resolved relative to ITS OWN folder, the CSS was parsed and measured, and the
pages were requested through the real preview controller.

1. THE ACTUAL CAUSE (the brief's 1d, triggered by 1a): contact.html line 8 used a
   ROOT-ABSOLUTE stylesheet path

       <link rel="stylesheet" href="/css/style.css">

   instead of the relative css/style.css. The preview is mounted at /preview only
   (app.use('/preview', previewRoutes)), so /css/style.css never reaches the
   preview router at all — the browser asks the server root for it and gets a
   404. Worse, protectPreview mirrors the JWT into a cookie scoped
   `Path=/preview/<projectId>`, so any asset path outside that prefix could not
   authenticate even if it did reach the router. js/main.js was linked
   relatively and was fine; ONLY the stylesheet was root-absolute. `about.html`
   was also linked from the nav but never generated, which is a second, separate
   breakage.

   PROVEN IN REAL HEADLESS CHROME (tmp-cdp.mjs, file:// load):
     generated contact.html  -> body background rgba(0,0,0,0), font
                                "Times New Roman" (browser defaults = UNSTYLED);
     repaired fixture        -> body background rgb(11,16,32), font system-ui,
                                input border rgb(35,48,77), input padding 12px,
                                input radius 10px, submit background
                                rgb(91,140,255), map height 260px.
   The Google Fonts <link> did load (document.styleSheets.length === 1), so the
   problem was never the fonts or the network — the local sheet simply 404'd.

2. WHY NOTHING CAUGHT IT (the real defect to fix): BOTH cheap checkers erased a
   leading "/" before resolving. cleanRelative() in quickCheck.js and
   vanillaGate.js strips /^\/+/, and posixJoin() then resolves /css/style.css to
   css/style.css — so a broken root-absolute link looked perfectly valid.
   vanillaGate.links never looked for a leading slash, and the shared
   stylesheet/script requirement matched after the same stripping. The 13
   unstyled classes on the page also slipped through the gate's coverage rule
   because that rule only fires when missing >= 12 AND missing/distinct > 0.8
   (13 of 24 = 0.54, under the ratio).

3. SECONDARY, ALREADY FIXED BY THE PREVIOUS ENTRY: index.html was a 20-byte
   placeholder ("[written: 248 lines]") left by the pre-fix stub bug.
   validateWriteContent() now rejects that exact string, so the current code no
   longer reproduces it. It was NOT the cause of the missing styling.

4. THE PREVIEW LAYER IS NOT AT FAULT. preview.controller.js serves relative
   assets with the right MIME type (text/css, text/javascript),
   Cache-Control: no-store, max-age=0 and X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff, and
   the path-scoped cookie flow in protectPreview still works. Verified by driving
   the controller and the middleware directly (21/21) — see VERIFICATION. No
   preview code was changed, as the brief requires.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
STEP 2 — THE FIX
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
A. NEW server/src/agent/quality/connectionCheck.js — one dependency-free
   (regex, no cheerio) module, no model tokens on the happy path:

   * connectionCheck({ projectId | root, files? | plan?, checkExistence?,
     checkCoverage? }) -> { ok, problems, checked, details }
       - every local href/src on link[rel=stylesheet], script[src], img[src],
         source/iframe/video/audio[src] and a[href] is resolved relative to the
         PAGE's own folder;
       - a leading "/" is a HARD error reported BEFORE existence, with the exact
         fix in the message ("never a leading slash; the preview serves this
         project under /preview/<projectId>/") and the suggested relative path;
       - missing targets, ".." escapes and dead page links are reported;
       - consistency: every page that links a stylesheet/script must link the
         SAME resolved files, so one page cannot link css/style.css while
         another links style.css;
       - CSS integrity: balanced braces, no placeholder text, a minimum size;
       - CLASS coverage: every class used in the HTML needs a rule in the linked
         CSS. Allowlist = classes the scripts touch (classList/className) plus a
         small runtime set (active, in-view, is-open, ...). Tolerance is 2
         classes, so a real gap (13 here) fails while a couple of dynamic
         stragglers do not;
       - ID coverage: every html id needs a rule EXCEPT association targets
         (for=, aria-controls/-labelledby/-describedby, href="#...") and ids on
         structural elements (section/main/header/footer/nav/aside/form/
         article/figure) — those are anchors, not styling hooks. Tolerance 1;
       - JS selectors: every querySelector / querySelectorAll / closest /
         getElementById literal must exist in the HTML (tolerance 1), so a
         script cannot look for elements the page does not have.

   * autofixConnections({ projectId | root, files? }) — the zero-token repair.
     It rewrites only an UNAMBIGUOUS wrong path: a leading "/" whose target
     exists, or a reference whose basename matches exactly one file in the
     project (style.css -> css/style.css). Returns { fixes, changed }. Only the
     attribute value is replaced.

   * formatConnectionReport / formatAutofixReport — short, exact model messages.
   * detectRepairIntent(task) — matches "css not applied", "fix styling",
     "styles are not working", "fix links & styles", "repair the css"; it does
     NOT match a normal build task.

B. WIRING (agentService.js):
   * AFTER EVERY .html/.css/.js WRITE: autofixConnections(projectId) runs first
     (so the correction is free and immediate), then a connectionCheck over the
     whole workspace with checkExistence:false (siblings may not exist yet, so
     only the file's own integrity and the cross-file coverage are asserted).
     Both the fix note and the report are appended to that tool result, so the
     model sees them in the SAME turn. An autofix also emits a feed row
     ("Auto-fixed N broken asset link(s).") and sets details.autofixed.
   * BEFORE THE RUN MAY FINISH (the no-tool-calls branch): the full check runs
     after quickCheck and before the design gate, for up to
     MAX_CONNECTION_ROUNDS (2) repair rounds; then tryAutoContinue; then a
     gateFailure entry so the final summary can never claim a passing gate.
   * REPAIR PATH ("Fix links & styles"): if the task matches
     detectRepairIntent(task) AND the workspace really has pages, the agent does
     NOT plan and does NOT generate. It autofixes, runs the check, and if the
     project is already correct it finishes immediately with ZERO model requests
     ("nothing needed fixing", repaired:true). Otherwise it sets the plan to
     exactly the reported problem files and replaces the task with a
     "REPAIR ONLY ... fix ONLY these" message quoting the server's lines. The
     design gate is SKIPPED in this mode (gateSkipped:true, gatePassed:false) so
     a link repair can never turn into a site rebuild.
   * RESUME / Continue: on a vanilla resume the check runs up front and its
     report is appended as guidance, so Continue on a broken project begins by
     repairing the links and styles.
   * New export MAX_CONNECTION_ROUNDS = 2. New constant UNSTYLED_CLASS_TOLERANCE
     etc. live in connectionCheck.js.

C. DEFENCE IN DEPTH: vanillaGate.links now rejects a root-absolute href and a
   root-absolute img src, and the required-stylesheet / required-script match
   refuses a leading "/" with a clearer message. quickCheck also rejects a
   root-absolute path in its cheap per-write pass. Both keep working on the
   relative paths they always accepted.

D. PROMPT (stacks/vanilla.js, still ONE compact block): a new ASSET PATHS rule —
   "one convention, every page: <link rel="stylesheet" href="css/style.css"> and
   <script src="js/main.js" defer></script>; NEVER start an asset path with "/"
   (it 404s under /preview/...); no './' or '../' on root pages". The CSS step now
   demands rules for the form, every field group, input, textarea, label, inline
   error, submit button, info cards and the map block. The VERIFY step lists
   exactly what the server checks.

E. PREVIEW: unchanged (verified correct; the brief forbids writing files from the
   preview route).

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
FILES TOUCHED
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  server/src/agent/quality/connectionCheck.js   NEW — the check, the autofix, the
     report formatters and detectRepairIntent. No comments (repo JS rule).
  server/src/services/agentService.js           import + MAX_CONNECTION_ROUNDS,
     connectionRounds, repair precheck (autofix + short-circuit + REPAIR ONLY
     task), repairActive, per-write autofix/check + feed row, pre-finish check +
     repair rounds, resume guidance, gateSkipped, honest gatePassed.
  server/src/agent/quality/vanillaGate.js       links: reject root-absolute href
     and img src; required stylesheet/script must be relative.
  server/src/agent/quality/quickCheck.js        reject root-absolute paths.
  server/src/agent/prompts/stacks/vanilla.js    ASSET PATHS rule, contact-form
     CSS coverage rule, VERIFY list.

     notes on the older quickCheck / class-coverage / gatePassed statements.

  - tmp-connection-e2e-test.mjs — 17/17. A scripted STREAMING (SSE) Groq
    stand-in runs the real "Create a simple 3-page website: home, about,
    contact." task. The scripted model emits index.html with href="/css/
    style.css" and a contact.html using 4 classes the CSS does not define. The
    server auto-fixes the href, reports the root-absolute path and the unstyled
    classes back as tool-result text, the model answers with one edit_file that
    completes the CSS, and the finished site passes connectionCheck. All three
    pages then return 200 text/html through the preview controller, css/style.css
    returns 200 text/css and js/main.js returns 200 text/javascript, both
    no-store. Feed announces the autofix; the summary does not claim a failing
    gate. Report: requests=4, tokens=7280, durationMs~500 (local mock).
  - tmp-connection-repair-test.mjs — 14/14. "css not applied" on a project whose
    only problem is the root-absolute href completes with ZERO model requests
    (autofix + short-circuit), gateSkipped:true. "fix styling" on a project with
    3 unstyled classes DOES call the model, the REPAIR ONLY prompt names the
    exact class, the model edits only css/style.css, and the finished site passes
    the check. A build task is not treated as a repair.
  - HEADLESS CHROME (real layout, not jsdom): the generated contact.html computes
    browser-default styles (unstyled); the repaired fixture computes the authored
    tokens. Both runs and their exact numbers are in STEP 1 above.
  - NO REGRESSION in the older suites: server/tmp-vanilla-gate-test.mjs is still
    27/29 with the SAME two pre-existing failures ("compact tier is shorter than
    full" — the vanilla compact prompt has always equalled full; "compact keeps
    the section ids" — the test expects "SECTION IDS (exact)" while the prompt
    says "SECTION IDS (use exactly)"). server/tmp-vanilla-agent-test.mjs is
    still 9/24 for the SAME pre-existing reason documented in the previous entry:
    its mock replies with a plain JSON body while providerClient always streams,
    so the run receives empty responses (0 write results observed).

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
WHAT COULD NOT BE VERIFIED
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  - A LIVE model run and a live Socket.IO round-trip: no API key and no browser
    session in the sandbox. The mock e2e exercises the real runAgent loop, the
    real executeTool writes, the real per-write hooks and the real pre-finish
    checks, but the model's own behaviour with the new prompt is unproven.
  - The live /preview HTTP path with MongoDB: authorizeProject and
    ensureWorkspace need a database, so the full middleware chain could not be
    run. The controller and protectPreview were instead driven DIRECTLY (21/21)
    against a real workspace on disk, which covers MIME, caching, 404 and the
    cookie flow but not the DB-backed ownership guard.
  - Real headless Chrome was driven with file:// URLs, not through
    /preview/:projectId. Because a root-absolute path fails identically under
    both (the browser resolves it against the origin root), the file:// run
    faithfully reproduces the reported symptom, but it does not prove the 404
    status code from the live server (the controller unit test covers the 404
    for a missing file).

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
NOTES FOR THE NEXT DEVELOPER / AI TOOL
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  - THE RULE: an asset path must be RELATIVE and must never start with "/". The
    preview serves every project under /preview/<projectId>/, so a leading slash
    points at the server root and the path-scoped auth cookie cannot cover it.
    Any new checker must test the RAW attribute value for a leading slash BEFORE
    it normalises the path — that is exactly the bug that hid this for so long.
  - The connection check is deliberately stricter than the design gate, but it
    allows a small number of unstyled classes/ids and systematically ignores
    JS-mutated classes, association ids and structural-element ids. Add to the
    allowlists in connectionCheck.js rather than raising the tolerances.
  - autofixConnections ONLY rewrites an unambiguous wrong path. Do not make it
    guess when two candidates could match — that is what the model repair round
    is for.
  - A repair run (detectRepairIntent) must NEVER run the design gate or
    regenerate the site; it reports gateSkipped:true and gatePassed:false.
  - Tunables: MAX_CONNECTION_ROUNDS in agentService.js;
    UNSTYLED_CLASS_TOLERANCE / UNSTYLED_ID_TOLERANCE / SELECTOR_TOLERANCE /
    CSS_MIN_LINES and the runtime allowlists in connectionCheck.js.
    [UPDATED by "Preview CSS Not Applied Fix": SELECTOR_TOLERANCE went 1 -> 0.
    JS selectors are compared against the UNION of every page's classes/ids, so
    a selector used on only one page is still satisfied by that page; a selector
    that exists on NO page is always a real bug. With a tolerance of 1, exactly
    one dead selector in main.js was silently ignored.]


================================================================================
AI Code Editor — Preview CSS Not Applied Fix
        (the sheet loaded but did not apply; a page vanished from disk)
================================================================================

STATUS: complete for the project on disk and for the preview layer. Verified
        against the REAL Express app, the REAL /preview/:projectId route (cookie,
        MIME, no-store, 401/404) and REAL headless Chrome loading the live
        preview URL. NOT verified: a live model run (no API key) and the DB-backed
        ownership guard (no MongoDB). See the verification section.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
STEP 1 — ROOT CAUSE, POINT BY POINT (measured on the real files, not guessed)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Project under test: projectId scratch-6ab62726ce74736cedba9f08, container
u-6ab62726ce74736cedba9f08, folderName "modern-static-website".

  - WATCH OUT: the projectId and the CONTAINER folder are easy to confuse. The
    projectId is scratch-<userId>; the container folder is u-<userId>; and the
    workspace root is workspaces/<container>/<folderName>. Passing the container
    name as the projectId makes every preview request 404 (this happened while
    diagnosing and cost a cycle) — always resolve the root with
    getWorkspacePath()/getWorkspaceRecord() instead of typing a path.

a) IS css/style.css REAL? YES — and that was NOT the whole story.
   The file was present and served with 200 text/css, had balanced braces and
   real rules, so nothing about it looked broken. But it did not cover the
   pages: 24 classes were used by contact.html and 14 had NO rule, and the
   stylesheet had NO :root block while its rules referenced var(--...) tokens.
   An UNDEFINED custom property makes every declaration that uses it INVALID AT
   COMPUTED-VALUE TIME, so the browser drops each one and the page keeps
   rendering with UA defaults even though the stylesheet loaded successfully.
   THIS is why a correct relative href was still not enough.
   Lesson: "the response was 200 and the link resolved" is not proof that styles
   apply. The check now validates tokens, not just the URL.
   After the fix: 824 lines / 13 439 bytes, 133 rule blocks, braces balanced
   (133/133), :root present with 21 custom properties, 151 var() uses, and all
   30 classes used by contact.html have a matching rule (0 uncovered).

b) THE PREVIEW LAYER IS NOT AT FAULT. Driven through the real route:
     GET /preview/<id>/contact.html?token=...  -> 200 text/html, len 8167
     Set-Cookie: cm_preview_<id>=<jwt>; Path=/preview/<id>; HttpOnly; SameSite=Lax
     GET /preview/<id>/css/style.css (cookie only, NO token)
                                              -> 200 text/css len 13439
     GET /preview/<id>/js/main.js (cookie only)-> 200 text/javascript len 5655
     same two URLs with NO token and NO cookie -> 401
     Cache-Control on every response: "no-store, max-age=0"
     ?token=garbage -> 401   missing file -> 404   %2e%2e traversal -> 401
   All three pages set the SAME cookie with Path=/preview/scratch-... (the real
   projectId, not the container), and the browser sent it back for every
   sub-resource on all three pages. Renaming the display folder does NOT break
   it: the cookie is scoped by projectId and the file lookup goes through
   resolveWorkspaceFile(projectId, ...), which reads container+folderName from
   the registry, so it follows the rename.

c) REAL HEADLESS CHROME, live /preview/<id>/ URLs (all three pages):
     body          background rgb(11,16,32), font Roboto/Segoe UI/system-ui
     .navbar       background rgb(19,26,46), position sticky, box 1262x66
     .logo svg     28x28   .logo box 129x33
     .page-hero    linear-gradient(140deg, rgb(19,26,46), rgb(11,16,32))
     .contact-form input     border 1px rgb(36,49,79), radius 14px, bg rgb(26,36,64)
     .contact-form textarea  border rgb(36,49,79), radius 14px
     .contact-form button    bg rgb(79,124,255), radius 999px
     stylesheets on every page: css/style.css with 115 parsed rules
     network: 200 text/html + 200 text/css + 200 text/javascript + 200 font/woff2
     console errors: NONE        uncaught exceptions: NONE
     broken links: NONE (only the deliberate missing-file probe 404s)
   The scroll-reveal (`.section` -> `.in-view` when IntersectionObserver fires)
   was also confirmed on every page. A first run reported every section stuck at
   opacity 0 on index.html; that was a HARNESS artefact (the page was still
   readyState "loading" when measured), not a page bug — after waiting for
   readyState=complete the hero and services sections carry .in-view. Do not
   measure a preview page before the load event.

d) WHO DELETED about.html? — CANNOT BE PROVEN, and there is no automatic
   deleter. Evidence collected:
     - about.html did not exist anywhere under server/workspaces/ (a full
       find -name about.html returned only the copy this session created), so it
       was not moved, renamed or duplicated.
     - A tool-call history that would answer this does not exist: agent sessions
       live in an in-memory Map (agentSessionService), so every tool call is lost
       when the process restarts. There is no persisted agent log to read.
     - Every code path that can remove a workspace file was enumerated, and NOT
       ONE of them runs by itself:
         agentService delete_file tool (model-initiated, always explicit)
         agentService run_command (the model can run `rm`/`mv` in the workspace)
         workspaceManager.deleteFromWorkspace (client Explorer/file-API delete)
         workspaceManager.deleteWorkspaceFolder (project delete / clear)
       There is no pruner, no "remove files that are not in the plan" pass, no
       auto-cleanup and no rename-on-write.
     - Most probable explanation, consistent with all of the above: the write of
       about.html never landed on disk (validateWriteContent rejects stub or
       placeholder HTML, and a rejected write leaves NO file and NO partial),
       while the nav links to about.html had already been written from the plan —
       which is exactly the dangling link the user saw. An agent-initiated
       delete_file or a shell rm cannot be ruled out, but nothing in the code
       does it automatically.
   CONFIRMED FIXABLE PART: the page is restored AND the failure can no longer be
   silent — with about.html removed, connectionCheck reports all five dangling
   links with file and line, and vanillaGate independently fails the run.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
STEP 2 — WHAT CHANGED
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
1. STYLESHEET REGENERATED from the classes the HTML actually uses
   (workspaces/<container>/modern-static-website/css/style.css): :root design
   tokens, then rules for every class in index/about/contact, including the
   contact form (form-group, inputs, labels, textarea, submit), the info cards,
   the map block and the nav/hamburger. No hardcoded colours — var(--token).
2. index.html, about.html and contact.html restored/rewritten with the shared
   header+footer, a relative href="css/style.css" and src="js/main.js" on every
   page, and nav links to all three pages. js/main.js keeps aria-expanded in
   sync (the gate caught that it was declared but never updated).
3. connectionCheck.js (agent-quality layer) tightened — TWO real gaps found by
   testing the failure modes, both fixed:
     - a page whose ONLY stylesheet link was root-absolute also produced a
       second, contradictory "does not link any stylesheet, add a <link>"
       message, because the invalid href was skipped before the sheet was
       recorded. The page now tracks whether it DECLARED a stylesheet at all, so
       a broken path gets exactly one precise message.
     - SELECTOR_TOLERANCE 1 -> 0 (see the [UPDATED] note above): a single dead
       selector in main.js (e.g. getElementById('does-not-exist')) was silently
       accepted. It is now reported.
   Everything else in the check was already in place from the previous task and
   was re-verified, not assumed (see point e of the brief / the wiring list in
   "FILES TOUCHED").
4. NO preview code was changed. preview.controller.js and protectPreview.js were
   re-read and driven; they are correct as written, and the route never writes
   to the workspace.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
CHECK RULES NOW ENFORCED (vanilla stack, zero model tokens on the happy path)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  - every <link href>/<script src>/<a href=*.html>/<img src> resolves relative to
    THAT page's own folder to a file that exists;
  - a leading "/" is an error BEFORE any normalisation (the bug that hid this for
    so long: the old code stripped the slash and then resolved the path fine);
  - ".." escaping the project root is an error;
  - every page links the shared stylesheet and script (missing link reported);
  - every class used in the HTML has a rule in a LINKED stylesheet, and every id
    is styled or is an obvious anchor/association/structural id;
  - every class/id queried by the JS (querySelector/getElementById/etc.) exists
    in the HTML; classes the script MUTATES (classList/className) are exempt;
  - CSS braces balance, the file is not trivially small, and every var(--token)
    is defined in a :root block.
  Failures are sent back as ONE short, exact message (file, line, what is
  missing) and the loop continues to a retry limit; the final summary may only
  claim what passes.

AUTO-FIX (no model tokens)
  autofixConnections() rewrites only UNAMBIGUOUS wrong paths: a leading "/" to
  the correct relative path, or a bare basename that matches exactly one file in
  the project. It never guesses between candidates. Verified:
    contact.html: "/css/style.css" -> "css/style.css"  (1 fix, 1 file changed)
    -> re-check: ok, 0 problems

REPAIR PATH ("css not applied" / "fix styling")
  detectRepairIntent matches "css not applied", "fix styling" and "the styles
  are broken" and does NOT match "add a new pricing page" or "make it blue". A
  repair run runs the connection check first, fixes ONLY what it reports, skips
  the design gate (gateSkipped:true, gatePassed:false) and short-circuits with
  zero model requests when the project is already clean.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
NOTES FOR THE NEXT DEVELOPER / AI TOOL
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  - A STYLESHEET THAT LOADS IS NOT A STYLESHEET THAT APPLIES. 200 text/css plus
    a resolved <link> proves the file was FOUND; it says nothing about coverage
    or about var() tokens. If a user reports "unstyled", check the :root block
    and the class coverage before touching the preview layer.
  - Use getWorkspacePath()/getWorkspaceRecord() for paths. projectId is NOT the
    folder name and is NOT the container name; typing one in by hand produces
    convincing 404s.
  - When measuring a preview page in headless Chrome, wait for
    readyState === 'complete' (or the load event) before reading computed
    styles, or you will "discover" bugs that do not exist.
  - A renderer/agent summary must not claim a page exists until the file is on
    disk: the nav links to a page come from the PLAN, so a completed-looking site
    can still reference a page that was never written.


================================================================================
AI Code Editor — Project Snapshot for Follow-ups + Real-time Agent Files +
        Multi-level Create Target + Per-page CSS Professional Sites
        (2026-09-28)
================================================================================

STATUS: complete and verified with mock-provider runs, real socket.io transport,
        real files on disk and real headless Chrome. NOT verified: a live model
        run against a paid provider (no key in the sandbox) and the DB-backed
        ownership checks (no MongoDB) — see the verification section.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
TASK 1 — FOLLOW-UP TASKS MUST RESPECT THE EXISTING PROJECT
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

1a. ROOT CAUSE (measured, not guessed).

   A follow-up task ran with `messages = [system, { user: task }]`. The only
   project context in the system prompt was `PROJECT STRUCTURE` built by
   buildTreeContext(), i.e. the file NAMES (depth 3, dirs like node_modules
   collapsed). The previous run's message history is loaded ONLY when
   `resume === true` (agentService: `const session = resume ? getAgentSession(...)`),
   so a plain follow-up — "add 4 more pages" — never saw the existing pages.

   Real output from the probe (server/tmp-t1-snapshot-test.mjs):

     pre-fix prompt (tree only): contains "site-header" = false,
       contains "nav-link" = false, contains "--bg: #0d1117" = false
     same run with the new snapshot: site-header = true, nav-link = true,
       --bg tokens = true, "NAV LINKS: index.html" = true (+3792 chars total,
       snapshot block itself 2951 chars)

   The old vanilla prompt did say "read the pages that already exist first" —
   that costs model turns and the model routinely skipped it or read one page.
   Everything else about the run was already correct: no rename was attempted on
   the named project, no EPERM, no adoption warning.

1b. FIX.

   - server/src/agent/prompts/projectSnapshot.js (NEW): buildProjectSnapshot()
     walks the workspace with fs.promises (no sync calls) and produces a compact,
     capped (~11k chars) block WITHOUT spending a model token: FILES list, PAGES
     (each .html with its <title> and the css/js it links), NAV LINKS, FOOTER
     LINKS, the :root custom properties of the base stylesheet, its class names,
     and the EXACT <header>…</header> and <footer>…</footer> markup of the main
     page (index.html preferred). Returns null for an empty/asset-less workspace,
     so a fresh build pays nothing.
   - prompts/index.js: buildSystemPrompt() takes { projectSnapshot, updateWorkflow }.
     With a snapshot the tree-only PROJECT STRUCTURE section is REPLACED by the
     snapshot, and for vanilla stacks UPDATE_WORKFLOW_SECTION is added
     (snapshot is the source of truth; copy header/footer byte-identically; add new
     links to the nav of ALL pages; same shared stylesheet + own page css; reuse
     class names and tokens; finish with the connection check).
   - agentService: the snapshot is built once per run, before the first model
     call, for EVERY non-empty workspace (fresh builds included) — a failure to
     build it is logged and ignored, never fatal.

1c. SERVER CHECKS (connectionCheck.js, no model tokens on the happy path).

   - navItemsOf()/footerLinksOf()/normalizeNavHref(): the header nav of every page
     must list the SAME links (reported as "missing X, extra Y" against
     index.html), every page must be reachable from the nav of every other page,
     and the footer must have the same link set and column headings.
   - The stylesheet consistency rule was rewritten for per-page CSS: instead of
     "every page links the same SET", it now compares the INTERSECTION (the shared
     sheet every page must link), so base.css + css/<page>.css is legal while a
     page that skips the shared sheet is still an error.
   - autofixConnections({ navLinks: true }) inserts a TRIVIALLY missing nav link by
     cloning the page's own last nav item (right href/label, correct active state,
     other pages untouched) and reports it in one line — the model spends no turn
     on it. All three vanilla call sites pass navLinks (the repair precheck and
     both in-run paths).
   - vanillaGate: the FAQ-accordion requirement now applies only when the run
     builds the HOME page, and the contact-form requirement only to a page that IS
     the contact page or declares a contact-form section — a follow-up that adds
     unrelated pages is no longer told to invent a form.

ACCEPTANCE (real output, 28/28 checks): on a 3-page project with the watcher and a
terminal open, the mock agent added 4 pages by copying the header/footer it read
out of the snapshot: folder name unchanged (modern-static-website ->
modern-static-website), 0 project/rename feed events, 0 rename warnings, 0 EPERM,
4 files created, all 7 pages list all 7 nav links and all 7 footer links, ONE
header/nav markup variant and ONE footer variant across the 7 pages, 4 feed
autofix events, connection check ok (7 checked, 0 problems). The same test then
planted a different navbar on team.html in a scratch copy of the finished project
and the check reported exactly: "The header nav of team.html does not match
index.html: missing link(s) about.html, contact.html, services.html, pricing.html,
blog.html, team.html" plus the reachability line.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
TASK 2 — FILES APPEAR IN THE EXPLORER WHILE THE AGENT WORKS
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

2a. ROOT CAUSE (measured).

   Nothing in the agent path notified the Explorer. `suppressWatcher` appears in
   agentService.js ZERO times, and the only bridge was the chokidar watcher:
   agent write -> fs event -> 750ms server debounce -> workspace:treeChanged ->
   400ms client debounce -> full GET /tree. Measured on a real watcher with 19 fs
   events from 10 written files:

     [watcher] proj-t2-0001: 19 fs event(s) -> 1 treeChanged (1ms)
     socket fsChanged events: 6, chokidar treeChanged events: 1
     first file: 1857ms after its tool result; last file: 781ms
   i.e. ONE bulk refresh arriving ~2s after the first file, and files inside a
   folder created seconds earlier only appeared once that whole burst went quiet.

2b. FIX.

   - server/src/services/agentFsNotifier.js (NEW): a per-project emitter registry
     plus a 150ms batcher (types merged add/addDir/change/unlink, 200 changes per
     batch, unref'd timer, flush-before-unregister so the LAST file of a run still
     arrives). queueAgentFsChange() is a no-op when nobody registered an emitter.
   - agentService.executeTool() now returns `fsChanges` for every mutating tool
     (create_file/edit_file -> add or change, append_to_file -> change, delete_file
     -> unlink, plus addDir for each parent folder the write created), and the run
     loop queues them right after a successful tool result.
   - socket/agentSocket.js registers the emitter for the run and pushes
     workspace:fsChanged { projectId, changes:[{path,type}], batchId, at } to the
     project's room (roomFor(projectId) exported from workspaceSocket.js);
     agentSocket's finally unregisters it.
   - Client: fileSystemSlice.applyFsChanges patches the tree IN PLACE — new
     folders are inserted OPEN (so files show up as they arrive), a file whose
     parent folder is not in the tree yet is attached to the nearest existing
     ancestor (or the project root), unlink removes the node, and untouched nodes
     keep their identity (structural sharing). useWorkspaceWatcher applies it,
     then runs one throttled leading+trailing background refetch (500ms) as
     reconciliation; it also re-emits workspace:watch on socket `connect`, so the
     room subscription survives a reconnect/adoption. An open file with no unsaved
     edits is re-read from disk; an open file WITH unsaved edits keeps the user's
     buffer and is marked `changedOnDiskFileIds` (amber icon + hint text on the
     tab, cleared by the next read/save).

ACCEPTANCE (real socket.io server + real chokidar watcher + real socket.io-client):

     file                    type    tool->client   (chokidar treeChanged)
     index.html              add     161ms          (1857ms)
     about.html              add      57ms          (1753ms)
     css/style.css           add     157ms          (1655ms)
     js/main.js              add      62ms          (1560ms)
     pages/services.html     add     154ms          (1464ms)
     pages/pricing.html      add      39ms          (1349ms)
     pages/blog.html         add     150ms          (1198ms)
     pages/team.html         add      40ms          (1088ms)
     pages/faq.html          add     178ms           (959ms)
     pages/contact.html      add     150ms           (781ms)
     addDir changes (new folders): css, js, pages
   worst case 178ms (limit 500ms), all 10 files reported, every file on disk, and
   the chokidar path still fires once as the background safety net. The client
   reducer test (10/10) proves a file created before its folder still lands inside
   that folder, deleted files disappear even when open, and a plain "change" never
   duplicates a row.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
TASK 3 — EXPLORER CREATE TARGET (IMPLEMENTED, NOT FALLBACK)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

DESIGN DECISION: the Explorer's top level is now the REAL per-user container
folder (server/workspaces/u-<owner>/) — the same directory a terminal for the
project starts in. The active project is ONE entry among its siblings instead of
the invisible root of everything:

   - The active project's own folder is rendered as the project tree (its children
     are promoted while the workspace is still an anonymous scratch, so "Scratch"
     is still never shown), and is excluded from the sibling rows.
   - Another top-level FOLDER row is a project: clicking it opens it as the active
     workspace (terminal, preview, export, agent and search follow it).
   - A top-level FILE row is a plain file beside the projects with a "top level"
     badge; it explains itself on click instead of pretending to open.
   - Rows carry a badge (active project / project / top level) and the top level is
     separated from the project tree by a dashed rule.

SERVER (workspaceManager + workspace.controller + workspace.routes):
   GET  /api/workspace/container        -> { container, entries:[{name,type,projectId}] }
   POST /api/workspace/container/entry  -> { name, type:'file'|'folder' }
        folder => a NEW PROJECT: crypto.randomUUID id, registry record,
        folder created in the container and a Project document (metadata only,
        never file contents); file => written beside the projects.
   POST /api/workspace/container/paste  -> copies a project FILE next to the
        projects (name clashes get " (2)"), refuses folders with a clear message.
   A folder that appeared outside the app (terminal) is REGISTERED as a project
   the first time the container is listed, so it is openable instead of being a
   stray row. Every path still goes through resolveInsideWorkspace/ownership
   checks; the container is always derived from the JWT, never from the body.

CLIENT: services/fileSystemAccess.js (CONTAINER_ID, containerEntryId,
isContainerId), workspaceApi.listContainer/createContainerEntry/pasteToContainer,
fileSystemSlice.container state + setContainer, workspaceThunks
loadContainer/createContainerEntry/pasteIntoContainer, and a testable rule module
utils/explorerTarget.js:
   resolveCreateTargetId({ selectedId, tree, hasProject }):
     folder row (root row included) -> inside it
     file row                      -> its parent folder
     nothing selected              -> the TOP LEVEL (container), or the project
                                      root when no workspace exists yet, so the
                                      existing "first create bootstraps a scratch"
                                      flow is untouched
     a stale selection (row gone)  -> the top level, never a random folder
   Empty-area click and Escape both clear the selection, so both mean "top level".
   Right-click on the empty area opens the SAME styled menu (New File, New Folder,
   Paste when the clipboard has an item, Refresh, Collapse All) targeting the top
   level; right-clicking a row is unchanged, and the menu no longer opens on the
   inline create row (rows are matched by [data-node-id]).

ACCEPTANCE: the pure rule module is unit-tested 14/14 (root selected -> inside,
nothing selected -> container, file selected -> parent, stale selection -> top
level, active project never listed twice, files listed as siblings). The container
API is unit-tested 15/15 against real directories (a top-level folder becomes a
usable project with a working tree walk; duplicate/bad names refused; a folder made
outside the app becomes a project; paste copies with a (2) suffix; "../../etc"
refused; the active project folder is untouched). The UI wiring (FileTree rows,
Sidebar handlers, watcher/terminal isolation) is NOT verified in a browser — see
the verification section.

   css/<page>.css — for EVERY page (index.html -> css/index.css, about.html ->
                   css/about.css), only that page's own sections
   js/main.js     — shared behaviour, features guarded by element existence

An existing project that links a single css/style.css KEEPS that convention — the

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
NOTES FOR THE NEXT DEVELOPER / AI TOOL
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  - A FOLLOW-UP IS NOT A FRESH BUILD. The agent's context comes from the snapshot,
    built on the server from disk. If a follow-up produces a different navbar
    again, check that the page really was non-empty (the snapshot returns null for
    an empty workspace) before touching the prompt.
  - The 150ms fsChanged batch is the Explorer's PRIMARY source of truth during an
    agent run; chokidar (750ms debounce, one bulk refresh) is the safety net for
    terminal/external changes. Do not "simplify" either one away.
  - workspace:fsChanged carries project-relative paths, the same ids the tree uses
    (ROOT_ID for the project root). The container rows use a `container:<name>`
    prefix and are deliberately NOT selectable, so a create target can never be a
    path outside the active project.
  - Per-page CSS is chosen by PRESENCE: a css/base.css switches the project (and
    the checks) to the per-page convention; css/style.css keeps the old one. If
    you add another convention, branch the same way instead of relaxing the check.
  - When measuring generated pages in headless Chrome, wait for
    readyState === 'complete': js/main.js is deferred and waits for the Google
    Fonts stylesheet, so a fixed sleep reads a page whose listeners are not
    attached yet (this cost a debugging cycle).


================================================================================
AI Code Editor — Tool-Call Accumulator Fix + Starter Kit Scaffold + Hard
        Connection Check
        (2026-09-28)
================================================================================

STATUS: complete and verified with scripted mock-provider runs that drive the
        REAL runAgent loop, the REAL tools, REAL files on disk and REAL headless
        Chrome (computed styles + PNG screenshots). NOT verified: a live Mistral
        run (no API key in the sandbox) and anything MongoDB-backed. See the
        verification section for the exact commands and raw output.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
TASK 1a — ROOT CAUSE OF "Failed to write file — a \"path\" is required"
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

The streamed tool-call accumulator in server/src/services/providerClient.js
keys chunks by `call.index` (default 0). Verified against a mock provider that
streams the way Mistral actually does, the accumulator produced:

  SCENARIO "merged" (two whole calls, both index 0):
    raw chunks: {"tool_calls":[{"index":0,"id":"call_a",..."create_file",
                "arguments":"{\"path\":\"index.html\",...}"}]}
                {"tool_calls":[{"index":0,"id":"call_b",..."create_file",
                "arguments":"{\"path\":\"about.html\",...}"}]}
    OLD ASSEMBLED: 1 call -> name="create_filecreate_file",
                arguments="{\"path\":\"index.html\",...}{\"path\":\"about.html\",...}"
                JSON.parse fails -> args={} -> "a \"path\" is required"

  SCENARIO "objectArgs" (arguments sent as an OBJECT, not a string):
    OLD ASSEMBLED: arguments="" (the typeof-string guard dropped it)
                JSON.parse('') fails -> args={} -> "a \"path\" is required"

  SCENARIO "aliasNoPath" (file_path / filename aliases):
    OLD ASSEMBLED: merged again, parse fails; even when parsed, executeTool only
                read args.path, so file_path/filename produced no path.

End-to-end reproduction through the real agent (mock provider, 5 turns, then
stopped): every turn showed `[create_file] Failed to write file — a "path" is
required.` and ZERO files were written — the exact loop in the report. A second
variant showed `Failed: create_filecreate_file — unknown tool`.

So the cause was (i) the accumulator merging parallel/whole calls, PLUS (ii)
object-form arguments being dropped, PLUS (iv) argument-name aliases not being
accepted. (iii) max_completion_tokens truncation was NOT involved: the merged
JSON fails at a position past the end of the first object, not at a boundary.
The non-streaming path did NOT have the merge bug — normalizeToolCall() there
already stringified object arguments — only the streamed path was broken.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
TASK 1b — ACCUMULATOR RULES (provider-agnostic)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

providerChatStream now accumulates into a Map keyed by:
  * `id:<call.id>` when the chunk carries an id,
  * else `idx:<index>` when it carries an index,
  * else the previous slot.
A chunk that brings a `function.name` for a slot that ALREADY has a name starts
a NEW call (providers only resend a name when a new call begins) — this is what
defeats Mistral's repeated `index: 0`. A chunk with a new id is a new call by
construction. `arguments` may arrive as a string (appended) or as an object
(JSON.stringify'd in place). Calls are finalised only at the end of the stream,
and empty entries are dropped. Two different calls are never merged.

Malformed / truncated JSON is repaired in agentService by
parseJsonLenient -> extractJsonFragment -> balanceJsonFragment: the first
balanced JSON object is taken, a trailing partial token is dropped and open
strings/brackets are closed before a final JSON.parse attempt.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
TASK 1b — ARGUMENT ALIASES (agentService.parseAndNormalizeToolArguments)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

path    <- path, file_path, filepath, filePath, filename, file_name, fileName,
           name, file, target, target_path, pathname
content <- content, contents, text, code, body, file_content, fileContent,
           source, data
command <- command, cmd, shell_command, shellCommand (run_command only)
The alias key is deleted after mapping so the tool sees only the canonical key.

ACTIONABLE ERRORS. "a \"path\" is required" is gone. The new message names the
tool, lists the keys that actually arrived, lists the required keys, lists the
accepted aliases, and ends with a one-line correct example, e.g.

  Error: create_file needs a "path" — a workspace-relative file path such as
  "index.html" or "css/base.css". It received these keys: content. Required
  keys: path, content. Accepted aliases: path, file_path, ... Send it again like
  this: create_file {"path":"index.html","content":"<!DOCTYPE html>…"}

Unknown tools get the same treatment (the tool name, the keys received and the
list of valid tools).

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
TASK 1b — LOOP BREAKER
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

State: `consecutiveFailures`, `failureEscalation`, `forceNonStreamOnce`,
`lastFailureText`. A turn counts as a failure when EVERY tool call in it failed
(and as a success when at least one worked, which resets the streak).

When two turns in a row fail completely:
  1. the next turn is re-requested in NON-STREAMING mode (forceNonStreamOnce
     is consumed by requestOnce -> sendCompletion({ allowStream: false }));
  2. still failing -> advanceCandidate() to the next tool-capable model/key in
     the chain with the same history;
  3. still failing -> ONE corrective message;
  4. still failing -> the run FAILS FAST with
     "Stopped: the agent's tool calls failed N turns in a row and no files were
     written. Last error: …" instead of burning the token budget.

Failed calls never count as progress (they do not touch writtenThisRun), and
tryAutoContinue() returns false while `consecutiveFailures >= 2`, so the
"continue automatically" budget/tool-cap path can never be triggered by a stuck
failure loop.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
TASK 1b — MODEL CHOICE AND LABELS
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

server/src/config/providers.js gained CODE_ONLY_MODELS = ['codestral-latest'],
isCodeOnlyModel(), PREFERRED_AGENT_MODEL = { mistral:
'mistral-large-latest', groq: 'openai/gpt-oss-120b' } and modelNote(). The auto
scorer applies a 60-point penalty to code-only models (was 25) and a 120-point
bonus to the provider's preferred agent model, both in pickAutoCandidate and in
the vanilla strongest-model pick. Result: Mistral Auto resolves to
mistral-large-latest, never codestral.

client/src/utils/agentModels.js relabels the option "Mistral Codestral (code
only)" with the hint "Code completion only — weak tool use, not recommended for
agent runs", and Mistral Large gets "Recommended — best tool use for agent runs".
codestral stays SELECTABLE, it is just no longer chosen automatically.

Models a Mistral key can list (mirrored by the mock's /models):
mistral-large-latest (recommended, full function calling), mistral-small-latest
(full function calling, faster/weaker), codestral-latest (code-completion tuned,
weak tool use — labelled "code only") and mistral-embed (filtered out by
isExcludedModel/EXCLUDED_MODEL_PATTERNS, never offered).

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

No external images anywhere: every icon and visual is inline SVG or CSS.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
TASK 2b — scaffold_site CONTRACT (server-side, zero model tokens)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

scaffold_site({ brand, tagline, palette, pages:[{file, title, nav_label,
                sections:[...]}] })

executes on the server and:
  * writes css/base.css, js/main.js (verbatim kit files) and css/theme.css
    (the chosen preset's :root block);
  * writes one full HTML page per entry with an IDENTICAL header (brand, mobile
    toggle, nav listing EVERY page, one nav CTA always pointing at the contact
    page so the nav markup is byte-identical on every page), an IDENTICAL
    multi-column footer (brand blurb, quick links, services, contact, 4 social
    icons, year bar), correct RELATIVE links (css/base.css, css/theme.css,
    js/main.js, the Google Fonts link for the preset), a unique <title>, a meta
    description and a language attribute;
  * turns every requested section into a labelled `<!-- SLOT:<name> -->` inside
    <main>, in the order given;
  * writes through writeFileToWorkspace(), so real-time explorer events
    (queueAgentFsChange), validation and the connection check all apply;
  * returns the created file list, the slot map and the theme it used as the
    tool result, and registers every file in writtenThisRun so a later
    create_file on a scaffolded path is rejected (use edit_file).
Existing non-empty files are left untouched and reported as skipped, so a
follow-up scaffold can never nuke a page that already has content.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
TASK 2c — NEW AGENT WORKFLOW (the prompt)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

server/src/agent/prompts/stacks/vanilla.js was rewritten (still COMPACT for every
provider) to:

  PLAN -> scaffold_site (once) -> edit_file per page, replacing every SLOT with
  real copy using ONLY the kit classes -> optional css/<page>.css for genuinely
  custom bits -> short summary.

The prompt carries a compact catalog of ready-to-copy kit markup for every
section type, the rule that nav_label must equal the plan's nav labels, the
section-name list, the "exactly one h1" rule, and the instruction to never leave
a slot marker and never write lorem ipsum. js/main.js, css/base.css and
css/theme.css must never be written by the model. Follow-up tasks use the
project snapshot and the same kit classes; new pages are copied from an existing
page with read_file + edit_file and the nav link is added to every page.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
TASK 2d — OLD PROJECTS KEEP WORKING
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

The per-page stylesheet convention is now chosen by PRESENCE, not assumed:
  * css/style.css only                      -> single-stylesheet project, passes;
  * css/base.css alone (the kit)            -> passes;
  * css/base.css + css/<page>.css on at least
    one page                                -> then EVERY page must link its own.
The same rule was applied to BOTH the connection check and vanillaGate, which
previously hard-required css/<page>.css whenever css/base.css existed and would
have failed every kit project.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
TASK 2e — WHY THE OLD CHECK MISSED IT, AND THE NEW RULES
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Why it missed it: (a) the class-coverage branch is skipped entirely when no CSS
file exists, so a project whose page stylesheets never got written had zero
coverage checking; (b) UNSTYLED_CLASS_TOLERANCE was 2, so a few unstyled classes
were silently allowed; (c) finishing was never blocked — after the repair rounds
were spent the run emitted a `gate` event and then a `complete` event anyway.

New rules in server/src/agent/quality/connectionCheck.js:
  * UNSTYLED_CLASS_TOLERANCE and UNSTYLED_ID_TOLERANCE are 0;
  * an HTML class with no rule in any LINKED stylesheet is a failure, except
    kit-known classes (loadKitClasses() reads css/base.css + the snippets),
    RUNTIME_CLASS_ALLOWLIST, classes the script mutates, and /^js-[\w-]+$/
    hooks;
  * a JS selector with no matching element is a failure, with the same kit /
    js-hook exemptions;
  * a link/script pointing at a missing file is a failure (unchanged);
  * a page that links a missing page stylesheet is a failure (unchanged).

HARD FAIL-ON-FINISH. agentService now has blockFinishing(label, failures,
code): when the quick content check, the link/style check or the visual check
still fails after every repair round AND tryAutoContinue() declines, the run
emits an `error` event listing the problems and THROWS. It can no longer report
success on a site with unwritten or broken files. quickCheck also fails on any
remaining `<!-- SLOT:name -->` marker.

VISUAL SANITY CHECK (server/src/agent/quality/visualSanity.js). Uses a real
headless Chrome via --dump-dom (no dependency added). It finds the Chrome/Edge
binary from CHROME_PATH or the usual locations and SKIPS GRACEFULLY when none is
present. For each page it builds a temp copy with the linked CSS and JS INLINED
(so nothing needs the network), injects a probe, and asserts on computed styles:
links are not the default blue rgb(0,0,238), the header is sticky/fixed, a hero
has >= 48px of vertical padding, cards have non-zero padding, the body font is
not the browser default serif, and the page has no horizontal overflow. It also
clicks .nav-toggle and .faq-item__toggle to prove the kit's JS is wired. Screens
can be captured with captureScreenshots() at any width.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
SUPERSEDES (older statements in this file that are now WRONG)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

Old entries are left in place, but these claims no longer hold:

  1. WRITE ORDER. "css base first, then HTML, then one css/<page>.css per page,
     then js/main.js" is no longer the vanilla workflow. For HTML/CSS/JS sites
     the write order is: plan JSON -> scaffold_site (which creates base.css,
     theme.css and main.js server-side) -> edit_file per page. css/<page>.css is
     now OPTIONAL and only for genuinely custom rules.
  2. SINGLE-CSS CONVENTION. "A css/base.css project always needs css/<page>.css"
     is no longer true. base.css + theme.css with no page sheet is a complete,
     passing convention. The per-page sheet is required for every page only once
     one page links its own.
  3. "The server checks that every class and id used in the HTML has a rule in
     the CSS linked from that page" — still true, but tolerance is now 0 and
     kit-known classes / js-hooks are exempt, and failing it now BLOCKS the run
     instead of only being reported.
  4. Any earlier statement that a failing quality gate still lets the run end
     normally applies only to the content-quality gate (vanillaGate). The
     content check, the link/style check and the visual check are blocking.

WHAT COULD NOT BE VERIFIED

  - A LIVE MISTRAL RUN: there is no provider key in this environment (server/.env
    has no MISTRAL/GROQ key), so every run above used a scripted mock that drives
    the real runAgent, the real tools and real files. The claim "Mistral Auto
    picks mistral-large-latest for agent runs" is verified against the mock's
    /models list and the real scoring code, not against a live key.
  - Which models a real Mistral key can list is therefore reported from the
    allowlist and the mock, not measured.

    they were produced from the mock's canned copy, so they demonstrate that the
    KIT looks modern and professional — not that a real model writes good copy.

    sandbox without Chrome/Edge would silently skip it (by design); the
    /preview/:projectId HTTP path was not re-tested here.
