================================================================================
FEATURE: Starter Kit Fixes (Footer/Hover/FAQ) + Implicit Multi-Project Access +
AGENT.md Project Memory   [documented late: the code landed in the working tree
earlier; this entry was written in the next round to keep the summary complete]
================================================================================

ROOT CAUSES FOUND THEN
  1. Footer repeated per section: the model re-emitted a whole "page tail"
     (</main><footer>…</footer></body></html>) after every section slot, so a
     single-page site ended up with four footers and four document closers.
  2. Invisible hover states: hover backgrounds resolved to the element's own
     resting surface (or a colour indistinguishable from it) in several of the
     six presets, so hovering a button/card/nav link changed nothing visible.
  3. FAQ accordion never opened: the handler bound only on DOMContentLoaded and
     looked for class/attribute names that did not match the scaffold output, so
     sections filled after scaffold time had no working toggle.
  4. Multi-project access needed an explicit "select project" click before the
     terminal/agent/preview followed a sibling project.
  5. "Update my existing project" requests jumped straight to writing without
     reading what was already on disk, and there was no running project memory.

WHAT CHANGED (already in the tree)
  * scaffold.js page shell now carries ONE header and ONE footer with the slot
    markers between them, plus the in-file comment that says so; connectionCheck
    and vanillaGate count <header>/<footer>/<main>/</body> per file and fail the
    run on a repeat (the check existed before; the messages and the counts are
    what this round hardened).
  * base.css gained derived hover tokens (--hover-overlay, --surface-hover,
    --surface-2-hover, --primary-hover) and every preset defines its own pair,
    so a hover can never equal the resting surface. Verified by measuring
    computed colours for every hover rule in all six presets (60/60 changed).
  * js/main.js FAQ handling is now event delegation on document with a
    MutationObserver that re-syncs dynamically added .faq-item nodes, closes
    siblings, and toggles aria-expanded/aria-hidden. visualSanity clicks a
    toggle in headless Chrome and fails when it does not open.
  * Implicit active project: projectsSlice derives the active project from
    whichever file/folder the user last touched; FileTree expands sibling
    top-level projects and their files are clickable directly, so no separate
    "activate" step exists.
  * NEW server/src/services/agentMemory.js appends a dated AGENT.md entry after
    every run (requested, added/edited/deleted, key decisions, status, note for
    the next run). Reading it is opt-in: the settings toggle "Give the agent
    project history (AGENT.md)" (OFF by default) is stored per user and sent
    with agent:run; when OFF the file is still written but its content is never
    injected into the prompt. Verified with a mock provider: 28,558 prompt
    characters with the toggle off (no PROJECT HISTORY section) versus 29,466
    with it on, and two entries appended to AGENT.md after two runs.
  * The Roman Urdu / mixed-language rule was added to the base prompt and the
    update workflow in projectSnapshot.js / stacks/vanilla.js.

================================================================================
FEATURE: Fixed-Header Offset Fix + App-vs-Site Classification + Domain-Aware
Theming + Navbar/Footer Link Rules
================================================================================

TASK 1 — FIXED HEADER COVERED THE PAGE

  1a ROOT CAUSE: the kit header is position: sticky (it reserves its own space),
     so the bug only appears when a header is taken OUT of the flow — the model
     wrote its own header with position: fixed (or an absolute bar) and nothing
     replaced the space it used to hold. <body>/<main> had no top offset at all,
     and the offset token that existed (--header-h: 72px) was a GUESS used for
     scroll-padding only, so a header that wrapped to two lines desynced from it.

     MEASURED BEFORE (real headless Chrome, real page, original js/main.js):
       viewport 1422px: header 73px tall, bottom edge 73px, first content top
       0px, body padding-top 0px  ->  73px of content covered, and the check
       said PASS (it never looked at the geometry at all).
       viewport  390px: identical, 73px covered, check PASS.

  1b FIX: (a) the kit keeps position: sticky as the default and documents why;
     (b) --header-offset is a NEW token (default var(--header-h)) that every
     offset uses, so --header-h stays the design minimum used by min-height;
     (c) js/main.js (site) and js/app.js (app) MEASURE the real rendered header
     height on load, on resize and after document.fonts.ready, write it into
     --header-offset, and toggle body.has-fixed-header when the header's
     computed position is fixed/absolute with top <= 1px — base.css then
     applies padding-top: var(--header-offset). No guessed pixel value, and no
     feedback loop, because the measured value is never fed back into
     min-height; (d) visualSanity measures the header box against the first
     visible block inside <main> and fails the run with the exact numbers and
     the exact fix.

     MEASURED AFTER (same page, NEW js/main.js):
       1422px: header 73px, content top 73px, body padding-top 73px, overlap 0
       390px with the header WRAPPED TO TWO LINES: header 119px (not 72px),
       content top 119px, body padding-top 119px, overlap 0 -> resizing the
       header no longer reintroduces the overlap.
     And the broken page (no offset, no guard) is now caught: FAIL with
     "hides content under the header at 1422px/390px… 73px of it is covered".

     The narrow pass is a real 390 CSS-pixel viewport: Chrome clamps a desktop
     window to ~500px, so the page is loaded inside a 390px-wide iframe wrapper
     and the inner report is copied to the outer document (needs
     --allow-file-access-from-files, which the check already passed).

TASK 2 — AN APP IS NOT A MARKETING SITE

  2a/2c: the plan JSON now has `type: "app" | "site"` as its FIRST field and
     `direction` as its second. base.js defines the rule with examples (app:
     calculator, todo list, timer, converter, quiz, notes, snake/tic-tac-toe/
     memory game; site: business/portfolio/blog/restaurant/SaaS landing/event),
     and normalizePlanType() reads type/project_type/kind from the model's JSON.
     The plan event's feed line is now "TYPE: app — Planned 2 file(s): …" (and
     "TYPE: site — …"), with projectType/direction in event.details.

  2b: NEW TOOL scaffold_app (tools: title, brand, description, palette) plus a
     NEW kit module server/src/templates/vanilla-kit/app-kit.js:
       css/theme.css — the preset tokens (unchanged)
       css/app.css   — the SHARED token block extracted from css/base.css
                       between the /* kit-tokens:start|end */ markers, then a
                       lean app layer: box-sizing/margin/font base, app bar
                       (sticky) + app bar inner/mark/title/meta, body
                       .has-fixed-header padding, app-shell, app-head, panel,
                       app-grid, toolbar, btn (primary/ghost/block), icon-btn,
                       field/label/input/textarea/select/hint/error, list and
                       list__item, chip, readout, notice, muted, sr-only,
                       3 media queries and a prefers-reduced-motion block.
       js/app.js     — window.CMApp helpers (ready/one/all/on/clamp/
                       formatNumber/save/load) plus the same measured
                       header-offset guard.
       index.html    — app bar with the page's single <h1>, ONE <main
                       class="app-shell" id="app"> and ONE <!-- SLOT:app -->.
                       NO nav, NO hero, NO footer, NO brand/social blurb.
     The kit is written through the same writeFileToWorkspace path as
     scaffold_site, so explorer events, validation and the checks all apply.

     The gate and the checks learned the difference:
       * vanillaGate reads plan.type (falling back to "css/app.css exists and
         css/base.css does not" so a REPAIR run with no plan is still judged by
         the app rules) and for an app skips header/nav/hamburger/footer/
         page-stylesheet/FAQ/contact-form/footer-year duties while still
         requiring doctype, lang, charset, viewport, description, title, exactly
         one h1, one <main>, a real stylesheet (css/app.css), a real script
         (js/app.js), :root tokens, >=2 media queries, prefers-reduced-motion, a
         compiling script with addEventListener, and no placeholder copy. It
         actively FAILS an app that has a <nav>, a <footer> or hero/FAQ/pricing/
         testimonial markup.
       * thresholds.js: SHARED_STYLESHEETS += css/app.css, APP_SCRIPT,
         APP_REQUIRED_JS_SNIPPETS (addEventListener only), APP_MIN_LINE_COUNTS
         (index.html 30 so a single-screen tool is not judged like a landing
         page), css/theme.css exempted from the 80-line CSS minimum, and
         SECONDARY_PAGE for legal pages.
       * connectionCheck detects an app project by css/app.css + no css/base.css
         and stops excusing marketing-kit class names there.

TASK 3 — THE VISUAL DIRECTION FOLLOWS THE DOMAIN

  3a/3b: stacks/vanilla.js gained "CHOOSE THE VISUAL DIRECTION FOR THE
     REQUEST — NEVER DEFAULT TO ONE LOOK" with the domain → mood pairings:
     art/gallery/creative → gallery-ivory (warm ivory, serif display font,
     generous whitespace); tech/SaaS → indigo-modern or midnight-dark;
     finance/legal/corporate → slate-corporate; food/restaurant → sunset-warm;
     kids/education → playful-pop; health/clinic/wellness → clinic-calm (or
     emerald-fresh); fashion/beauty/luxury → rose-elegant. The plan's
     "direction" line must name the choice and why, and it is echoed in the
     plan event.

  3c: three presets were added (see themes.js above): gallery-ivory,
     playful-pop, clinic-calm — nine in total.

     VERIFIED: "make an art gallery portfolio website" → gallery-ivory
     (primary #9a6b3f, accent #c2a878, bg #faf7f2, Karla + Cormorant Garamond)
     versus "make a SaaS landing page" → indigo-modern (primary #4f46e5,
     accent #06b6d4, bg #f7f8fc, Inter + Sora). Both single-page sites pass the
     link/style check and the visual check and render at 1440 and 390.

TASK 4 — NAVBAR LINKS vs FOOTER LINKS

  4a: rule added to stacks/vanilla.js: the navbar holds ONLY the primary
     navigation the visitor browses (pages or in-page anchors); the footer holds
     the brand blurb, social icons, contact details, a convenience copy of the
     quick links AND the secondary/legal links (Privacy Policy, Terms &
     Conditions, Cookie Policy, Sitemap, Disclaimer) in the footer legal row.
     A requested privacy/terms link must be a REAL page linked from the footer.

  4b: scaffold.js splits the plan's pages into primary and legal (LEGAL_PAGE
     regex over file name and nav label): the nav and the footer "Explore"
     column list only primary pages, and the legal pages are rendered as links
     in a new .footer-legal row inside .site-footer__bottom (base.css styles
     it). connectionCheck raises a hard failure when "privacy"/"terms"/
     "cookie"/"sitemap" text or an href with those words appears inside a
     <nav>, and exempts SECONDARY_PAGE files from the "every page must be
     reachable from the nav" rule so a footer-only legal page is legal.

     VERIFIED: a 2-page project (index.html + privacy.html) scaffolds with
     nav "Home" only, the footer legal row linking privacy.html, and the check
     PASSES; moving those links into the header nav makes it FAIL with
     "index.html, terms.html place a legal/secondary link … inside the header
     <nav>".

STATEMENTS THIS ROUND MAKES WRONG / SUPERSEDES

    fails it if it does.

================================================================================
AI Code Editor — Root-Caused Repeated Pause/Incomplete Output Bug
================================================================================

STATUS: fixed, and MEASURED. This entry replaces the earlier guesswork with a
reproduction of the real failure: "build a landing page" stopping with a
Continue button over and over and producing a near-empty page.

HOW IT WAS FOUND (raw evidence, not a claim)
  A local mock OpenAI-compatible provider was driven through the REAL runAgent()
  loop with the real vanilla system prompt, the real starter kit and the real
  quality checks. No provider key exists in server/.env and no provider is
  reachable from this environment, so a real-provider run was impossible — every
  number below comes from the production control flow. Full raw numbers:
  tmp-acceptance/root-cause-pause-incomplete.txt

  Reproduced stop #1 (model keeps rewriting one page): 16 requests, 150,702
  tokens (146,123 prompt / 4,579 completion), limitReached=true, message
  "Stopped: this task reached its ~150K token budget. Every planned file was
  written." → the Continue button, on a 28-line index.html with no footer, no h1
  and 5 of its 6 planned sections missing.
  Reproduced stop #2 (Continue clicked): the resumed run stopped at the
  IDENTICAL ~150K budget (150,132 tokens) with the IDENTICAL message and the
  IDENTICAL 28-line page. Every click then burned another ~150K tokens.
  Reproduced false success (model stops early after the scaffold): 27 requests,
  307,992 tokens, reports "The landing page is complete." with
  limitReached=false (so no Continue) while the gate listed 12 real failures.

THE ACTUAL ROOT CAUSE (proven by the runs above)
  * Plan completeness was FILE EXISTENCE, not content. plannedFileIssues() only
    checked that index.html existed and ended with </html>; it never looked for
    the starter kit's unfilled <!-- SLOT:x --> markers or the plan's declared
    <section id="..."> blocks. Once scaffold_site wrote the file, the loop told
    the model "Progress 1 of 1 planned files. Remaining: none. Next: finish with
    your short summary", and the budget stop asserted "Every planned file was
    written."
  * With no issues reported, the soft token budget became the stop condition and
    hard-stopped the run at ~150K cumulative tokens with limitReached=true.
  * A failing quickCheck did NOT block completion: it emitted a gate event,
    SKIPPED the entire vanilla gate (guarded by `quick.ok`), and fell through to
    a SUCCESS result.
  * The budget auto-continue added a FULL 150K per continuation (5 x 150K), so a
    "per-task" budget was silently reset, and the stuck-loop counter was reset
    by it too.

WHICH EARLIER CLAIMS TURNED OUT WRONG (corrections)
  * "Auto-continue must not end the run" — tryAutoContinue() exists, but it was
    NOT wired into the budget stop or the tool-call stop when the plan looked
    complete, which is exactly the landing-page case. Those stops still produced
    the Continue button.
  * "Token budget" — documented as a clean per-task cap, but it (a) stopped a
    normal task once content was mis-reported as complete, and (b) was reset to
    a fresh 150K by every internal auto-continue.
  * "Write validation / connection check accept nothing broken" — connectionCheck
    is fine, but the cheap content check (quickCheck) and the gate could both be
    walked past: a failed quickCheck skipped the gate and the run reported
    success. An unfilled scaffold, a missing footer and a page with no <h1> all
    shipped as "complete".
  * NOT the cause: the plan/classification. The plan was correct ("TYPE: site —
    Planned 1 file(s): index.html" with hero/services/features/testimonials/faq/
    cta). The failure was verification, not planning.

WHAT CHANGED (structural — loop control, budget, validation)
  1. server/src/services/agentService.js — plannedFileIssues(projectId, paths,
     planDoc) now reports CONTENT incompleteness: a page with unfilled
     <!-- SLOT:x --> markers, or missing a <section id="..."> the plan declared,
     is an error (new kinds 'slots' / 'missingSection'). planDoc is threaded
     through every caller (wrap-up hint, progress hint, plan reminder, budget
     auto-continue, tool-cap auto-continue, stuck detection, append limit).
  2. server/src/agent/quality/quickCheck.js — the cheap check now also fails a
     page with no <header>, no <footer>, no/!=1 <h1>, or a plan-declared section
     that is missing. This is the "obviously incomplete page" gate.
  3. agentService.js — a still-failing quickCheck after its repair rounds now
     calls blockFinishing() instead of falling through to success, and a
     still-failing vanilla gate does the same. The run keeps working or stops
     honestly (Continue); it never reports success on a broken page.
  4. agentService.js — the budget is now per task and measured:
     MEASURED_LANDING_PAGE_TOKENS = 44000 (a complete one-page landing page =
     4 requests / 43,840 tokens, measured below); TASK_TOKEN_BUDGET = 4 x that =
     176,000; and an auto-continue tops it up by a bounded +34%, capped at 2.5x,
     instead of resetting it to a fresh 150K.
  5. agentService.js — the stuck-loop counter is cumulative (MAX_STUCK_WARNINGS=3)
     and is no longer reset by auto-continue, and an identical unchanged
     quickCheck failure is only auto-continued MAX_STALLED_CONTINUES=1 time, so a
     looping model is bounded instead of burning the budget.
  6. client/src/features/settingsSlice.js — two control-character regex literals
     replaced with an equivalent helper so oxlint is back to 0 warnings / 0
     errors (those warnings came from the uncommitted strict-rules work).

PROOF (the same "build a landing page" task, 3 runs in a row)
  run 1: 4 requests, 43,840 tokens (37,998 prompt / 5,842 completion), 10.8s, 0 pauses
  run 2: 4 requests, 43,840 tokens (37,998 prompt / 5,842 completion),  4.7s, 0 pauses
  run 3: 4 requests, 43,840 tokens (37,998 prompt / 5,842 completion),  4.7s, 0 pauses
  every run: limitReached=false, gatePassed=true, index.html = 257 lines with
  header=1 footer=1 main=1 h1=1 sections=7 leftoverSlots=0.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
TASK 1 — AUTH "NO DATABASE CONFIGURED": ROOT CAUSE + FIX
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

The message is thrown by assertDatabase() in
server/src/controllers/auth.controller.js when isDatabaseConnected() is false.

CODE SIDE WAS CHECKED FIRST AND IS CORRECT
  - config/env.js loads dotenv from server/.env via an ABSOLUTE path resolved
    from the module file (path.resolve(here, '..', '..', '.env')), so the process
    working directory does not matter.
  - The variable names match: MONGODB_URI / JWT_SECRET / PORT / CLIENT_ORIGIN are
    exactly what env.js reads.
  - server/server.js start() awaits connectDatabase() BEFORE listening, and
    config/db.js already logged either the connection or the failure reason.
  - The live server on http://localhost:4000 reports database:"connected" and its
    startup log shows "[db] connected to MongoDB".

REAL CAUSE IN THIS ENVIRONMENT: a leftover test override. client/.env.local
(created during the previous debugging session) set
VITE_API_BASE_URL=http://localhost:4100, and :4100 was a throwaway server started
with no MONGODB_URI (its health showed database:"not-configured"). Vite gives
.env.local priority over .env, so a dev client on that instance talked to the
DB-less server and got exactly this 503. Sign-in had "worked earlier" because the
original server on :4000 uses the committed client/.env
(VITE_API_BASE_URL=http://localhost:4000) and has a working database.

FIX (targeted, no secrets touched)
  - Removed the throwaway client/.env.local and stopped the DB-less server on
    :4100. No .env file was overwritten or deleted; client/.env is unchanged.
  - config/db.js now emits ONE clear, greppable line per outcome:
      "[db] connected to MongoDB"
      "[db] NOT connected: MONGODB_URI is not set in server/.env — auth and
       projects are unavailable."
      "[db] NOT connected: <driver error message>"
    Only the message text is logged — never a connection string or secret.

OUTSIDE-THE-CODE CASE: when the provider cannot reach MongoDB Atlas (a transient
querySrv ECONNREFUSED on the SRV lookup), the server still runs but with
database:"not-configured" and prints the NOT-connected line. The fix is then
network/DNS or a reachable URI — nothing to change in the code. app.js already
pins public DNS servers (8.8.8.8 / 1.1.1.1) in development.

VERIFIED (live, against the real MongoDB on :4000)
  - A spare-port start printed "[db] connected to MongoDB".
  - 15/15 HTTP checks: register 201 + token/user; /me 200; /me with no token 401;
    /me with a bad token 401; login 200; wrong password 401; duplicate register
    409; create project 201; owner sees it in /projects; a second user does NOT;
    second user DELETE -> 403; owner DELETE -> 200.
  - The healthy DB no longer returns the error; a server started with
    MONGODB_URI empty returns the same clear AUTH_UNAVAILABLE message and logs
    the NOT-connected line.
  - JWT, bcrypt (User.comparePassword) and the ownership guards
    (authorizeProject / deleteProjectHandler) are untouched.

================================================================================
AI Code Editor — Live Preview Path Resolution After the Unified Container
        (2026-10-05)
================================================================================

STATUS: complete. Client-only fix, verified by a pure-module test (7/7), a live
        preview-route run against the real Express app (12 checks) and a live
        socket reload run (1/1). oxlint 0/0 and vite build pass. The full
        signed-in browser click-through was not run (see below).

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
ROOT CAUSE
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
The unified container made every tree node's id/path CONTAINER-relative
("todo-app/index.html") while the preview route serves ONE project at
/preview/:projectId/<project-relative-path>. The resolver for that mapping
already existed (utils/containerPaths.projectIdForPath), and the Sidebar preview
handler already stripped the top segment. But GET /api/workspace/container/tree
tags each top-level project folder with `projectId`, and toReduxTree did NOT
copy that field, so projectIdForPath returned null for every path. Preview then
fell back to the FOCUSED project's id while keeping the full container path:

    /preview/<focusedId>/todo-app/index.html

The server resolved that against the focused project's folder and answered
"\"todo-app/index.html\" was not found in this workspace." (FILE_MISSING). The
same null projectId also silently broke the focus-follow used by the terminal,
export and the agent, plus the two searches' project scoping.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
FIX (client only; the server preview route was already correct)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  client/src/utils/cloudTree.js   toReduxTree now carries a folder node's
     projectId, and areSameNode compares it so structural sharing cannot keep a
     stale untagged node.
  client/src/utils/containerPaths.js   NEW previewTargetForPath(path, nodes)
     returns { projectId, innerPath } — the top segment's project id plus the
     path INSIDE that project ('' for the folder itself, which serves its
     index.html); null when the path belongs to no project.
  client/src/components/Sidebar/Sidebar.jsx   "Open with Live Server" uses
     previewTargetForPath (no more focused-project fallback).
  client/src/components/Topbar/LivePreviewButton.jsx   resolves each HTML file's
     own project id + inner path; the picker lists only resolvable pages, and the
     "focused project root index.html" step is now project-relative.

The preview URL format is UNCHANGED (/preview/:projectId/<project-relative-path>
?token=): only the values put into it are corrected. Relative assets still
resolve under /preview/<projectId>/ and protectPreview still sets the
path-scoped cm_preview_<projectId> cookie; the injected live-reload script still
embeds the project id we now pass.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
FILES TOUCHED
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  client/src/utils/cloudTree.js
  client/src/utils/containerPaths.js
  client/src/components/Sidebar/Sidebar.jsx
  client/src/components/Topbar/LivePreviewButton.jsx
  summary.txt   this entry + the STATUS SUMMARY/OPEN ITEMS update in the
                unified-container entry above.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
VERIFICATION PERFORMED (real output)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  - Pure-module test (real cloudTree + containerPaths, deleted after): 7/7 —
    projectId preserved on project folders; container paths resolve to the right
    project id; previewTargetForPath strips the top segment (nested
    frontend/pages/about.html -> pages/about.html); a folder maps to innerPath
    ''; a path outside any project returns null; siblings resolve to different
    project ids.
  - Live preview route (node server.js on a spare port, isolated
    WORKSPACES_ROOT, MONGODB_URI empty, a container with sibling frontend/ and
    backend/ projects):
      * GET /preview/pid-frontend/index.html -> 200 text/html, injected reload
        script with PROJECT_ID="pid-frontend", Set-Cookie
        cm_preview_pid-frontend; Path=/preview/pid-frontend; HttpOnly
      * css/style.css and js/main.js with the COOKIE ONLY -> 200 text/css and
        text/javascript
      * nested pages/about.html -> 200 ; /preview/pid-frontend/ -> 200 index.html
      * sibling /preview/pid-backend/index.html -> 200
      * missing file -> 404 FILE_MISSING ; ".." traversal -> 400 INVALID_PATH ;
        no token -> 401 ; garbage token -> 401 ; another project's cookie -> 401
      * the OLD URL shape /preview/pid-backend/frontend/index.html -> 404
        "\"frontend/index.html\" was not found in this workspace." (the reported
        bug, reproduced)
  - Live reload (socket.io-client): after preview:watch {projectId:pid-frontend}
    an on-disk edit emitted preview:reload {projectId:pid-frontend}.
  - oxlint: 0 warnings, 0 errors (80 files). vite build: OK (2030 modules).
  - All temporary test files and the isolated workspace root were removed.

  - Ownership enforcement: the test server ran without MongoDB, so

  - Root-absolute asset URLs (/css/style.css) still do NOT load from a previewed
    page because the preview is mounted only at /preview/<projectId>/; this is

    browser tab reload.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
TASK 2 DIAGNOSIS — why the "fix the testimonials" run failed
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  * The kit writes each section on ONE long line. The model reproduced the
    snippet with newlines/indentation, so edit_file's exact match failed; its
    only fallbacks were CRLF-conversion and whole-string trim, neither of which
    handled reformatted whitespace.
  * On failure edit_file answered "Read the file with read_file and copy the
    exact snippet" and nothing else, so the model re-read (read_file truncates
    at 8000 chars, with no outline and no line numbers) and retried the same
    text.
  * There was no section-addressed edit and no cheap outline/line-range read, so
    a targeted follow-up still needed the whole file each turn — the 21-request,
    365K-token run.
  * Writes were not atomic (fs.writeFileSync/appendFileSync), so a budget stop
    mid-write could truncate a page.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

    rating + icons), and added the three missing sections (stats, faq, cta) so
    hero/services/features/testimonials/stats/faq/cta are all present.

    column with .testimonial__meta pinned (margin-top:auto), plus the icon rules.

CLIENT — files created
  client/src/utils/imageAttachments.js
    MAX_IMAGES = 5, ACCEPTED_IMAGE_TYPES = png/jpeg/webp, MAX_SOURCE_BYTES =
    12MB, MAX_DIMENSION = 1600, OUTPUT_QUALITY = 0.85.
    processImageFile(file) validates the type and size, decodes via
    createImageBitmap (Image fallback), downscales the longest side to 1600px on
    a canvas, re-encodes to JPEG and returns { id, name, dataUrl, mime, width,
    height, bytes }. pickImageFiles(list) filters a FileList to accepted images.

CLIENT — modified
  components/AIPanel/AIPanel.jsx
    - Paperclip button next to the input opens a hidden <input type=file
      multiple accept="image/png,image/jpeg,image/webp"> (aria-labels on every
      control).
    - Ctrl+V with an image in the clipboard attaches it (onPaste keeps text
      paste working; only image items are intercepted).
    - Drag and drop: dragenter/over/leave/drop on the panel, with a dashed
      "Drop images to attach" overlay while dragging; text drags are ignored.
    - Thumbnails above the input: click to enlarge (modal lightbox, Escape
      closes), an x to remove each, and a spinner slot per image still
      processing. Sent messages keep their thumbnails in the chat bubble.
    - Max 5 per message: extras are ignored with a toast. Oversized/invalid
      files are rejected with a clear toast.
    - Send is enabled with images only, text only, or both; images-only sends a
      default "Review the attached screenshot(s)…" task.
    - Send/Stop, Continue/reconnect and the usage meters are unchanged.
    - Failed tool actions, and a failed vision step, now expose their COMPLETE
      message under a collapsible "View full error" (the old line truncated the
      reason at 160 chars).
    - The vision step lands in the action stream as "Analyzing … screenshot(s)…"
      then "Analysis ready", with the report in a collapsible block.

  features/aiSlice.js
    startAgentTask accepts `images`, dispatches agentStarted({ task, images }),
    stores the thumbnails on the user timeline entry, and sends the data URLs in
    the agent:run payload.

  components/AIPanel/AIPanel.css
    additive rules for the attachment strip, thumbnails, remove button, attach
    button, message thumbnails, lightbox, drop overlay and the two collapsible
    blocks. .ai-panel gained position: relative.

SERVER — files created
  server/src/services/visionService.js
    sanitizeImagePayload(rawImages) validates count (<=5), data-URL shape, mime
    (png/jpeg/webp) and decoded size (<=6MB) server-side and returns valid
    images plus human-readable problems.
    analyzeScreenshots({ images, task, keys, userId, taskId, emitUsage }) picks
    the user's Mistral key(s), lists each key's models and walks a
    vision-capable chain — mistral-large-latest, mistral-medium-latest,
    mistral-small-latest, pixtral-large-latest, pixtral-12b-2409 — sending the
    images as base64 data URLs in a chat-completions content array
    ({ type: "image_url", image_url: <data URL> }). It records usage per call
    (recordUsageEvent), emits the usage snapshot, marks a key invalid/cooling on
    401/429 and returns a compact structured text report (SCREENS / PROBLEMS /
    LOCATION / EXPECTED), capped at 1600 chars. No vision-capable key (or a
    total failure) throws a clear coded error instead of silently dropping the
    images.

SERVER — modified
  services/agentService.js
    runAgent takes `images`, sanitizes them, emits the vision action pair and,
    when a report comes back, injects it into the user message as
    "SCREENSHOT ANALYSIS (…) — the images themselves are NOT available to you".
    The coding model never receives image data. Vision usage is recorded against
    the run's taskId, so it lands in "This task" and the per-key budget.
  socket/agentSocket.js   forwards payload.images into runAgent.
  server.js               Socket.IO maxHttpBufferSize raised to 25MB so base64
                          screenshots fit in one agent:run frame.

TASK 2 — RELIABLE HTML SECTION EDITING (diagnosis + repair + hardening)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

DIAGNOSIS OF THE aura-glow FAILURE (evidence: the file on disk)
  - server/workspaces/u-6ac206d781c7e207307dab35/aura-glow/index.html had an
    UNCLOSED <section id="testimonials">: the third testimonial's SVG <path>
    was cut mid-attribute (d="M12 2l3.09 6.) and the following
    <section id="stats"> started immediately, with no </section>, </figure> or
    </div> in between. The browser therefore parsed the stats <section> as a
    child of the testimonials .grid → the "By the numbers / Trusted by beauty
    lovers" heading block appeared as a third grid column. That is exactly the
    reported symptom.
  - findSectionBlock() matched <section id="testimonials"> and, finding no
    closing tag, returned end = end-of-file. A replace therefore deleted the
    stats, faq and cta sections, </main> and the footer, so the document
    integrity check (exactly one <header>/<footer>/<h1> and a closing </html>)
    rejected the write. The message was the raw integrity error.
  - describeFailure() then sliced the reason to 160 chars and to the FIRST line,
    which is why the UI showed "that section edit would leave index.html i…".
    The full text existed in details.result but the panel had no way to open it.
  - The 8-request / 155.6K-token loop: every rejected edit answered with a
    generic "read the file and copy the exact snippet", the model re-read the
    whole file (truncated at 8000 chars, no outline), retried, failed again, and
    the loop kept going until the budget cap.

WHAT CHANGED
  server/src/utils/htmlStructure.js (NEW)
    A real HTML scanner that ignores tags inside comments, <!doctype>/<?…>,
    <script>/<style>/<textarea>/<title> raw text and attribute values (it is
    quote-aware, so `title="a > b"` cannot confuse it). On top of it:
      - findSectionBlock(): id/data-section match by the balanced element of the
        SAME tag name; returns { start, end, tagName, line, endLine } or
        { unbalanced: true } when no closing tag exists.
      - sectionLineRange(): the line range of one section.
      - sectionStructureProblems(): structural (container-tag) balance PLUS a
        nested-section check — a <section id="x"> inside another section fails
        with "sits inside".
  server/src/services/agentService.js
    - findSectionBlock now comes from htmlStructure (comment/script/attribute
      aware) instead of the old regex scan.
    - edit_section refuses an unbalanced target with a precise message (id,
      opening line, "never closed") instead of eating the rest of the file.
    - A replacement that would introduce NEW structural problems is rejected
      with the exact problems (what is unbalanced, expected tag, line numbers)
      and the instruction to resend the complete section; the file is not
      written. Document integrity is still enforced too.
    - read_file gained section: "<id>" — it returns ONLY that section with line
      numbers, so a targeted follow-up never re-reads the whole file.
    - describeFailure's reason cap raised 160 → 400 chars.
    - The budget hard-stop no longer interrupts a failing edit: while an edit
      streak is pending it auto-continues once with a focused instruction, and
      the 70% "wrap up" guidance is skipped mid-edit.
  server/src/agent/prompts/stacks/vanilla.js
    - The EDITING rule now spells out read_file { path, section: "<id>" }.
    - NEW LAYOUT-ONLY rule: for "2 rows and 2 columns" / "equal-height cards",
      change the CSS (grid-template-columns: repeat(2, minmax(0,1fr)),
      align-items: stretch, card display:flex; flex-direction:column;
      height:100%, meta margin-top:auto) with append_to_file and touch the
      markup only to get the right card count (exactly 4) — never rewrite the
      whole section.
  server/src/agent/quality/visualSanity.js (additive)
    New probe metric unequalCardRows: cards in the same rendered grid row whose
    heights differ by more than 12px now fail with the equal-height / no-empty-
    space fix.
  server/workspaces/u-6ac206d781c7e207307dab35/aura-glow/index.html (REPAIRED)
    The truncated testimonials section was rebuilt into a well-formed
    <section id="testimonials"> with its own heading and FOUR testimonial cards
    (2x2 grid class), each with a 5-star rating, quote and author. The stats
    section is untouched and now sits after it as a sibling. The document is
    balanced and valid again. No other project or file was touched.

AI Code Editor — Targeted Edits That Cannot Expand, Forbidden-Write Instructions,
        a Cheap Resilient Vision Step, and aura-glow Green Checks (2026-10-05)
================================================================================

STATUS: complete and verified. A follow-up edit is now scoped to the files the
        task names, cannot create an unrelated page, and gets a precise
        instruction (not a retry) when a write is impossible. The "token budget
        at 76% wrap up" message no longer appears for a small edit. aura-glow
        passes connectionCheck + quickCheck + visualSanity + vanillaGate in real
        headless Chrome with real privacy/terms pages. The vision step honours
        Retry-After, falls back across models, and reports a readable message.
        Verified by a 16/16 mock-provider harness (deleted after the run) plus
        oxlint 0/0, vite build OK and node --check OK. NOT verified: live
        Mistral limits and live model vision output (no provider key here).

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
TASK 1 — WHY privacy.html ENTERED A TESTIMONIALS RUN (root cause)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  * The stack prompt's FIRST rule is "your first reply is the plan JSON only"
    and the follow-up block says "READ FIRST, THEN PLAN, THEN WRITE". So on
    EVERY run, including a one-line edit, the model emits a whole-site plan
    (index/about/shop/privacy/terms + missing sections). The loop adopted that
    plan verbatim and plannedFileIssues() then demanded those unrelated files
    and sections. That is what expanded a testimonials edit into about.html,
    shop.html and privacy.html.
  * AGENT.md (project history) preserved the earlier failed "add privacy/terms"
    run, including "Last error: privacy.html does not exist (use create_file)",
    so the model read it as unfinished work.
  * The failed write itself was NOT the app-vs-website legal-page ban: aura-glow
    is a website (css/base.css, no css/app.css) and legal pages are allowed. The
    create_file for privacy.html was rejected by the document-integrity check in
    validateWriteContent ("... was NOT written, because the document is
    incomplete") because the model sent a partial page. describeFailure() then
    sliced the reason to 400 chars/first line, so the UI showed a cut-off text
    even though details.result held the full error.
  * The rule conflict to fix: connectionCheck forbids legal pages for an APP,
    while the vanilla prompt told the model to "create the real page" for a
    privacy/terms link. Two rules could demand opposite things.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
TASK 2 — TARGETED EDITS, NON-RETRYABLE FAILURES, BUDGET (what changed)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
server/src/services/agentService.js
  * NEW isTargetedEditTask(task, snapshot) / namedExistingFiles(task, files):
    an existing project + a task that is not asking for a NEW page and either
    names an existing file or uses an edit verb is a TARGETED EDIT.
  * Plan scoping: when targeted, the model's whole-site plan is DISCARDED and
    plan/planDoc are limited to the named files (sections only when the task
    names an existing section id). No unrelated file or section can be required.
  * The user message for a targeted edit says so explicitly (change only the
    named files/sections; never create a page a link points at).
  * Write guard: during a targeted edit, create_file for a path the task did not
    name is REFUSED with a "remove or repoint the link instead" instruction.
  * removePlannedPath(): a file proven impossible (missing for edit_file, or a
    forbidden write) is dropped from plan/planDoc immediately.
  * Non-retryable failures now append a specific instruction to the tool result:
    a missing file gets "call create_file ONCE, or drop it from the task"; a
    forbidden write gets "do not retry it; remove or repoint the link".
  * planAckPending: after the plan JSON is parsed the model is told to implement
    it. Without this a scoped (empty-file) plan finished the run with no change.
  * Budget: the 70% "wrap up" guidance is skipped for a targeted edit, and the
    100% hard stop auto-continues once for a targeted edit instead of cutting it
    off mid-change.
  * Full error in the UI: the tool event's result cap was raised 2000 -> 8000
    chars, so the panel's "View full error" shows the complete failure text.

server/src/agent/quality/connectionCheck.js
  * A link to a legal/secondary page in an APP now reports "Remove or repoint
    that link; do not create the page." instead of a generic missing-file error,
    so the two rules can no longer demand opposite things.

server/src/agent/prompts/stacks/vanilla.js
  * The privacy/terms rules are now CONDITIONAL on project type: WEBSITE -> a
    requested legal page is created and linked from the footer legal row; APP ->
    never create it, remove or repoint any existing legal link. Applied to both
    the main rule block and the follow-up block.

TASK 3 — VISION: CHEAP, RESILIENT, VISIBLE
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
server/src/services/visionService.js
  * MAX_REPORT_CHARS 1600 -> 1200; maxCompletionTokens 700 -> 500.
  * On 429 it now HONOURS Retry-After once (bounded: 20s per wait, 30s total)
    and retries the same model, then moves to the next vision-capable model on
    the key, then throws VISION_FAILED with a readable message that names the
    limit, the wait and the fallback: "The Mistral per-minute token limit was
    reached during analysis (waited 1s) ... Your images were not dropped ...".
  * The vision call already runs only when images exist and is never gated by
    the coding task's token budget.
server/src/services/agentService.js
  * A failed vision step now also emits a plain note with the full message, so
    the limit is visible in the stream, not only under "View full error".
client/src/utils/imageAttachments.js
  * Downscale 1600 -> 1280px, JPEG quality 0.85 -> 0.8 (cheaper images, same
    readability).
  * A new message already starts with a fresh task budget (taskId is minted per
    run in the socket and clearTaskTotals() runs before the vision step); no
    stale budget from a failed run is reused.

AI Code Editor — Deterministic Vanilla Builds: Final-State Validation,
        Server-Side Section Composition, Request Resolver, Contrast Checks,
        Cleaner Sidebar, and a Repaired contact-form-landing   (2026-10-06)
================================================================================

STATUS: complete for the parts that can be exercised locally; VERIFIED by a
        mock provider driving the REAL runAgent, and by the REAL quality checks
        (including headless Chrome). oxlint 0/0, vite build OK, node --check OK
        on every changed server file. NOT verified: a live Mistral run, live
        visual taste, and the client reducer/UI in a real browser session.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
PART 1 — DIAGNOSIS (no code changed for this part)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

The workspace server/workspaces/u-6ac206d781c7e207307dab35/contact-form-landing
held a skeleton index.html with the header, the hero slot already filled and ONE
remaining `<!-- SLOT:contact-form -->` marker, plus the internal
"ONE DOCUMENT PER FILE" template comment. Root causes found:

  1. DEADLOCK. documentIntegrityIssues() required exactly one <h1> on EVERY
     write. A scaffold page whose hero slot is still a marker has 0 <h1>, so a
     perfectly valid edit that filled any OTHER slot was rejected with
     "that edit would leave index.html incomplete (0 <h1>)". The per-edit
     validator (whole-document rule) and the slot workflow (fill one slot per
     call) deadlocked.
  2. NO SECTION IDS. scaffold_site writes `<!-- SLOT:name -->` markers, not
     real elements, so edit_section found no id/data-section for a section the
     agent could see in the outline. The agent invented "contact-form".
  3. EMPTY HERO CARD + FOOTER CONTRAST. The hero card held only an icon. The
     footer used dark bg + light text tokens, but css/theme.css had been
     corrupted: it contained a full copy of base.css including
     `.site-footer { background-color: var(--surface-2) }` (light) loaded AFTER
     base.css, so `.footer-col__title` / `.footer-link` stayed light on light.
  4. NAV = HOME ONLY. Nav items come from the plan's page list; a single-page
     site has one page, so only "Home" exists. Footer "Services" links were
     hardcoded to `#services` even when no such section exists.
  5. RAW SIDEBAR ERRORS. describeFailure() already produced a short reason, and
     the full tool result was kept in details.result, but retry/budget/strategy
     note events were pushed as one row each and recovered failures stayed red.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
PART 2 — DETERMINISTIC BUILD WORKFLOW
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

FINAL-STATE VALIDATION (server/src/services/agentService.js)
  - documentIntegrityIssues() now enforces exactly-one-<h1> ONLY when the page
    has no `<!-- SLOT: -->` marker left (new hasSlotMarkers()). Header/footer
    presence and the closing </html> are still required always; the h1 rule and
    the leftover-slot rule are final-state rules enforced by quickCheck.
  - edit_file's surgical path now ALSO rejects an inserted fragment that
    unbalances the document (newStructureProblems before/after), so a slot edit
    is valid only when the fragment is well formed and balanced.
  - New finalizeHtmlWrite() strips the internal "ONE DOCUMENT PER FILE" template
    comment on every HTML write, so nothing internal reaches the user's file.

SERVER-SIDE COMPOSITION (new)
  - server/src/templates/vanilla-kit/compose.js (NEW): normalizeComposeInput,
    renderSection, buildComposedFiles, sectionSummary. Renders structured
    section objects into the kit templates for hero, page-hero, services,
    features, stats, testimonials, pricing, steps (process), faq, gallery,
    about (story), team, contact (working form), contact-info, values, mission,
    blogs and cta, and falls back gracefully for other types. Every section
    carries id + data-section + data-title.
  - New tool compose_page (agentService.js): the PREFERRED path for new pages.
    The model sends structured section data; the server renders the pages, fills
    them completely, writes NO slot markers and NO template comments, and
    derives the nav and footer from the sections/pages that really exist
    (in-page anchors for a one-pager, page links for a multi-page site; footer
    Services/Contact columns come from the real sections).
  - scaffold_site + edit_file/edit_section stay fully working (additive).

OUTLINE
  - fileOutline() now lists every section as
    `<section id="…" data-section="…" title="…">` with its line range, so the
    agent and the resolver always see real ids.

COMPLETENESS
  - requestedFeatureSections(task) maps a named contact form / pricing / FAQ /
    gallery / testimonials / login / calculator to a required section; quickCheck
    fails the run when a named feature is missing, and requires a real
    `<form data-validate>` with labelled name/email/message fields for a
    contact form.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
PART 3 — NATURAL EDIT REQUESTS
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  - resolveSectionRequest() (agentService.js): maps the user's words onto a real
    section using id, data-section, data-title, heading text and a synonym
    table (FAQs/questions/accordion → faq; plans/packages → pricing;
    reviews/quotes → testimonials; banner → hero; …), with instruction-word
    stopwords so "the FAQs on the home page" resolves to faq, not to the hero.
    edit_section now uses it when the given id does not exist; an invented id
    always gets a message listing the real ids from the outline.
  - New tool edit_css { path, selector, content }: replaces or appends ONE rule
    block by selector (cssRuleRange), so "make pricing 3 columns" / "make the
    footer darker" never need a whole-file rewrite.
  - The vanilla prompt now prefers compose_page, says read_file{outline:true} is
    the source of real section ids, points styling requests at edit_css, and
    makes named features mandatory.

    (an element/ancestor with a gradient that has no solid backgroundColor is

================================================================================
AI Code Editor — Reliable Edits: A Separate Edit Pipeline, a Word-to-Target
        Resolver, Fragment-Only Validation, and Errors Routed to the Terminal
        (2026-10-06)
================================================================================

STATUS: complete for the parts that can be exercised locally; VERIFIED by a
        mock provider driving the REAL runAgent and by the REAL quality checks
        (quickCheck + connectionCheck) on every edited fixture. oxlint 0/0
        (81 files), vite build OK, node --check OK on every changed server file.
        NOT verified: a live Mistral run (no provider key), and the sidebar in a
        real browser session.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
PART 1 — DIAGNOSIS (reproduced before any pipeline code changed)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

A scripted mock provider (tmp-harness/edit-cases.mjs) drove the REAL runAgent
through the five failing edit requests on a composed site (hero, services,
testimonials, faq, pricing), logging every request and the server's full
decision trail to the terminal.

What the old pipeline did, exactly:
  1. "add a contact form section" was NOT even an edit. isTargetedEditTask()
     only knew edit verbs without "add", so the request left the edit path and
     the whole build loop (plan -> slots -> plan-completeness gate) ran instead.
  2. "update the UI of the FAQ" / "change the hero heading" / "make pricing 3
     columns" / "make the footer darker" all entered the build loop too, where
     the model had to invent section ids and an exact old_string, whole-document
     validation ran between edits, and repair/budget rounds piled up.
  3. The sidebar received raw tool results (details.result), retry/fallback
     notes, wrap-up notices and a red error row per failure; agent:done also
     carried the raw thrown message.

The new pipeline resolves each request with one server-side decision plus at
most one model reply.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
PART 2 — EDIT MODE IS SEPARATE FROM BUILD MODE (agentService.js)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  - runAgent now branches into runEditMode() when the workspace already has
    files, the request names both an action and a target word, and it is not a
    new-page request. Edit mode never touches the plan, the SLOT workflow, the
    plan-completeness gate, the repair rounds or the budget wrap-up.
  - Edit mode sends NO tools. It asks for one JSON object and parses it.
  - Model: mistral-large-latest is chosen EXPLICITLY for the edit whenever the
    user did not pin a model or provider (otherwise auto mode could hand the
    edit to another provider); on a 429 that candidate is cooled and the next is
    tried, so Codestral is only reached as a rate-limit fallback.
  - Targeted edits take 1 request on the happy path, 2 when the fragment is
    rejected once.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
PART 3 — THE RESOLVER AND THE SERVER-OWNED FRAGMENT
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  - server/src/agent/editPipeline.js (NEW): resolveEditTarget() maps the user's
    words to a page + target using the page outline and a synonym table
    (FAQs/questions/accordion -> faq, plans/packages -> pricing,
    reviews/quotes -> testimonials, banner/headline -> hero, ...). header, nav
    and footer are separate area targets. A named section that does not exist
    resolves to "new-section", never an error. Style-only requests
    (columns, darker, spacing, colours...) resolve to css-only.
  - The server extracts ONLY the target section's HTML and the CSS rules whose
    selectors use its classes and sends them with the request. The model returns
    the complete replacement element (and optional CSS) as
    {"html":..., "css":..., "summary":...}.
  - The server owns ids: stampSection() strips any id/data-section/data-title the
    model returned and writes back the real id + data-section + data-title.
  - Fragment validation only: one balanced element of the expected tag, no
    unbalanced structure, no leftover SLOT marker or template comment, no extra
    <h1> on the page, no <script>/<style>, and every class either already in the
    stylesheets or declared in the returned CSS.
  - Document rules (one <h1>, header and footer present, ends with </html>) run
    ONCE on the candidate document, never between edits.
  - On rejection the file is untouched and the server retries ONCE, appending a
    precise problem list to the prompt (never to the sidebar).
  - Atomic write via writeFileToWorkspace + a direct fs-change event for the
    Explorer.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
PART 4 — NEW SECTIONS, NAV LINKS AND CSS UPSERTS
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  - A new section is inserted inside <main>, immediately before </main>/the
    footer, with a server-chosen id, and a matching nav anchor is added to the
    existing <ul class="nav-list"> when the nav already uses in-page anchors and
    the type is a navigable one (services, pricing, faq, contact, gallery...).
  - "add a contact form section" is rendered SERVER-SIDE from the vanilla kit
    (renderContactSection): <form data-validate novalidate> with labelled name,
    email, subject and message fields, validation and the .form-success state,
    all already wired by js/main.js.
  - CSS-only requests return rules only; upsertCssRules() MERGES the returned
    declarations into the existing rule for that selector (keeping every
    declaration that was already there) or appends the rule when the selector is
    new. :root/html/body/* are never rewritten in place — they are appended, so
    a token block cannot be wiped.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
PART 5 — ERRORS GO TO THE TERMINAL, NOT THE SIDEBAR
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

  - New agentLog() writes one line per event to the server terminal:
    "[agent] [scope] <timestamp> tool=<tool> file=<file> <full message>".
  - Tool errors, retries, fallback notices, validator rejections, repair rounds
    and the budget/wrap-up notices now go through agentLog instead of the
    sidebar. A failed tool row no longer carries details.result, so no raw error
    text (and no tooltip payload) reaches the client.
  - blockFinishing() and the tool-failure stop log the full failure list and
    throw ONE short plain sentence; the 70% wrap-up note and the repeated
    retry/gate rows are logged only.
  - client AIPanel: the "View full error" collapsible was removed.
  - The sidebar now receives only progress lines (thinking + note) and the final
    one-sentence completion/failure message. Stop and Continue are untouched;
    a failed edit persists a session so Continue is accepted.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
FILES TOUCHED
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  server/src/agent/editPipeline.js            NEW — resolver, extraction, fragment
                                              validation, swap/insert, nav, CSS upsert
  server/src/services/agentService.js         edit-mode branch + runEditMode,
                                              agentLog, errors routed to the terminal
  client/src/components/AIPanel/AIPanel.jsx   "View full error" removed
  summary.txt                                 this entry

  (NOT touched: auth, explorer, terminal, preview, settings, themes, the
   new-project scaffolds, compose.js, scaffold.js, the quality-check modules.)

AI Code Editor — Right-Sidebar Agent/Terminal Switch + Freebuff-Ready Terminal
        (ConPTY, key passthrough, mouse, scrollback, reconnect)
        (2026-10-06)

STATUS: the right-sidebar Agent/Terminal switch and the sidebar-terminal
        changes are COMPLETE and VERIFIED in a real headless Chrome session
        (36/36 checks, tmp-harness/sidebar-verify.mjs). NOT DONE: the two-stage
        edit pipeline, brand-aware content and the aura-glow repair (Parts 1-4
        of the same request) — see OPEN ITEMS. A live freebuff run could not be
        tested (freebuff needs its own login); a raw-mode Node key probe stood
        in for its TUI.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
PART A — RIGHT SIDEBAR: AGENT / TERMINAL SWITCH
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
The right sidebar (the AI-panel slot) now has a two-button "Agent | Terminal"
segmented switch. Agent is the default and shows the existing AIPanel unchanged.
Selecting Terminal replaces the slot with a terminal that fills the sidebar.

NEW CLIENT FILES
  components/RightPanel/RightPanel.jsx + .css
    role="tablist" switch; BOTH views stay mounted and are stacked
    (position:absolute + visibility, never display:none) so switching never
    unmounts the agent run or the terminal pty. The terminal view is only
    mounted once it exists (or while its tab is selected), so a plain load on
    the Agent tab does not spawn a shell.
  components/RightPanel/SidebarTerminal.jsx + .css
    ONE terminal backed by the existing TerminalView, with the same shell
    selector and a "new terminal" button. Uses the SAME cwd logic as the docked
    panel (workspace.projectId || scratch-<userId>, one level above the
    project), so cd <folder> works identically.
  features/sidebarTerminalSlice.js
    Owns the sidebar terminal's terminalId + shell. Deliberately SEPARATE from
    the docked/floating panel's `terminal` slice, so the two are independent
    ptys that can never attach to the same session or duplicate input/output.

MODIFIED CLIENT FILES
  features/uiSlice.js       rightPanelTab ('agent' | 'terminal') persisted to
                            localStorage under 'code-matrix:rightPanelTab';
                            AI_PANEL_MAX_WIDTH 600 -> 1000 so a TUI fits.
  app/store.js              registers the sidebarTerminal reducer.
  App.jsx                   renders <RightPanel /> in the AI slot, persists the
                            tab, resets the sidebar terminal on sign-out.
  hooks/useGlobalShortcuts.js  returns early while a terminal has focus, so
                            Ctrl+B / Ctrl+P / Ctrl+S (and Ctrl+J) reach the pty
                            instead of firing editor shortcuts.
  components/Terminal/TerminalView.jsx  blurs the xterm when its pane becomes
                            inactive, so a hidden terminal cannot keep focus.

AUTH GATE: unchanged. The terminal has never been auth-gated (Phase 7: the
Topbar toggle is always enabled, scratch-guest before sign-in); the sidebar
terminal matches that exactly, so the gate is neither added nor weakened.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
PART B — TERMINAL MADE FREEBBUFF-READY (Part 5 of the request)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
ROOT CAUSES FOUND
  - The interactive terminal forced node-pty's legacy winpty backend on Windows
    (useConpty:false). winpty does not faithfully pass through the escape
    sequences, arrow/Tab/Esc keys and mouse reporting a full-screen TUI needs,
    so freebuff's model-picker keys and scrolling could not work.
  - A socket drop killed every pty of that socket, so a reconnect could never
    reattach a session.
  - scrollback was 2000, and the animated sidebar width could refit the pty at
    tiny intermediate sizes, reflowing and garbling the screen.

FIXES
  server/src/utils/terminalManager.js
    - interactive sessions now use ConPTY on Windows (useConpty:true); TERM,
      COLORTERM, LANG and LC_ALL are set for UTF-8 / 256-colour output.
    - sessions carry their socket, so output follows a reattach without touching
      the running pty.
    - attachTerminalSession(terminalId, socket) re-binds a live pty to a new
      socket; detachTerminalsForSocket(socketId, graceMs=120s) keeps ptys alive
      across a drop and kills only the ones never reclaimed.
  server/src/socket/terminalSocket.js
    - terminal:create reattaches an existing terminalId instead of erroring;
      disconnect detaches (with grace) instead of killing.
  server/server.js
    - process-wide unhandledRejection guard for node-pty's known ConPTY
      kill-path rejection, so it can no longer take the server down.
  client/src/components/Terminal/TerminalView.jsx
    - scrollback 2000 -> 10000.
    - re-emits terminal:create on a genuine socket reconnect (same terminalId)
      so the server reattaches the same pty with its scrollback.
    - refit is debounced and skips an undersized pane, so an animated panel
      width never refits the pty at tiny sizes (no garbled screen).
  No timeout is imposed on user commands; output/commands are never filtered or
  rewritten. node_modules/.git skipping is unchanged.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
VERIFICATION PERFORMED (tmp-harness/sidebar-verify.mjs — 36/36, real headless
Chrome + CDP driving the running app on http://localhost:5173, server on :4000)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  - Switch renders with Agent default; Terminal click swaps the view; the Agent
    view stays mounted (aria-hidden) while hidden.
  - Typing, paste and a node REPL (50-8 -> 42) all work in the sidebar terminal;
    Ctrl+C interrupts a running ping and returns to the shell.
  - ArrowUp, ArrowDown, Tab, Shift+Tab and Escape all reach the pty unchanged
    (raw-mode Node probe) — the full-screen-TUI key requirement.
  - Ctrl+B and Ctrl+S do NOT fire editor shortcuts while the terminal is
    focused; the keys reach the terminal instead.
  - Agent <-> Terminal round trips keep the pty alive and the scrollback intact.
  - The docked/floating terminal opens its own separate session; closing it does
    not kill the sidebar terminal.
  - Dragging the panel wider refits the pty (45 -> 100 cols); collapsing and
    reopening keeps the shell and refits (100 -> 100 cols); a window resize
    leaves it healthy.
  - The shell selector lists the server shells and picking one restarts the
    session with that shell.
  - The chosen tab persists across a full page reload.
  - scrollback keeps 500 lines of output and the mouse wheel scrolls the
    viewport (viewportY 532 -> 529).
  - Signed in, `mkdir SIDEBAR_E2E_FOLDER` in the sidebar terminal appeared live
    in the Explorer with no reload (chokidar + container watcher).
  - oxlint 0/0 (84 files), vite build OK, node --check OK on the changed server
    files.

        editor previews them, and Strict Rules is now hidden from non-admins.

        16/16 live preview + ZIP checks (server/tmp-audit-preview.mjs) and
        33/33 real headless-Chrome UI checks (server/tmp-ui-verify.mjs) against
        the running app on http://localhost:5173 with the API on :4000.
        oxlint 0 warnings / 0 errors (88 files), vite build OK,
        node --check OK on every changed server file.

        HONEST NOTE: the whole Explorer Upload feature was already committed in
        "agent temp off for users" but was NEVER written up in this file, and it
        was BROKEN at runtime (every upload answered HTTP 500). This entry
        documents both the pre-existing design and the fixes made here.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
WHY NOTHING UPLOADED BEFORE (the actual blocker)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
server/src/controllers/upload.controller.js did:

    const receiver = multer({ storage, limits: {...} })
    receiver(req, res, cb)

multer 2.4.0 changed its API: multer(options) returns a Multer INSTANCE, not
Express middleware. The middleware is the instance's .array() / .single() /
.any() method. Calling the instance threw
    TypeError: receiver is not a function
which the error handler turned into 500 for EVERY request to
POST /api/workspace/container/upload — so no upload could ever appear in the
Explorer, whatever the client did. Found by minting a JWT and hitting the live
route, not by reading code.

FIXES (server)
  src/controllers/upload.controller.js
    - keeps the instance in `uploader` and builds the middleware once:
      `const receiver = uploader.array('files', MAX_UPLOAD_FILES)`.
    - added `preservePath: true` to the multer options. Without it multer
      silently basenames the client-supplied filename, so the traversal check in
      sanitizeUploadFileName never saw "../../evil.png" and a traversal attempt
      was accepted (stored safely, but accepted). With it, a traversal filename
      is rejected with 400 INVALID_NAME, as required.
  src/controllers/preview.controller.js
    - a bare /preview/<segment> request used to force target='index.html', so a
      loose top-level file beside the project folders (e.g. an image dropped on
      the Explorer root) 404'd. It now resolves the segment itself first and only
      falls back to index.html when the segment is not a file. Nested-folder and
      sibling-project assets already worked.

FIXES (client)
  src/components/Sidebar/Sidebar.jsx
    - handlePickerChange read the live event.target.files and THEN set
      event.target.value = ''; clearing the value empties that same FileList, so
      the upload was silently skipped. It now snapshots the list with
      Array.from() before clearing.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
PART 1 — THE UPLOAD FEATURE (end-to-end)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
ALREADY IN PLACE (committed earlier, validated here — nothing rebuilt)
  Server: POST /api/workspace/container/upload behind protect + ownership, a
  per-request temp dir under workspaces/.uploads (outside every container),
  sanitized + length-capped names, conflict-free "name (copy).png" naming via
  uniqueNameInFolder, atomic rename into place (EXDEV fallback), and
  GET /container/file/raw + /:projectId/file/raw for byte-exact reads with the
  right Content-Type. Guarded by resolveContainerOwnerPath, so a traversal in
  the TARGET path is refused.
  Client: utils/fileTypes.js (allowed extensions, 10 MB cap, binary detection,
  formatBytes), hooks/useUploadFiles.js, a hidden multi-file input in Sidebar,
  the folder context-menu "Upload files...", "Copy relative path" and
  "Copy path relative to open file", folder-row drag highlight
  (.tree-row.is-drop-target), the editor asset preview (checkerboard frame,
  dimensions, size, SVG preview/text toggle, "binary file" card) and
  EditorAsset.css.
  Preview: a generic cm_preview_all cookie plus the per-project cookie, the
  validatePreviewSegment guard, and resolvePreviewTarget() delegating
  non-project segments to the requester's own container (this is what makes
  "../sibling/logo.png" and a loose top-level asset load).
  Limits: uploads are 10 MB per file, 40 files, 100 MB per request. Import
  Folder's own 2 MB per-file limit (MAX_IMPORT_FILE_BYTES) is UNCHANGED.

ADDED IN THIS ENTRY
  Explorer toolbar upload button (src/components/Sidebar/Sidebar.jsx)
    An Upload icon button in the Explorer header. It targets the selected folder,
    else the selected file's parent folder, else the container root — the same
    rule the New File / New Folder buttons use (resolveCreateTargetId).
  Drag and drop
    src/components/Sidebar/FileTree.jsx
      - rows now accept dragover/drop on EVERY row, not only folders.
      - the root-level drag state/handlers were REMOVED from FileTree.
    src/components/Sidebar/Sidebar.jsx
      - the root drop zone moved up to the <aside class="sidebar">, so the empty
        area below the tree AND the Explorer header area both count as "root"
        (the requirement asked for the header too, which the old FileTree-only
        zone missed). The whole Explorer gets .is-root-drop-target while dragging.
      - handleDropFiles(node, files, { hasDirectory }): a folder row uploads into
        that folder; a FILE row uploads into that file's parent folder; the root
        uploads into the per-user container root, so the files appear as
        top-level items next to the project folders.
      - a dropped FOLDER (detected with DataTransferItem.webkitGetAsEntry in the
        drop event) shows "Folders cannot be dropped here — use Import Folder to
        add a folder from your computer." and the directory entries are filtered
        out instead of producing a confusing "not an allowed file type".
    src/utils/dropFiles.js (NEW) — shared isFileDrag / hasDroppedFolder helpers.
    REMOVED: src/components/Sidebar/UploadFolderDialog.jsx + .css. The previous
    build answered a root drop with an "upload to which folder?" picker, which
    contradicts the requirement (root drop = container root). Dropping the dead
    dialog keeps the flow single-purpose.
  Upload progress
    src/services/api.js — uploadFiles() now uses XMLHttpRequest (fetch cannot
    report upload progress) and accepts an onProgress callback; it still returns
    the parsed JSON and still throws ApiError with the server's message/code.
    src/hooks/useUploadFiles.js — exposes { uploadFiles, isUploading, progress }.
    src/components/Sidebar/Sidebar.jsx — a slim progress bar + percentage strip
    under the Explorer header while a transfer runs.
    src/components/Sidebar/UploadDrop.css — root highlight, row highlight and the
    progress strip styles (the old .file-tree.is-root-drop-target rule moved to
    .sidebar.is-root-drop-target).
  Toast — an upload success toast already carried a "Copy path" action button
  (Toast.jsx runAction -> copyTextToClipboard); unchanged, verified working.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
PART 2 — BINARY CORRECTNESS
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  src/hooks/useExportToFolder.js and src/hooks/useSyncToLaptop.js fetched every
  file with workspaceApi.getFile(...), which returns UTF-8 TEXT — so any uploaded
  image/font/video was corrupted on export and sync. Both now branch on
  isBinaryPath(path) and read bytes through the new
  workspaceApi.getProjectFileBytes(projectId, path) (the /:projectId/file/raw
  route) and write the Blob unchanged.
  src/components/Sidebar/SearchInFiles.jsx filters isBinaryPath(node.id) out of
  the searched file list, so binary content is never fetched or searched.
  Quick Open (TopbarSearch.jsx) only ever matches file NAMES, and the agent
  snapshot (agent/prompts/projectSnapshot.js) only reads .html/.css/.js — both
  already ignored binary content. ZIP export streams the on-disk directory with
  archiver, so it is byte-for-byte by construction (verified by inflating an
  entry out of the downloaded zip and comparing bytes).

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
PART 3 — STRICT RULES HIDDEN FROM NON-ADMINS
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
  The role system already existed (committed earlier): User.model.js has
  role: 'admin' | 'user' (default 'user', missing -> 'user', no API/UI to change
  it), auth.controller.js publicUser() returns it, GET /api/auth/me exposes it,
  server/src/middlewares/requireAdmin.js has isAdminUser(userId) (reads the role
  from the DB for the CURRENT request — the JWT is never trusted for
  authorization) and every agent socket event (agent:attach / agent:stop /
  agent:run) answers 403 FORBIDDEN for a non-admin. strictRules are consumed only
  inside the admin-gated agent:run, so a non-admin's saved rules can never be
  applied or sent to the agent.
  src/components/Settings/SettingsModal.jsx
    - the nav list now filters out BOTH 'ai' and 'strict' for non-admins.
    - NEW shownSection: if state.settings.activeSection is a section the caller
      may not see (a deep link such as openSettings('strict')), rendering and the
      nav highlight fall back to Appearance. No effect and no state write is
      needed, so the hidden section can never render for a non-admin.
  Admin behaviour is unchanged: for an admin the list and the Strict Rules
  section are exactly as before (verified in the browser).
  NOTE ON THE "403 ENDPOINTS" PART: there are NO HTTP endpoints that read or
  write strict rules — a grep of server/src/routes and server/src/controllers
  finds none. Strict rules live in the client (localStorage /
  settingsStorage.js) and travel over Socket.IO with the agent run, which is
  already admin-gated. Nothing was invented to add such endpoints; the existing
  gate is what enforces it. (The same requireAdmin middleware is verified live on
  the admin-only keys API.)
